## Ranks and Advancement

### 1. Summary

Rank is the world's measure of power. It runs from normal people through iron, bronze, silver, gold and diamond; beyond diamond are gods and great astral beings, who are not part of the ladder. Essence users and monsters share the ladder, and each rank "has a suppressive effect on lower ranks" (Book 1, Ch19 L42). Rank changes the body, the length of life, what a person can do with their aura and the way other people treat them. It is the setting's class system.

### 2. How it works

**The ladder.** Normal people with fewer than four essences are unranked. Four essences make iron rank (Book 1, Ch15 L100; Book 1, Ch21 L65-66). Rufus names diamond as the highest rank (Book 1, Ch22 L50-51). The sequence in the text is iron, bronze, silver, gold, diamond (Book 1, Ch22 L58).

**What iron does.** The System lists what iron gives Jason: damage reduction against normal-rank damage, increased resistance to normal-rank effects, aura sense, and the ability to sustain himself with concentrated magic (Book 1, Ch21 L67-70). Reaching iron "made your body advance closer to a state of perfection, which included purging your body of impurities" (Book 1, Ch22 L9). Iron-rankers then eat spirit coins: "Then you can just eat some coins as well" (Book 1, Ch19 L28); with a full set of essences a person "doesn't need to eat or drink anymore", though they can for pleasure (Book 1, Ch15 L85).

**Advancing past iron.** A person cannot "properly progress towards bronze rank" until every essence ability is awakened (Book 1, Ch22 L68). The System then reports "Progress to bronze rank: 50% (4/4 essences complete)" (Book 3, Ch14 L98), counting essences whose abilities are complete. An ability held back says "cannot advance further until all attributes have reached bronze rank" (Book 3, Ch15 L56). See `Abilities_and_Attributes.md`. Methods of advancing, and how monster cores damage them, are in `Monster_Cores_and_Training.md`.

**Lifespan.** "All essence users age slower. At iron rank you wouldn't notice the difference, but bronze rankers can live well past a hundred. Silvers can double that; reach silver rank young enough and you'll look young for decades. Gold rankers live for centuries, and I'm not even sure diamond rankers can die of old age" (Book 1, Ch22 L58). Rufus calls the idea that diamond rank is immortal "a rumour, but a persistent one" and says there is an unwritten rule that diamond rankers do not share their limits (Book 1, Ch22 L60). The same rule is part of why diamond rankers are "mysterious figures pursuing goals known only to them" (Book 1, Ch22 L55).

**Body changes by rank.** Clive says that essence users "all go through physiological changes as we increase in rank. At iron rank, our digestion starts operating very differently. Our gold-rankers here don't even need to breathe. Each time we rank up, in addition to making our bodies superior vessels for magic, there are changes to how our bodies operate" (Book 2, Ch34 L73). He adds that vulnerable points in the torso are less vulnerable because the body uses them less and that silver and gold ranks are mostly containers for a living mass of magic (Book 2, Ch34 L73). Ranking up also beautifies people: bronze rank gave a woman "the healthy, athletic look of a magically idealised body" (Book 3, Ch69 L759). The effect is "akin to polishing an already stunning gemstone" for people already beautiful and more striking for others (Book 3, Ch69 L759). People closer to "how we are represented in our souls" look more like that each time they rank (Book 9, Ch5 L55). Hiro and Ken regained "the healthiness of their youth" on taking essences, and bronze might "turn back the clock somewhat" (Book 5, Ch5 L82). The renewal of youth is stronger with certain essences: Yumi, who chose blood, flesh and bone, looks no older than Jason (Book 5, Ch31 L147).

**Silver: levitation.** Levitation is "an intrinsic property of a silver-rank soul, allowing the aura it projected to physically affect the environment" (Book 4, Ch73 L92). Farrah calls it "inherent to silver-rank auras" (Book 4, Ch74 L8). At silver rank this was "mostly just a power to levitate that helped their increasingly heavy bodies walk on weaker surfaces or even water, but it was easily disrupted" (Book 6, Ch14 L6).

**Gold.** Gold rank is where the body no longer needs to breathe (Book 2, Ch34 L73), and where adventurers live for centuries (Book 1, Ch22 L58). Gold-rankers are the big names of a city; during a surge "the golds put aside their interests and agendas and step up" (Book 7, Ch10 L19).

**Diamond.** Diamond is the peak of mortal power. The Adventure Society keeps two or three diamond-rank essence users in the largest cities "in case a diamond rank monster shows up" (Book 1, Ch22 L55). Diamond-rankers are the ones who can travel between physical realities (Book 2, Ch25 L49). Mr North compares them to "a nuclear bomb that can walk around and do what it likes" (Book 6, Ch43 L52). Diamond-rank monsters usually turn up only during surges, "when the hidden diamond-rankers show themselves" (Book 7, Ch10 L19).

**Badges.** Adventure Society badges are tied to the wearer's aura: "Adventurers need new badges at each rank for a reason", because a change in rank changes the aura and tracking stones stop working (Book 3, Ch32 L88).

### 3. Variations and exceptions

**Rank suppression.** "Whether an essence user or a monster, each rank has a suppressive effect on lower ranks. We're all bronze rank. If you were to fight any of us, your iron-rank abilities would have much less effect" (Book 1, Ch19 L42). Resistances grow with rank disparity: a skilled iron-ranker's afflictions are shrugged off by a higher-ranked enemy (Book 1, Ch87 L137). A bandit leader assumes Jason's spells failed "due to rank disparity" (Book 3, Ch28 L78). A silver-ranker fights as "silver-rank was silver-rank and the monster didn't die" even against attacks that ignore disparity (Book 3, Ch69 L808). Spirit coins offer a temporary workaround: "You can overcome that briefly by boosting your attributes with spirit coins... not for very long" and it "will leave you weaker once the strength fades" (Book 1, Ch19 L43).

**Ignoring disparity.** Some abilities and titles remove part of the penalty. A System text from Jason's Book 3 title says: "Ignore the enhanced resistances derived from rank disparity. This only affects the enhanced resistance from being higher rank, not other sources of resistance" (Book 3, Ch21 L109), and a second line ignores "the enhanced aura suppression and aura suppression resistance derived from rank disparity" without touching "the inherently superior strength of higher-rank auras" (Book 3, Ch21 L110). Sophie's Boundary Breaker removes the damage reduction from disparity (Book 3, Ch69 L808). Humphrey's Hero's Drive lets him ignore resistances of disparity (Book 3, Ch67 L95). Clive notes that Humphrey and Jason "can overlook rank disparity in certain regards" (Book 3, Ch37 L9).

**Transcendent damage.** It ignores "all forms of physical and magical defence, damage reduction and resistances", so an iron-rank ability that deals it can annihilate a bronze monster (Book 1, Ch99 L169). See `Afflictions_Boons_and_Damage.md`.

**Racial difference.** Cores take more for people who are not human, and humans' abilities advance faster (Book 1, Ch15 L58; Book 1, Ch31 L96).

### 4. Examples from the books

- **Rufus and Farrah.** Both bronze "not that far off silver" when Jason is first an essence user (Book 1, Ch22 L33).
- **Greenstone's Society.** Elspeth Arella is a silver-rank bureaucrat (Book 1, Ch101 L16).
- **Silver-rank core user.** Miles Cotezee's silver rank comes "entirely from cores" (Book 6, Ch47 L878).

### 5. Common misconceptions in-world

- **A higher rank always wins.** Disparity-ignoring abilities and transcendent damage exist (see section 3), and Jason's team repeatedly beats higher-ranked enemies.
- **Rank is earned only by killing.** Cores let rich people reach bronze "without ever facing a monster" (Book 1, Ch31 L100).
- **Immortality is certain.** Rufus: "It's a rumour, but a persistent one" (Book 1, Ch22 L60).
- **Your first rank is the same everywhere.** A transformation can rank people up: becoming a corpse-host for worms "seemed to rank up the body" (Book 9, Ch60 L7).

### 6. Changes over time

- **Level 2:** On Earth the ranks are called categories, "named after the colours of spirit coins": category one is iron, two bronze and three silver (Book 4, Ch21 L33; Book 4, Ch31 L48). The grid registers incursions above category five, and "there is no above category five" (Book 4, Ch5 L13-14). See `Earth_Magic.md`.

---

## Rituals, Arrays and Formations

### 1. Summary

Ritual magic is the practice of drawing a diagram, a circle with lines and glyphs, and powering it with external magic to do something an essence ability cannot. It is used for absorbing essences, awakening stones, summoning familiars, spreading wards and tracking, and, in permanent form, as formations and arrays. Ritual magic is learned, not given by an essence, and a competent ritualist is a valuable member of any team. Anyone can learn external magic; only essence users can combine it with essence powers.

### 2. How it works

**What it is.** Farrah's summary: "Ritual magic relies on external sources of magic. That's ambient magic, plus more concentrated sources, like quintessence or spirit coins" (Book 1, Ch23 L27). "External magic doesn't require you to have an essence. There are people who make careers out of learning a specialised slice of external magic" (Book 1, Ch23 L30), like plumbers who know "just enough to do a specific job" (Book 1, Ch23 L31-32). The fundamental theory is shared across rituals, magical craftsmanship and alchemy: "Same basis, different applications" (Book 1, Ch23 L32).

**The circle and the ambient magic.** A ritual diagram must be adjusted to the local magic: "Every time you draw a magic diagram you need to adjust for the ambient magic conditions. A weak source of congealed magic like the core of a lesser monster is a perfect way to check your work" (Book 1, Ch23 L15). A ritual room is designed with a permanent circle and a regulated environment (Book 1, Ch23 L16-17). A flat stone floor helps: Emir's ritual room has "a floor made from a single slab of black stone, cut perfectly level and smooth" (Book 2, Ch6 L41). A cloud floor is poor for circles (Book 3, Ch25 L38). Materials vary with the ritual: a salt circle will do for ordinary summoning, while exotic summons call for obsidian dust and iron filings (Book 1, Ch93 L41). A permanent brass circle is made by carving channels into stone and pouring molten metal (Book 3, Ch7 L96).

**Clive's gift.** Clive's racial gift balances the ambient magic: "It balances out the ambient magic so you don't need to adjust your ritual circle" (Book 1, Ch85 L60), and his essence ability, Enact Ritual, draws diagrams in lines of golden light (Book 1, Ch103 L44; Book 2, Ch6 L43). "His ability to draw them with his power, along with balancing out the ambient magic, saved immense amounts of time when going through many rituals in sequence" (Book 2, Ch66 L90).

**Essence rituals.** "Essence rituals are very gentle. It's only if you shove the essence inside yourself without one that the experience is a harsh one" (Book 2, Ch7 L130). In a ritual the essences turn to glowing dust that swirls around the user; when the last essence is absorbed the dust stops glowing and falls (Book 2, Ch7 L117). The standard sequence for Sophie's stones in Emir's palace takes hours, with Clive resetting the circle between stones (Book 2, Ch66 L90; Book 2, Ch67 L3, L162).

**Casting rituals as powers.** A ritual can itself be an essence ability. Farrah notes that "rituals that are also essence powers tend to be quick and dirty", like Gary's and her short-lived summons (Book 1, Ch23 L38-39). A familiar summoning is an "elaborate" ritual needed only per rank (Book 1, Ch23 L42). See `Familiars_Summons_and_Conjurations.md`.

**Large rituals.** The blood cult's star seed ritual uses a magic circle "to gather and concentrate the ambient magic to fuel the star seed", supplemented periodically with spirit coins (Book 3, Ch19 L37). Carlos's soul-projection ritual, used to check for star seeds, is "a gold rank ritual", devised for the occasion and rare in Greenstone (Book 3, Ch23 L19). A circle for it was "easily the most sophisticated circle Jason had ever seen", with sections coloured red, green and blue as well as gold (Book 3, Ch23 L32). Clive's combat ritual circles glow on the floor under his feet during a fight (Book 3, Ch38 L30).

**Divine rituals.** A god's rituals work differently: "divine rituals are not so vulnerable to vagaries of ambient magic as the mundane varieties" (Book 2, Ch19 L77). They "still draw on the power of ambient magic but are infused with the glorious might of the divine. My god's will moves the magic and not the other way around" (Book 2, Ch19 L79).

**Mirage chambers.** A mirage chamber is a training room with a control block of runes at one end, carved into a stone block under a window (Book 1, Ch48 L3). It runs at a chosen rank: in Greenstone Rufus asks to borrow one but the family can only run it at bronze rank for limited periods because local magical density is low; Danielle offers hers at iron rank instead (Book 1, Ch37 L41-55).

### 3. Variations and exceptions

- **Formation, array, grid.** Farrah is a specialist in "formation magic and arrays. Permanent and semi-permanent versions of ritual magic, which is one of the core magical fields" (Book 5, Ch54 L11). Jason's account: "Formations were permanent or semi-permanent ritual effects. Arrays were formations layered in sequence or even atop one another", with "some interdisciplinary crossover with artifice" (Book 4, Ch25 L20).
- **The Grid.** On Earth the grid is "like the landscape is somehow operating as a series of interconnected rituals, creating a magical array from the natural features of the world itself... nodes the size of cities, with elements made up of mountains, hills and rivers", covering the entire planet (Book 5, Ch5 L11). See `Earth_Magic.md`.
- **Landscaped magic.** Clive says of a large estate that whoever designed it showed "the foresight to wait for plants to grow over decades, planning out the shifts in magic as plants and trees grew" (Book 2, Ch18 L11).
- **Dice and triggers.** Humphrey's summoning die activates its power by being rolled in a "simple circle of powdered chalk" (Book 3, Ch10 L5).
- **Ritual knowledge from skill books.** Jason's ritual skill book gave him "the bare minimum to be considered a proper ritual magician" (Book 1, Ch23 L36). See `Skill_Books_and_Learning.md`.
- **Who is allowed to draw.** A person who does not know external magic cannot awaken a familiar summoning power (Book 1, Ch23 L37).

### 4. Examples from the books

- **Absorption of essences and stones:** Book 1, Ch21 (Jason, no ritual needed), Book 2, Ch7 (Sophie).
- **Familiar summonings:** the first sanguine horror in Book 1, Ch23; Shade's bronze resummoning materials in Book 2, Ch63.
- **A ritual room with permanent circle:** Landemere Vane's room, found under his burned house and described as "an ordinary, if impressive, ritual room" which "predates the settlement of Greenstone" (Book 3, Ch11 L90).
- **Portal rituals:** In Landemere's old ritual room, the Builder cult's agents build an astral bridge from a brass circle and a crude brick archway (Book 3, Ch7 L96-97).

### 5. Common misconceptions in-world

- **Magic circles are universal.** Each needs adjusting to local magic (Book 1, Ch23 L15).
- **Divine rituals are just bigger.** They are less vulnerable to the vagaries of ambient magic than mundane rituals (Book 2, Ch19 L77).
- **Rituals need an essence.** External magic "doesn't require you to have an essence" (Book 1, Ch23 L30).

### 6. Changes over time

- **Level 1:** Clive's racial gift and Enact Ritual speed up circle-drawing in the Book 2 sessions (Book 2, Ch66 L90).
- **Level 2:** On Earth, Farrah is called "the foremost expert in array magic on the planet" (Book 5, Ch36 L84) and the Grid is shown to be a planetary array (Book 5, Ch5 L11).

---

## Skill Books and Learning

### 1. Summary

A skill book is a consumable magical book that implants knowledge directly into the reader. It can teach a language, a martial art, the basics of ritual magic or a branch of astral magic. A skill book gives technique and information, not understanding or practice, and the people who use them well prepare beforehand and consolidate afterwards. They are the fast, flawed route to ability in a skill, in the same way that cores are the fast, flawed route to rank.

### 2. How it works

**Reading one.** The book describes itself and its requirements on a System panel. Ritual Magic I "can impart the fundamentals of performing magic rituals", and its only requirement is the "ability to use skill books" (Book 1, Ch7 L50-51). Higher books list more: Astral Magic II needs bronze rank, the ability to use skill books, and basic and intermediate ritual magic theory and basic astral magic theory (Book 1, Ch7 L41-43). The player-style response is: "Jason was familiar with skill books from video games that instantly gave out spells or special abilities" (Book 1, Ch7 L46).

**What happens.** The book floats over the reader, opens, and its text rises from the page as glowing runes that swarm around the reader and sink into them (Book 1, Ch50 L65-73). The process is painful: "Using a skill book is strenuous. The more it's trying to teach you, the greater the strain" (Book 1, Ch50 L71). A big book takes long and hurts more: the martial-art skill book that Jason uses was bigger than any Farrah had seen (Book 1, Ch50 L67).

**Staying conscious.** "People often pass out while using them, but the information isn't passed on as well once they're unconscious. It takes them longer to consolidate what they've learned afterwards" (Book 1, Ch50 L72). Rufus urges Jason to "try and last out the whole thing" (Book 1, Ch50 L69).

**How it feels.** The knowledge arrives as memory of something never learned: "Remembering something he never learned in the first place was an odd sensation, like déjà vu" (Book 1, Ch15 L113). A reader recognises a thing as if he had always known it (Book 1, Ch10 L38).

**What it gives and what it does not.** Rufus: "The thing you need to understand about the skill book is that it isn't going to teach you how to fight... It will teach you technique, not how to use it. It's a shortcut that saves you years of repetitive exercise, but that isn't fighting... Even the best hammer doesn't push the nails in itself" (Book 1, Ch50 L20-22). Farrah: "Knowing isn't the same as understanding. You've used a ritual magic skill book, but knowing practical applications isn't the same as grasping the theory" (Book 1, Ch39 L112). Rufus: "Learning to fight through skill books is ultimately faster than training from nothing because you save yourself years of repetition to ingrain the skills. There is a danger, however, of not fully comprehending the techniques. By preparing well before using a skill book, then consolidating well after, you avoid developing flaws in your skillset" (Book 1, Ch39 L115). The Remore family academy has to beat the habit of confusing technique with fighting out of "skill book users" (Book 1, Ch39 L117).

**Preparation.** Before a martial skill book Jason is made to learn to stand, move and sit properly: "When it stops feeling awkward, you will be ready to use a skill book" (Book 1, Ch40 L26).

**Contents are practical.** "People make skill books with knowledge practical for adventurers, not for farmers. It's one of many reasons that skill books aren't proper magic instruction. They only give the flimsiest theoretical grounding" (Book 1, Ch85 L8). Jason's ritual book gives him the minimum "to be considered a proper ritual magician", enough to awaken a familiar summoning power and to perform an essence ritual (Book 1, Ch23 L36; Book 1, Ch29 L87).

### 3. Variations and exceptions

- **The ability to use them.** Jason's racial ability Mysterious Stranger lists "Essence, awakening stone and skill-book absorption" as one of its functions, alongside language adaptation and immunity to identification and tracking (Book 1, Ch7 L55-58). Jason reads the description as the power to use skill books (Book 1, Ch7 L60). For others, the requirement of being able to use skill books is met by ordinary means, since the Remore academy trains with them (Book 1, Ch39 L109, L117).
- **Languages.** Gary notes that Jason sounds as if he "learned the language from a skill book" (Book 1, Ch15 L41).
- **Martial art.** Way of the Reaper: Five Forms I is a skill book detailing "the foundational techniques for all five forms" (Book 1, Ch50 L41), later found in separate volumes in a hidden cache (Book 1, Ch88 L80-85). Jason's experience is that the forms "aren't just five mini martial arts bundled into a skill book anthology" (Book 1, Ch75 L72). The skill book gave him proficiency with an array of weaponry, although Rufus concentrated training on only a few (Book 1, Ch56 L18).
- **Opponents' view.** Sophie, who has fought skill-book users, says fighting Jason "didn't feel like that" (Book 2, Ch1 L47).
- **Knowledge without ground.** A reader can find a text "above his head" when the field is more specific than the skill book (Book 1, Ch12 L15).
- **Cooking and crafts.** Jason wants "a cooking magic skill book" to extricate syrup from food (Book 1, Ch38 L11).

### 4. Examples from the books

- **Ritual Magic I** taken from Landemere Vane and read in Book 1 (Book 1, Ch8 L88-93; Book 1, Ch69 L84).
- **Astral magic book**, also from Landemere (Book 1, Ch69 L84).
- **Way of the Reaper: Five Forms I**, Rufus's gift (Book 1, Ch56 L47; Book 1, Ch50 L41).
- **Magic Society records** are not skill books but are sold as tablets of public knowledge on powers (Book 1, Ch92 L95).

### 5. Common misconceptions in-world

- **Use it and you're good.** Jason's own assumption: "Isn't the whole point of skill books to just use them and you're good?" (Book 1, Ch39 L110). Rufus and Farrah correct it.
- **A skill book is proper instruction.** Clive: it is not (Book 1, Ch85 L8).
- **A skill book makes you a fighter.** It teaches technique, not the fighting (Book 1, Ch50 L22).

### 6. Changes over time

- **Level 1:** Jason's skill-book martial art becomes the basis of his fighting once he learns to combine the forms rather than use them separately (Book 1, Ch75 L72).
- **Level 2:** A Network enemy's training in Book 5 is derived from human methods and wastes bronze-rank potential (Book 5, Ch1 L51-52); see `Monster_Cores_and_Training.md`.

---

## Souls, Death and Healing

### 1. Summary

A soul is the part of a person that essences and ranks change. It shapes the body, projects the aura, leaves marks when wounded and, on death, lingers briefly before drifting into the astral. Healing magic can reverse a death only in that grace period and only with powerful magic. Everyday healing is done by the Healer's church, by alchemists and by healing abilities, and it is in short supply compared to demand.

### 2. How it works

**Death and the grace period.** Clive: "When the soul dies, it only lingers with the body for a small time. Usually minutes, but an annihilated body might have the soul depart in seconds, while freezing to death might have it linger for an hour or even longer. It's why if a gold rank healer can repair the body in that grace period, the death can be turned back" (Book 2, Ch25 L53). After that, "an untethered soul is an astral object and drifts into the astral" (Book 2, Ch25 L55).

**Outworlders.** "An outworlder is someone whose soul has re-entered a physical reality, reflexively manifesting a body for itself... like a monster" (Book 2, Ch25 L57-58; Book 2, Ch25 L59). The Builder and others do not just resurrect their own minions because the great astral beings "are careful about encroaching upon one another's interests" (Book 2, Ch25 L51).

**The Reaper and the World-Phoenix.** Shade: the World-Phoenix has "the right to return mortals from death", used with restraint out of respect for the Reaper's authority, and the Reaper "only cares if you cheat being dead. Once your soul has gone to the astral, that is when it becomes his concern. Until then, it is the affair of any death god a world might have" (Book 4, Ch3 L34, L36). Jason's Nirvanic Transfiguration trades ordinary resurrection for a limited number of rebirths: "you would have only one chance to revive at each rank" (Book 4, Ch2 L50, L60). Its restriction is "not an artificially imposed restriction": a gestalt of body and soul, once it dies, stays spiritual (Book 4, Ch3 L40). The description says its restrictions "will change on reaching diamond rank" (Book 4, Ch2 L50).

**Souls and marks.** A soul "can't truly be harmed by external forces", but it can be marked (Book 4, Ch37 L14). Jason's interface "used titles to translate the scars on his soul into game terms" (Book 4, Ch2 L26); in Book 3 a woman who has been watching him says she can see the scars on his soul "more clearly than the ones on your body" (Book 3, Ch29 L46). A soul scar leaves a person depleted though physically well; Neil advises against rushing recovery with potions after four days asleep (Book 3, Ch14 L9). See `Auras.md` for the titles.

**Crests.** A soul crest "is a magical tattoo printed not on the body, but on the soul. That imprint would appear on the body in turn, in a form that resisted design. The form of the crest was a visible reflection of the bearer's true nature" (Book 2, Ch70 L36). The unique imprint on the aura stays the same even when the aura changes, making it "a guaranteed proof of identity" once recorded (Book 2, Ch70 L37-38). The Immortal Crest is an iron-rank consumable applied by a mystical tattooist, usable only on an iron-rank essence user (Book 2, Ch70 L33-35). The tattoo is purged as the wearer ranks up, so a new one is needed (Book 2, Ch70 L42). Humphrey has one in vivid blue (Book 2, Ch70 L44).

**Healing in practice.** "Most healing was done through the church of the Healer. From what he'd been told, their god supplied the essences and awakening stones that gave them their healing abilities. They could be sought out for a fee, but also sent people around the delta to heal people at more reasonable prices. It sounded good, but Jason had seen firsthand that there was always more demand for such services than supply" (Book 1, Ch35 L23). Both ritual magic and alchemy have ways to heal, but "the cost and expertise required placed both out of the reach for most people" (Book 1, Ch35 L22). The Healer personally tells a patient that an alchemist could not regrow a foot, and offers to station a healer at Jory's clinic (Book 1, Ch95 L69, L126).

**Healer powers.** Healing abilities exist for cleansing and recovery; see `Afflictions_Boons_and_Damage.md`. A healer who stops a person dying within the grace period is a "gold rank healer" in Clive's account (Book 2, Ch25 L53).

### 3. Variations and exceptions

- **Messengers.** Their life "is not just physical. It's tenacious": remnant life force remains after the body is annihilated and "resurrection magic doesn't work on messengers. Or anyone like them" (Book 9, Ch65 L22-23).
- **Diamond-rank effects.** "More potent diamond-rank resurrection effects" can return the soul after it has left the body, as long as it lingers in the world, and death gods may govern them (Book 4, Ch3 L38).
- **Healing the immune.** A person with an immunity may need special handling; see the afflictions that negate immunity in `Afflictions_Boons_and_Damage.md`.
- **Recovery after soul damage.** Jason's soul was "battered and beaten to the point that his entire torso was a landscape of ragged marks" and the damage gave him powerful capabilities (Book 4, Ch2 L25-26).

### 4. Examples from the books

- **Farrah's death** is the example Jason's team recalls when Clive explains the grace period (Book 2, Ch25 L54).
- **Humphrey's crest**, with its blue sigil (Book 2, Ch70 L44).
- **Jory's clinic**, an alchemy lab and healing station (Book 1, Ch35 L63).

### 5. Common misconceptions in-world

- **A good healer can undo any death.** Only within the grace period, and only against bodies that can be repaired (Book 2, Ch25 L53).
- **A soul can be harmed.** Soul scars are "marks" on a soul that "can't truly be harmed by external forces" (Book 4, Ch37 L14).
- **A crest can be chosen.** It cannot be (Book 2, Ch70 L39).

### 6. Changes over time

- **Level 2:** By Book 9, "Resurrection magic has been harder for a few years now. Even at gold rank, you have minutes at best, and only the most complex and difficult healing magic can do it", something "the gods of healing and death did to how magic works" (Book 9, Ch81 L61-63).

---

## Titles and Blessings

### 1. Summary

A **title** is how the System writes down a change to the soul: it appears as a window after a soul has been scarred or tested and usually changes the owner's aura. A **blessing** is a gift from a god or great astral being that evolves a racial gift. Titles come from what happens to a person, blessings from what a transcendent being chooses to give. The two meet in Jason's story because the same events scar his soul and attract the blessings of the World-Phoenix and the Reaper.

### 2. Titles

#### What they are

- **The Interface's translation.** "When recovery from soul trauma affected Jason's abilities, his interface power used titles to translate the scars on his soul into game terms" (Book 4, Ch2 L26). Anyone who fully recovers from soul trauma finds themselves enhanced (Book 4, Ch2 L25).
- **They change the aura signature.** Each window says so: "Your aura signature has changed", with a note of what can be detected in it (for example Book 3, Ch14 L83). See Auras.md.

#### Titles in the text

| Title | When and why | Effect as the window gives it | Cite |
|---|---|---|---|
| Godless Prophet | Jason's aura is "damaged by the direct, concerted focus of multiple transcendent-level entities" (the gods in Book 2) | Aura's suppressive force and resistance to suppression from higher-ranked auras increased; an echo of transcendent power in the aura signature | Book 2, Ch65 L85 |
| Resolute | "The damage you suffered in your stand against a much more powerful enemy has marked your soul" | Resistance to the suppressive force of higher-ranked auras increased; his "unflinching resolve" can be detected | Book 3, Ch14 L81-83 |
| Spirit Warrior | Fighting off a concerted attack on his soul by a transcendent entity | Aura's suppressive force and resistance increased; suppression resistance applies to other forms of magical suppression; after fully suppressing an aura he may attack the target's soul directly; the echo of transcendent power increases | Book 3, Ch21 L149-152 |
| Indomitable | "Repeated defiance in the face of more powerful enemies and willingness to sacrifice everything for a cause" | Resistance to aura suppression further enhanced and ignores rank disparity; allies within his aura gain increased resistance | Book 4, Ch2 L22-24; Book 6, Ch46 L28; Book 8, Ch14 L24 |
| Giant Slayer | Overcoming a much stronger enemy: the gold-rank King of the Dinosaurs | A permanent mark sensed by others; may trigger a fear reaction from the unintelligent and weak-willed if his aura is significantly stronger than theirs, regardless of his actual rank | Book 5, Ch29 L68; Book 5, Ch30 L30; Book 5, Ch64 L128 |

| Title | When and why | Effect as the window gives it | Cite |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reality Hegemon | A title that appears with a notice that he has "awakened potential your soul cannot sustain at its current rank" | The maximum total size of his spirit domains increases; the effect of his spirit domain on hostile intruders ignores rank disparity. A restricted effect lets him influence physical reality within his spirit domains, which he does not meet the rank to use; it is "forcibly unsealed" with a backlash for any change | Book 6, Ch15 L45; Book 8, Ch57 L95-96 |

### 3. Blessings

#### What a blessing does

- **A racial gift evolution.** A god or great astral being can give a follower a blessing that triggers an evolution of a racial gift (Book 1, Ch85 L47). Clive's blessing from the Celestial Book changed the human Special Attack Affinity to a spell affinity (Book 1, Ch85 L22).
- **Harmless and irrevocable.** The blessing "could not control the recipient outside of ordinary methods like gratitude and obligation" and, once granted, could be neither managed nor revoked by the giver, unlike an ability from a divine awakening stone (Book 4, Ch2 L44). Clive: "The blessings are harmless" (Book 2, Ch63 L47).
- **Balanced by the great astral beings.** They do not give favoured supporters blessings that contain as much magic as can be withstood, "to avoid escalation and to minimise mortal interference" (Book 4, Ch3 L46).
- **Not the same as star seeds.** Astral beings can bestow blessings like gods but cannot bestow essences and stones; instead they can send followers star seeds (Book 2, Ch21 L26). Blessing powers "do not offer control over their recipients" (Book 4, Ch3 L70).
- **Given to opponents too.** Some great astral beings give blessings to those who oppose their interests when ideologies mesh, or to enemies worthy of respect (Book 4, Ch2 L45; Book 2, Ch63 L47).

#### Tokens

- **Astral blessing tokens.** "Something great astral beings give out to bestow blessings, as signs of approval", usable in an awakening ritual to arrange specific abilities, which only transcendent beings can arrange (Book 2, Ch63 L45-49). Clive's own arrived "one night while I was studying" as a patch of moonlight (Book 2, Ch63 L51).
- **Jason's tokens.** His Reaper token, produced by his quest system, is a surprise to Clive, who says only great astral beings can produce them (Book 2, Ch63 L53-55).

#### Notable blessings

- **The World-Phoenix's blessing to Jason**: on rebirth he is told "You have received the blessing of the World-Phoenix"; if accepted his outworlder racial ability Astral Affinity evolves to Nirvanic Transfiguration (Book 4, Ch1 L33-35). It is "a product of a bargain struck between great astral beings" and works outside the normal balance (Book 4, Ch3 L48). Jason had already received a transfiguration through a Reaper token, with his use of the token while absorbing a stone serving as consent (Book 4, Ch2 L45).
- **Farrah's blessings**: after the bargain between the World-Phoenix, the Reaper and the Builder, the World-Phoenix agrees if "we bestow blessings to evolve her racial gifts. All her racial gifts" (Book 3, Ch69 L315-317). Magic Society investigators later find that every racial gift of an outworlder woman evolved through a blessing, each from a different great astral being, most commonly from the Reaper and the Celestial Book and rarely from the World-Phoenix (Book 7, Ch11 L73-75). The Builder "only blesses its own people" (Book 7, Ch11 L78). Farrah describes feeling "all these blessings ready to evolve my new outworlder ones" (Book 4, Ch58 L19).
- **Sophie's Boundary Breaker**: her celestine racial gift Astral Affinity evolves into Boundary Breaker in a fight (Book 3, Ch68 L13-16), where Clive calls it a likely "rank-jumping power".

#### A god's blessing on a place

- The Healer gives Jory's clinic his blessing and declares it a sanctuary (Book 1, Ch95 L120, L128). Compare Church_of_the_Healer.md.

#### Names given by enemies

- **The Rejector**: Builder cultists call Jason "the Rejector" (Book 3, Ch32 L105), a living demonstration that the Builder's power is not absolute (Book 3, Ch34 L7). Jason finds it "not a cool nickname" (Book 3, Ch32 L106).

### 4. Not in the text

The full list of titles a person can earn is not given; only Jason's and Delia's are shown. Blessings from the Legion and the All-Devouring Eye are described as not handed out (Book 7, Ch11 L75).

---

## Unusual and Unique Magic

### 1. Summary

Most magic in the setting follows the essence-and-rank structure described elsewhere in this folder. A smaller body of magic does not: the gifts a person is born with or earns as a race, the outworlder abilities that give Jason a game-like interface, titles drawn from soul damage, time magic, and the powers of great astral beings and gods. This file collects them so a storyteller knows what exists and who has what. It does not attempt to explain the cosmology; see `02_Cosmology_and_Powers/`.

### 2. How it works

**Racial gifts.** Races have inherent abilities. Humans, for example, have an aptitude for special attacks (Book 4, Ch40 L72), "Human Ambition", "Special Attack Affinity" and "Essence Gift" (Book 2, Ch61 L144, L147; Book 2, Ch67 L113). Elves have "Life Affinity" and celestines "Mana Integrity" (Book 2, Ch61 L145-146). Gary notes that other races are less efficient: humans "have their essence abilities increase faster than other races" (Book 1, Ch15 L58). Racial gifts can evolve (see below).

**The outworlder gifts.** Jason's first status screen lists six racial abilities labelled Outworlder: Interface, Quest System, Inventory, Map, Astral Affinity and Mysterious Stranger (Book 1, Ch1 L37-43).
- **Interface:** the screens and System messages; "interpret reality through a recognisable medium" (Book 1, Ch87 L160).
- **Quest System:** quests with rewards, including a "racial gift transfiguration" (Book 2, Ch61 L130).
- **Inventory:** a grid of forty slots with a currency counter; items are plucked from and pushed into the screen (Book 1, Ch1 L82-89).
- **Map:** a self-updating map (Book 2, Ch61 L141).
- **Astral Affinity:** "Increased resistance to dimension effects and astral forces. Dimension abilities have increased effect and transcendent damage is increased" (Book 1, Ch1 L93).
- **Mysterious Stranger:** "Language adaptation. Essence, awakening stone and skill-book absorption. Immunity to identification and tracking effects" (Book 1, Ch1 L96-99).

**Evolutions of racial gifts.** A racial gift can transfigure when the owner completes a task or takes a blessing. In Book 2 Jason's Interface becomes Party Interface, with a contacts list and party features that members keep while they remain in the party (Book 1, Ch87 L157-162). Map becomes Tactical Map, which tracks nearby allies and enemies (Book 2, Ch61 L138-142). His teammates' gifts evolve in the same reward: Clive's Human Ambition to Thirst For Knowledge, Neil's Life Affinity to Life Guard, Sophie's Mana Integrity to Mana Wellspring, Humphrey's Special Attack Affinity to Attack of the Mirage Dragon (Book 2, Ch61 L144-147). Quest System later becomes Defiant, which loses the quest effects and gains the ability to "ignore the enhanced resistances derived from rank disparity" and rank-disparity aura suppression (Book 3, Ch21 L105-111). Mysterious Stranger evolves into Dark Rider in Book 2 (Book 2, Ch63 L83-85).

**Blessings.** A great astral being can bless a person: "You have received the blessing of the World-Phoenix. If you accept the blessing, your outworlder racial ability [Astral Affinity] will evolve to [Nirvanic Transfiguration]" (Book 4, Ch2 L39-40). Clive's explanation: such a blessing "could not control the recipient outside of ordinary methods like gratitude and obligation" and "once granted, could not be revoked like that from a divine awakening stone" (Book 4, Ch2 L44). A Reaper token used while absorbing a stone counted as consent to a transfiguration (Book 4, Ch2 L45).

**Titles.** Titles are given by the System for soul damage that changes the aura (see `Auras.md`).

**Time magic.** Time can be "sped up or slowed down" and one can "move forwards, vanishing and then reappearing at some point in the future. Affecting the past, however, is impossible. Even the Keeper of Moments, the great astral being that governs time, cannot do such a thing" (Book 6, Ch14 L80). The Builder sealed dead parts of his constructed worlds in time, "frozen and unchanging", where altered things revert to the state at the moment of sealing (Book 6, Ch14 L78). Danielle's time confluence has a limited scope at silver rank (Book 6, Ch14 L79).

### 3. Variations and exceptions

- **Immunity to identification.** Jason's identification immunity means the Adventure Society badge does not track him (Book 1, Ch62 L55-59).
- **Removal.** A divine stone's ability can be revoked; a great astral being's blessing cannot (Book 4, Ch2 L44).

### 4. Examples from the books

- **Humphrey's Hero's Drive.** The text credits Humphrey's Hero's Drive with letting him ignore the resistances of rank disparity (Book 3, Ch67 L95).
- **Sophie's Boundary Breaker.** Eliminates the damage reduction from rank disparity (Book 3, Ch69 L808).
- **Jason's interface** appears to other people in his party (Book 1, Ch87 L162).

### 5. Common misconceptions in-world

- **Racial gifts are rare.** Every race has them, although most never evolve.
- **A blessing is an obligation.** Clive describes it as no stronger than gratitude (Book 4, Ch2 L44).
- **Time travel into the past is possible.** Shade: "The past is inviolate" (Book 6, Ch14 L82).

### 6. Changes over time

- **Level 1:** The Reaper trials reward the team with racial transfigurations (Book 2, Ch61 L130-147).
- **Level 2:** Astral Affinity becomes Nirvanic Transfiguration in Book 4, with a limited number of rebirths (Book 4, Ch2 L46-49).

## Belinda Callahan's Power Set

### 1. Summary

Belinda is the team's trickster and utility caster: Magic, Trap and Adept, producing the Charlatan confluence (Book 2, Ch32 L67-72). Her set borrows other people's powers, shapes herself into other roles, sets traps and teleports allies. She is delighted by her own power: "I was looking through the abilities it's known to give and they sound fantastic" (Book 2, Ch32 L72, quoted in the character file).

### 2. Abilities

| Ability (essence) | Type and cost | Effect at iron | Cite |

| Echo Spirit (charlatan) | familiar (ritual), extreme mana | Summons an Echo Spirit to serve as a familiar | Book 2, Ch68 L40-45 |
| Instant Adept (adept) | special ability, very high mana, 6 hour cooldown | Large Speed boost and temporary proficiency with acrobatics, small blades and ranged weapons; more stamina | Book 2, Ch71 L57 |
| Counterfeit Combatant (charlatan) | special ability (shape-change), very high mana, 6 hour cooldown | Large Power boost and temporary proficiency with armour and melee weapons; her physique enlarges | Book 2, Ch71 L70 |
| Specious Sorcerer (charlatan) | special ability, very high mana, 6 hour cooldown | Large Spirit boost and the ability to use magical tools; more mana | Book 2, Ch71 L90 |
| Blessing of Readiness (adept) | spell, moderate mana | Cooldown of an ally's next ability reduced by up to a minute (ally only) | Book 2, Ch68 L8 |
| Blessing of Relentlessness (adept) | spell, extreme mana, 24 hour cooldown | Resets all cooldowns of a single ally of bronze rank or below; bronze adds ongoing mana and stamina recovery | Book 3, Ch59 L91 |
| Masterful (adept) | aura (recovery) | Aura ability (details not in the line read) | Book 3, Ch43 L147 |
| Mirror Magic (magic) | special ability | Cast the same spell a nearby ally just used, once, at the strength of her own rank and attributes | Book 3, Ch11 L27 |

| Force Tether (trap) | conjuration, low mana per second | A crystal rod tethers nearby enemies and drags them toward it; the rod is protected by a force field | Book 3, Ch11 L45 |

| Unexpected Allies (charlatan) | spell (illusion, dimension, teleport), extreme mana, 1 hour cooldown | You and your allies take the form of nearby enemies and all in the area are randomly switch-teleported | Book 3, Ch40 L131 |

### 3. Racial gifts

- **Essence Gift to Quick Learner and Face in the Crowd** (Book 2, Ch68 L17, L50) and **Special Attack Affinity to Form and Function** (Book 2, Ch68 L56). The text shows the names but not their effects in the lines read.

### 4. At silver (Books 8 and 9)

## Clive Standish's Power Set

### 1. Summary

Clive is the team's battlemage and ritualist: Magic, Rune, Balance and the Karmic confluence (Book 2, Ch28 L56). His set is built on one big spell, retributive karma effects that punish attackers, ritual circles and mana support. Spells are his focus; he uses magical weapons like staves and wands and works with his familiar (Book 2, Ch28 L56).

### 2. Abilities

| Ability (essence) | Type and cost | Effect at iron | Cite |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wrath of the Magister (magic) | spell, moderate mana plus per effect, 1 minute cooldown | Locks a prismatic beam on an enemy; extra mana either alters the target's reality with colour effects (red heat, yellow raised mana cost, pink reduced resistances, green self-poisoning blood, purple harm on spending mana) or unmakes reality in a localised area as an annihilating void sphere | Book 2, Ch42 L52-62; Book 3, Ch37 L32 |
| Tools of the Magister (magic) | special ability/ritual | Use specialty magic tools, vehicles and weapons; bronze uses a ritual circle to strengthen a staff or wand | Book 3, Ch38 L38 |
| Rune Trap (rune) | spell, high mana, 1 minute cooldown | An explosive rune that triggers by proximity, caster or both; bronze adds a secondary explosion from affected enemies | Book 3, Ch38 L68 |
| Enact Ritual (rune) | special ability | Manifest ritual diagrams and use materials directly from dimensional storage; bronze alters magical items' parameters | Book 3, Ch43 L79 |
| Rune Mantle (rune) | spell, low mana, 10 second cooldown | A ring of random runes around an ally; attacks against the ally trigger a rune; bronze extends to all nearby allies | Book 3, Ch48 L54 |
| Juxtapose (balance) | special ability, high mana, 1 minute cooldown | Swap two allies and/or enemies you can see; bronze makes affected enemies take more damage from all sources | Book 3, Ch38 L60 |
| Blood Magic (balance) | special ability | Consume your own life force to replenish mana; bronze enhances an active ritual | Book 3, Ch38 L78 |
| Mantle of Retribution (karmic) | spell, low mana, 10 second cooldown | Retributive impact damage on anyone who attacks the target ally; bronze bestows it on all nearby allies | Book 3, Ch48 L62 |
| Vengeance Mirror (karmic) | special ability | Replicate the last spell or special attack used on you; bronze lets it be used a second time | Book 3, Ch38 L97 |
| Eye of Karma (karmic) | spell (zone), extreme mana, 24 hour cooldown | A wide zone in which damage dealt by enemies inflicts disruptive-force damage on the source; bronze adds Good and Bad Karma | Book 3, Ch40 L118 |

### 3. Racial gifts

- **Mana Equilibrium**: balances ambient magic around him; he uses it before rituals (Book 1, Ch85 L17; Book 1, Ch103 L44).
- **Human Ambition to Thirst For Knowledge**: essence abilities advance more quickly and he can learn information through skill books (Book 2, Ch61 L144; Book 2, Ch62 L27-30).
- **A blessing from the Celestial Book** turned his Special Attack Affinity into a spell affinity (see Clive_Standish.md; Book 1, Ch85 L22-26).
- **Spirit Reaper** (magic): "special attack (melee, dimension, drain)" inflicting extra disruptive-force damage and draining mana (Book 2, Ch64 L75). The line introducing it names Humphrey, but the system message calls it a magic essence ability and Humphrey has no magic essence (Book 2, Ch64 L72-73); it is listed here for that reason, and Open_Questions does not track it.

### 4. At silver (Books 8 and 9)

| Ability | Rank and effect | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| Instant Karma (karma) | Silver 2: the target suffers damage identical to what it dealt recently, as retributive damage; silver adds a pair of spells inflicting different versions of the Instant Karma affliction | Book 8, Ch7 L36 |
| Mantle of Retribution | Silver 1: after being attacked, the recipient's damage against the attacker is increased briefly | Book 8, Ch7 L61 |
| Juxtapose | Silver 1: affected enemies are inflicted with Inescapable | Book 8, Ch12 L30 |
| Eldritch Imbalance (balance) | Silver 3: drains mana for as long as it is channelled; silver adds an instantaneous execute variant that scales with low mana | Book 8, Ch39 L73 |
| Blood Magic | Silver 4: spend life force to enhance spells | Book 8, Ch39 L222 |
| Enact Ritual | Silver 4: conjure mana lamps with enhanced efficiency; refined mana enhances ritual magic | Book 9, Ch59 L4 |
| Mana Tide (balance) | Silver 4: draws mana from the astral to replenish allies, escalating over time; silver enhances allies' mana-cost abilities | Book 9, Ch85 L32 |
| Crystallise Mana (magic) | Conjures floating mana crystals above him (a restoration conjuration) | Book 9, Ch69 L17 |

### 5. How the set plays

He sets up before a fight and refreshes: the rune circle that triggers effects when attacked and the damage-reflecting mantle (Book 2, Ch30 L53), then uses Wrath of the Magister as his big attack (Book 3, Ch4 L33; Book 3, Ch10 L23). Neil's shields boost retribution damage, so Clive's karma powers work best behind them (Book 8, Ch7 L60). At silver the text says Instant Karma "had long been the power most difficult for him to raise, as he was simply bad at using it" (Book 8, Ch7 L48).

### 6. Not in the text

Gold-rank effects of his abilities are not shown in the lines read.

## Farrah Hurin's Power Set

### 1. Summary

Farrah is a volcano-themed battlemage and ritualist: Earth, Fire and Potent, which form the Volcano confluence (Book 1, Ch19 L30). She fights in glossy obsidian armour with a sword that becomes a lava whip, burning orbs and lava spells, and her ritual magic underlies the team's summons and later the Grid repairs.

### 2. Powers

- **Obsidian armour**: heavy obsidian armour that glows with internal heat (Book 1, Ch109 L12); a full set of glossy obsidian shards swept into wing shapes with a red glow between the segments (Book 4, Ch61 L18).
- **Sword**: a huge obsidian sword whose blade is "a pillar of jagged segments" that can break up and whip around on a cord of glowing magma (Book 1, Ch108 L94). Unextended it is a jagged-edge greatsword; as a whip-sword the teeth are strung on a flexible cord of red-hot lava (Book 5, Ch60 L46).
- **Lava cannon**: a showstopping power that "devoured mana like pigs with a fresh corpse" (Book 4, Ch41 L31); she uses it to devastate a team of silver-rankers (Book 5, Ch50 L36).
- **Orbs**: three flaming orbs manifest over her head and shoot fiery beams (Book 4, Ch61 L34); at the Network fight they are "searing flame" orbs (Book 5, Ch50 L38).
- **Magma and obsidian terrain**: a glowing sigil on the ground produces a magma geyser (Book 4, Ch61 L33), a curtain of obsidian shards from a stomp (Book 4, Ch61 L34), an obsidian wall that explodes into shards (Book 5, Ch60 L37), and a spike that impales (Book 5, Ch60 L48).
- **Finisher**: she turns the obsidian fragments left in an enemy into lava (Book 5, Ch60 L68).
- **Magma elemental summon**: a pile of black and red magma with arms, summoned from a ritual circle like Gary's golem (Book 1, Ch17 L72).
- **Ritual magic**: she adjusts circles for ambient magic (Book 1, Ch23 L15-17) and teaches Emi, Hiro and others magical theory (Book 7, Ch55 L35).

### 4. How the set plays

She is a front-line brawler in heavy armour with a sweeping weapon. Jason notes she has a "peak bronze-rank speed attribute" and sweeping attacks that clear clusters but lacks mobility (Book 4, Ch70 L48). Against a gold-ranked vampire, her sword hits "nothing but air" because of the speed difference (Book 5, Ch60 L50).

### 5. Not in the text

No System window for Farrah's abilities is shown in the lines read, so the ability names beyond "lava cannon" and "Volcano" and their ranks are not stated.

## Gary's Power Set

### 1. Summary

### 2. Powers

- **Armour and weapons**: conjured full-body armour of dark steel plates held together with large bolts, engraved with runes (Book 1, Ch16 L28); he fights with a huge hammer and a shield (Book 1, Ch109 L14). He forges a hammer himself to fight constructs (Book 2, Ch76 L45).
- **Forge golem**: he pours iron filings into a circle, his hand bursts into flame, and a three-metre golem of ugly black iron rises with molten metal between the joints (Book 1, Ch17 L56-61). The text calls it a foundry golem when he names it (Book 1, Ch17 L61) and a forge golem later (Book 2, Ch28 L46; Book 3, Ch6 L35). It is a temporary summon, unlike a familiar (Book 2, Ch28 L46). Summoning powers need ritual understanding, as for Farrah (Book 1, Ch23 L37).
- **Forge powers**: he uses a forge essence power to repair a wagon wheel (Book 1, Ch31 L40), and creates "alloys heavier and stronger than steel" for the academy annex (Book 3, Ch26 L9).
- **Smithing**: he forges Jason's Dread Salvation (Book 4, Ch70 L44; see Hegemons_Will_and_Dominion.md for its later reforging).

- **Rescue work**: in the Book 9 surge he and other craftspeople shape stone and reinforce structures, and his golem's chest plate opens to carry survivors (Book 9, Ch82 L22-24).

### 4. Not in the text

His Book 1 to Book 9 abilities are not shown as System windows in the lines read, so the ranks and costs of his hammer, fire and iron abilities are not stated.

## Humphrey Geller's Power Set

### 1. Summary

Humphrey is the team's heavy hitter: "simple, reliable power" (Book 3, Ch43 L69). His dragon, wing and might essences give him a dragon-winged flyer with fire, a weapon-heavy attack set and racial gifts that scale with risk. His familiar is the mirage dragon Stash.

### 2. Essences and confluence

- **Dragon confluence**: reached through the might and wing essences (Book 9, Ch33 L15; Essence_Catalogue.md lists him with might, wing and dragon, Book 4, Ch65 L28).
- **Dragon abilities**: he awakens all five dragon essence abilities when he gets Spartoi, the party interface reporting it (Book 1, Ch88 L94-96).

### 3. Abilities

| Ability (essence) | Type and cost | Effect | Cite |
|---|---|---|---|
| Spartoi (dragon) | summoning ritual, very high mana, 6 hour cooldown | Summons three dragon-tooth warriors at iron | Book 1, Ch88 L97 |
| Fire Breath (dragon) | special attack, very high mana, 50 second cooldown | A stream of fire lasting several seconds; at bronze anyone burned suffers ongoing fire damage | Book 3, Ch63 L25 |
| Dragon Armour (dragon) | conjuration, high mana | Dragon-scale armour: strong physical protection and fire resistance; bronze adds resistance to non-physical damage | Book 3, Ch40 L80 |
| Dragon Wings (wing) | conjuration, high mana per second | Powerful but unagile wings; bronze lets them make crude attacks to the sides and rear and gives strong damage and fire resistance | Book 3, Ch43 L7 |
| Dragon Wing Sword (wing) | conjuration (fire), high mana, 1 minute cooldown | A huge sword in the shape of a dragon's wing; movement special attacks with it do extra damage; at bronze its attacks burn | Book 3, Ch40 L63 |
| Dive Bomb (wing) | special attack, high stamina, 20 second cooldown | Accelerate down onto a target, combinable with other melee attacks; no falling damage; bronze adds a shockwave | Book 3, Ch43 L46 |
| Flying Leap (wing) | special attack, low stamina, 10 second cooldown | A swift leap with limited air control, combinable with melee attacks; bronze increases all damage from combined melee special attacks | Book 3, Ch58 L20 |
| Unstoppable Force (might) | special attack, high mana and extreme stamina, 1 minute cooldown | A melee attack with massive momentum dealing extra resonating-force and disruptive-force damage; needs a heavy weapon | Book 3, Ch40 L72 |
| Shield Breaker (might) | special attack, 10 second cooldown | Extra resonating-force damage, strong against physical defences; bronze greatly increases damage to rigid material | Book 3, Ch43 L58 |
| Relentless Assault (might) | special attack | Each use in quick succession increases the damage; at bronze, escalating resonating-force damage past a threshold | Book 3, Ch43 L154 |
| Immortality (might) | special ability, 24 hour cooldown | Instantly restores a large portion of health, mana and stamina, based on how depleted they are | Book 2, Ch64 L86 |

### 4. Racial gifts

- **Hero's Drive** (from Human Ambition): essence abilities advance faster; higher-ranked enemies gain no extra resistance or damage reduction against his abilities; cooldowns are reduced when fighting higher-ranked or much more numerous enemies; mana and stamina gained over time (Book 1, Ch88 L25-30).
- **Attack of the Mirage Dragon** (from Special Attack Affinity): the evolution is reported for him in Book 2 (Book 2, Ch61 L147; Book 2, Ch62 L53). Neil asks "Why mirage dragon?" and Humphrey answers that Stash is a mirage dragon (Book 2, Ch62 L49-50).
- **Essence Gift evolutions**: Dragon Blood enhances Power and Spirit for moderate mana per second (Book 3, Ch43 L42-44); Wing Raider (Book 3, Ch43 L54); Unstoppable makes movement abilities impossible to negate or impair and imparts resonating-force and disruptive-force damage to obstructing objects (Book 3, Ch43 L70-72). The text shows it triggering against a Sphere of Incarceration (Book 3, Ch43 L66-68).
- **Hero's Sacrifice**: his evolved gift breaches his soul's limits: "Sacrifice your health to enhance the power of your special attacks", and the earlier effects of Attack of the Mirage Dragon are lost (Book 3, Ch68 L68-73).

### 5. Later (Book 9)

- **Razor-Wing Sword** (wing, silver 4, 11%): a conjured sword shaped like a wing; movement powers are enhanced while wielding it; feathers can be thrown as projectiles at bronze and animated to intercept physical projectiles at silver (Book 9, Ch59 L71).
- **Dragon Might** (dragon): an aura (recovery) (Book 9, Ch59 L82). Separately, his dragon essence racial gift lets him burn mana to increase his physical and magical strength (Book 2, Ch62 L47).

### 6. How the set plays

He opens with Dragon Wings and Dive Bomb or Flying Leap, stacks Unstoppable Force or Relentless Assault into a single hit and relies on teammates for shields: Neil's shielding and healing and Clive's retributive effects let him leave openings to attack (Book 2, Ch30 L54). Compare Taika's garuda set, built on the same might and wing essences (Taika_Power_Set.md).

### 7. Not in the text

## Neil Davone's Power Set

### 1. Summary

Neil is the team's healer and shield-caster: Shield, Growth and Renewal, producing the Prosperity confluence (Jason's notebook, Book 2, Ch28 L44). His set is built around timed, short-lived shields, buffs on allies and ritual healing, and Prosperity luck effects. Rufus is struck by the shielding: the shields "lasted only moments and would end after absorbing only a single attack", so they need good judgement and timing (Book 2, Ch30 L52).

| Burst Shield (shield) | special ability, moderate mana, 20 second cooldown | Short-lived shield that negates an attack and explodes, knocking enemies back with concussive damage; bronze inflicts Vibrant Echo | Book 3, Ch40 L108 |

| Giant's Might (growth) | spell, high mana, 10 minute cooldown | Target ally and equipment grow larger with enhanced Power; bronze adds resistance to physical damage | Book 3, Ch48 L46 |

### 3. Racial gifts

- **Life Affinity to Life Guard** (elf): effects used or received with a positive effect on life have greater effect; a shield-based essence ability on allies also gives a heal-over-time (Book 2, Ch61 L145; Book 2, Ch62 L36-40).

### 4. At silver (Books 8 and 9)

| Ability | Rank and effect | Cite |

| Burst Shield | Silver 2: inflicts Slow Learner, which increases all retributive damage suffered by the target | Book 8, Ch7 L50 |
| Grand Renewal | Silver 2: multiple people can be healed in one ritual; better at removing wound afflictions | Book 8, Ch7 L100 |
| Verdant Cage (growth) | Silver 1: binding plants with thorns that carry poison suited to the environment | Book 8, Ch10 L31 |
| Reaper's Redoubt | Silver 3: enemies are afflicted with Death's Grip | Book 9, Ch60 L33 |
| Absorbing Shield | Silver 4: the recipient gains Priority Ward | Book 9, Ch85 L16 |

### 5. How the set plays

He keeps buffs running (armour of renewal, which reduces damage taken and heals over time, and the size-growth power, Book 2, Ch30 L53) and times shield bubbles for teammates; Humphrey trusts Neil's shield so completely that he does not even block (Book 8, Ch7 L49). His shield's retribution boost makes Clive's effects stronger (Book 8, Ch7 L60). Hero's Moment is a team-wide trick with a price: when it wears off, the recipients suffer "debilitating effects as potent as the boosts" (Book 3, Ch59 L101).

### 6. Not in the text

His gold-rank abilities and his Prosperity abilities beyond bronze are not shown in the lines read.

## Rufus Remore's Power Set

### 1. Summary

Rufus is a swordsman-teleporter built on the sun and moon: a skilled fighter whose powers blend movement, teleportation, illusory after-images and attacks. In the Greenstone years he fights as a bronze-ranker with a golden and a silver sword; by Book 9 his essence is described as the eclipse confluence, and his combat shifts between sun, moon and eclipse states.

### 2. Powers (Books 1 and 2)

- **Conjured swords**: a golden sword and a silver sword that appear in his hand (Book 1, Ch108 L96-98; Book 1, Ch112 L44). The swords leave marks: those cut by the golden sword have a small orb of fire, "a miniature golden sun", floating around them and scorching them; those touched by the silver sword have a tiny moon that soaks up heat instead (Book 2, Ch76 L51).
- **Speed of light**: when he activates it "the world seemed to freeze in time around him", giving "only two seconds of accelerated time" (Book 1, Ch108 L99); the text calls it "one of his strongest trump cards" (Book 1, Ch109 L23).
- **Movement chain**: unlike Jason, he does not have one teleport he can use over and over; "he had a slew of powers that blended movement, teleportation, illusionary after-images and attacks" and chaining them takes skill and practice (Book 2, Ch76 L66).
- **Water-walking boots** are noted in his character file (Book 1, Ch16 L38).
- **Kingdom of Eclipse**: his incantation-driven power, whose chant ends "bring forth the kingdom of eclipse" (Book 2, Ch76 L71); it fires an eclipse orb that kills dozens (Book 2, Ch76; see Rufus_Remore.md).

### 3. At silver (Books 9 and 10)

- **The three states**: "Rufus had the eclipse confluence essence." The sun state is built around speed and offence, the moon state around elusiveness and stealth, and the eclipse state offers powerful but short-term buffs or powerful finishers; each state combines how he fights, moves and which powers he uses (Book 9, Ch20 L12-13).
- **Light of the Sun, Shadow of the Moon**: one of several abilities that change with his state; in the moon state it can make him intangible for a few seconds (Book 9, Ch20 L14).
- **Eclipse zone**: his zone power, Eclipse, consumes the afflictions of the gold and silver flames he leaves on monsters (Book 9, Ch88 L53). His gold and silver swords leave gold and silver flames in his wake (Book 9, Ch89 L24).
- **Mobility**: silver-rank acrobatics and short-range teleports let him spend little time on the ground (Book 9, Ch89 L22).

### 4. How the set plays

He is a skirmisher who strikes, vanishes and re-appears; the moon state lets him dodge and the sun state lets him close the gap. In a duel Soramir notes he baited his opponent by burning one of his strongest abilities on a statement attack (Book 9, Ch20 L20).

### 5. Not in the text

No System window for Rufus's abilities is shown in the lines read; his essence list is the Sun essence (Book 9, Ch20 L16; Essence_Catalogue.md) with the eclipse confluence (Book 9, Ch20 L12), and the other essences are not stated here.

## Sophie Wexler's Power Set

### 1. Summary

Sophie is a skill-based speed fighter: a celestine whose Swift, Balance and Wind essences form the Mystic confluence. Her set stacks movement, perception and unarmed techniques around one idea, acting faster and more precisely than the enemy. Before Jason's help she had only the Swift essence and its single ability for more than half a dozen years (Book 2, Ch5 L37-41).

### 2. Essences and confluence

- **Choice**: Swift, Balance and Wind, producing the Mystic confluence, which Clive names when Rufus asks (Book 2, Ch5 L82-87). She absorbs Wind, Balance and Mystic in order (Book 2, Ch7 L118, L123, L152).
- **Stones**: five awakening stones are chosen for the team after the Reaper trials; Sophie takes a stone of the celestials on Constance's advice, and Clive hands her his karma stone (Book 2, Ch63 L25, L30-31). She also trades a death essence for two epic stones of the moment, and picks uncommon stones of preparation and of the surge for a skill-oriented set (Book 2, Ch65 L96-98).

### 3. Abilities

Iron-rank effects as shown when awakened.

| Cloud Step (balance) | special ability, 20 second cooldown | One step on air as solid ground, becoming intangible for a moment; no cooldown in mist, fog or cloud | Book 2, Ch8 L122 |
| Mirage Step (mystic) | special ability, 40 second cooldown | Move instantly to a nearby location, leaving an afterimage | Book 2, Ch8 L173 |

| Alacrity's Reward (swift) | special ability (holy) | Accumulates instances of [Blessing of Anticipation] over time, faster with speed of movement | Book 2, Ch66 L68 |

| Child of the Celestial Wind (wind) | special ability (dimension, holy) | Celestine racial powers have increased effect; damage reduction to disruptive-force damage | Book 2, Ch67 L6 |

### 4. Racial gifts

- **Mana Integrity to Mana Wellspring** (celestine): ongoing mana costs of maintained abilities are reduced, resistance to mana drain increased, and mana regeneration for her and allies in her aura significantly increased when mana is not in use (Book 2, Ch61 L146; Book 2, Ch62 L41-44).
- **Astral Affinity to Boundary Breaker** (Book 3, Ch68 L15; see Sophie_Wexler.md for its effect against rank disparity).

- **Special Ability Affinity to Way of the Wind**: special abilities have increased effect (Book 8, Ch21 L36).
- **Celestial Swiftness to Princess of the Firmament** (Book 8, Ch22 L37).

### 4A. At silver (Book 8)

| Ability | Rank and effect | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| Between the Raindrops | Silver 3: dodge using spatial distortion | Book 8, Ch11 L13 |
| Mirage Step | Silver 2: a third use; afterimages fire dimensional blades at random enemies | Book 8, Ch11 L23 |
| Eternal Moment | Silver 2: time increases to 3 seconds at silver (2 at bronze) | Book 8, Ch11 L32 |
| Wind Blade | Silver 2: blades explode on impact, detonating a ring of cutting force from each penetrated enemy | Book 8, Ch11 L42 |
| Immortal Fist | Silver 3: damage increases with each blow in rapid consecutive attacks | Book 8, Ch11 L52 |
| Radiant Fist | Silver 2: after intercepting a magical projectile she may make a disruptive-force projectile attack | Book 8, Ch11 L66 |
| Child of the Celestial Wind | Silver 2: boons with maximum effect thresholds have them raised | Book 8, Ch11 L76 |
| Karmic Warrior | Silver 3: when an enemy with Bad Karma dies, her cooldowns are reduced for each instance of Good Karma | Book 8, Ch11 L88 |
| Wind Wave | Silver 2: a high-mana circle of extremely powerful pushing wind she can aim selectively | Book 8, Ch8 L54 |
| Leaf on the Wind | Silver 3: flight for moderate mana per second, with control of airflow, carrying others | Book 8, Ch21 L12 |
| Free Runner | Silver 4: controlled leaps and chained glide and flight to exceed normal speeds | Book 8, Ch21 L23 |
| Cloud Step | Silver 2: a third use; intangibility can be extended within mist | Book 8, Ch22 L13 |

### 5. How the set plays

Wind Blade and Wind Wave give her ranged and mobility options, Free Runner, Leaf on the Wind and Cloud Step carry her across walls, water and air, and the Mystic fist abilities turn her unarmed strikes into attacks that bypass physical and magical defences. Clive argued that stones of the moment suit a skill-based power set because their abilities "require precise timing but were incredibly impactful" (Book 2, Ch65 L97).

### 6. Not in the text

Her abilities' ranks after the iron entries above and the effect of her higher-rank rewrites are not shown in the lines read.

## Calendar, Holidays, Naming Customs and Forms of Address

### 1. Summary

The Pallimustus calendar is close to Earth's but not the same: twelve months of thirty days, five extra days and a six-day week with simple day names. Naming customs and forms of address mark rank, family and role, and Rimaros has a distinctive palindrome custom for prominent adults.

### 2. Calendar

- **Months and seasons**: "twelve months of thirty days, divided up into early, mid and late stages of each season" (Book 1, Ch22 L19). Seasons appear in the text as early autumn (Book 2, Ch20 L3).
- **Extra days**: "five additional days that didn't count as days of the month for the solstices, the equinoxes and the new year, which was at the beginning of spring" (Book 1, Ch22 L19).
- **The six-day week**: Jason acknowledges "you have a six-day week" (Book 6, Ch10 L41).
- **Day names**: the names are "super-simple": Jason muddles "Fifthday" and "Fourthday" (Book 8, Ch58 L19).

### 3. Holidays and festivals

The festivals the text names (Greenstone's flower wine festival, post-surge festivals in Rimaros) are collected in Clothing_Food_Festivals_and_Transport_by_Region.md. The text names no religious holiday on Pallimustus in the lines read; the holiday Jason refers to in Book 2 is an Earth one ("a religious holiday that we appropriated to stimulate the economy once a year", Book 2, Ch52 L32).

### 4. Naming customs

- **Palindromic names**: "in the local culture, palindromic first and last names were assigned in adulthood rather than at birth and denoted important members of a given family", most common in small families and for a prominent adventurer or high civic official; the more prominent the family, the less common the practice (Book 7, Ch18 L42).
- **Mononyms**: Zato says his name is "my only name" (Book 3, Ch57 L121-122).
- **Family names**: "family names appeared on plaques near the entry gates" of Greenstone's houses (Book 2, Ch23 L3). The Gellers send scions from branches around the world to train in Greenstone even where they share a last name only (Book 1, Ch66 L4).

- **Dropped names**: Elizabeth the vampire says she "discarded" her last name "long before you were born" (Book 6, Ch41 L116-117).
- **First-name usage**: using a first name is a step in a relationship (Book 5, Ch35 L71); Shade uses first names only for people he likes (Book 9, Ch93 L91-92). Jason gives his surname and personal name in that order, "My surname is Asano and my personal name is Jason" (Book 9, Ch100 L23).

### 5. Forms of address

- **Servants to nobles**: "milady" and "milord" (Book 1, Ch9 L28, L53). A cult high priestess styles herself "Lady Cressida Vane" (Book 1, Ch11 L80); "Lady Geller" is used for Danielle (Book 1, Ch37 L8).
- **Officials and clergy**: "Madam Director" for a director present at the star-seed ritual (Book 2, Ch19 L75); "Instructor" for assessors (Book 1, Ch54 L36); "Priestess Pellin" (Book 7, Ch9 L18). See Tone_and_Voice.md.
- **Royalty**: the Mirror King is "Your Majesty" (Book 3, Ch5 L35), and kneeling is expected before him (Book 3, Ch5 L23).

- **Messengers**: "lord" whatever their sex, and "Voice Kaal" for a Voice of the Will (Book 9, Ch56 L13-14).

### 6. Not in the text

The names of the months and seasons, a complete list of the day names, religious holidays and the full rules for palindromic names are not given in the lines read.

---

## Celestines

### 1. Summary

Metallic-haired, metallic-eyed people with an astral affinity, produced when two outworlders have a child.

### 2. Facts

- **Appearance**: Sophie has chocolate skin and shining silver hair, her eyes matching it exactly, "the matching metallics a giveaway trait of the celestine race" (Book 1, Ch43 L18). In Kurdansk many celestines have dark skin and silver hair (Book 6, Ch47 L508).
- **Astral affinity**: astral affinity is a celestine racial gift (Book 1, Ch15 L76-80); celestines are similar to elves and humans (Book 1, Ch15 L78). The rare Child of the Celestial Wind appears only in races with astral affinity, on Pallimustus meaning celestines, or potentially outworlders (Book 8, Ch21 L40).
- **Origin**: Dawn says two outworlders who have a child produce a celestine, and celestines propagate with their own kind; she is herself a child of two outworlders (Book 5, Ch44 L72).
- **Gift evolutions**: Sophie's Mana Integrity becomes Mana Wellspring (Book 2, Ch61 L146), her Astral Affinity becomes Boundary Breaker (Book 3, Ch68 L14) and her Celestial Swiftness becomes Princess of the Firmament (Book 8, Ch22 L37).
- **Communities**: Zartos is a subterranean city largely populated by celestines (Book 7, Ch2 L75).
- **Messengers**: messengers are about half again as tall as a celestine, and their servants take care never to imply the resemblance (Book 9, Ch55 L9).
- **Dawn's true form**: a celestine with ruby eyes and hair (Book 7, Ch54 L87).

### 3. Using this in a story

Celestines are visibly striking and often the children of the outworlder arrivals; their culture is as varied as any people's.

---

## Clothing, Food, Festivals and Transport by Region

### 1. Summary

A regional table of how people dress, eat, celebrate and travel. `Daily_Life.md` covers Greenstone and the delta in detail; this file adds Rimaros and the Storm Kingdom, Yaresh, Kurdansk, Pranay, Vitesse and smaller places, and gathers the festivals the text names.

### 2. Festivals

- **Greenstone's flower wine festival**: a festival in the Greenstone year that Gary and his friends attend (Book 1, Ch49 L79, L113); afterwards Jason asks Bahadir "How was the wine festival?" (Book 1, Ch99 L39).
- **Memorials as celebrations**: for a dead adventurer the mood "shifted from mourning to a celebration of life" at a wake (Book 1, Ch91 L64).
- **A town festival for weeks**: a small town on a lake had been "going through what was essentially a festival for the better part of three weeks" as adventurers poured through (Book 2, Ch62 L3).
- **Wall-completion feast**: a small town holds a feast with tables and spit roasts in the central square to celebrate completing its new walls (Book 6, Ch47 L350).
- **Kurdansk parade**: Neil and the others merge into "a raucous parade" in a Kurdansk street (Book 6, Ch47 L424).
- **Rimaros after the surge**: "The post-surge festivals here are world-famous" (Book 8, Ch37 L63). At the end of the surge the Magic Society announces the end and Rimaros holds a lengthy festival, "a week for some much-needed celebratory catharsis" (Book 9, Ch2 L16-19). The street festival takes over the market district, boutique ward and Adventure Society campus, with food stalls everywhere and thousands of colour-changing paper lanterns released by the Magic Society (Book 9, Ch3 L3). The aristocratic celebration ball is a political "show"; "the festivals on the streets are the real celebration" (Book 9, Ch2 L20). Formalwear for the ball is tailored for the team (Book 9, Ch13 L3-25).

### 3. By region

#### Greenstone and the delta

See Daily_Life.md: loose breathable clothes for the heat (Book 1, Ch59 L30), familiar dishes with different ingredients (Book 1, Ch45 L8), spirit-coin-run household devices (Book 1, Ch80 L40), heidels and the loop (Book 1, Ch17 L106; Book 1, Ch43 L5).

#### Rimaros and the Storm Kingdom

- **Food**: the night markets of Livaros supply breakfast ingredients (Book 7, Ch12 L66); the stalls serve deep-fried mash sausage on a stick (made of vegetables) and sweet-and-savoury dumplings (Book 7, Ch12 L24-34); a woman eats a fruit pastry as Jason questions her (Book 7, Ch11 L62). Jason's clifftop barbecue draws crowds (Book 7, Ch18 L3).
- **Dress**: formal pantsuits with defensive magical augmentation for the team at the ball, tailored by Alejandro Albericci, who notes leonid fashion has "given me pause" (Book 9, Ch13 L19-25).
- **Transport**: flying carriages reach the royal sky island through a defended ring (Book 7, Ch17 L3, L16). See Rimaros.md for the city's layout.

#### Yaresh

- **Dress**: elves wear "neat casual suits", like business suits "worn loose, with long, tapered sleeves and coattails", in light colours by night and darker shades for thugs (Book 9, Ch44 L16). Elf fashion has flowing, tapered lines (Book 9, Ch46 L5).
- **People and hair**: skin tones from almond to milk chocolate, and hair from honey to rich brown, in waves, ringlets or frizz (Book 9, Ch43 L64).
- **Food and drink**: bars, cafes and places offering delights "from the chaste to the downright saucy" in the entertainment district, and small pubs with chalkboard menus (Book 9, Ch43 L85).
- **Transport**: land skimmers, carriages and personal floatation discs are present but minimal and "never driven by the elves that made up the bulk of the population" (Book 9, Ch43 L64).

#### Kurdansk (Dreisil)

- **Transport**: see Kurdansk_Culture.md for the skyport.
- **Festivals**: a street parade (above).

#### Pranay and Jayapura

- **Dress and food**: the team's visit brings "new customs, new food. New sights and sounds, tastes and smells" (Book 2, Ch74 L14); Jason recalls kulfi-like ice cream (Book 7, Ch14 L52).
- **Transport**: platter-sized floating discs for those who can afford them (Book 2, Ch70 L3).

#### Vitesse and Estercost

See Vitesse_Culture.md and Estercost_Culture.md.

### 4. Not in the text

Regional recipes, festival names beyond the flower wine festival, and Pallimustus dress codes by rank are not described in the lines read. This file does not claim to cover every meal or outfit in the series.
