## The Cabal

### 1. Summary

The Cabal is the oldest of Earth's three dominant magical forces: a loose collection of factions of vampires, fae and other creatures older than recorded history, with old money and old habits. It split when ancient vampires were woken and the vampire lords rose. Part of it sides with humanity and, in the end, is openly led by Boris Ket Lundi. Earth_Powers.md covers it briefly; this file is the full account.

### 2. Structure and character

- **The old magic**: "The Cabal represents the old magic of this world. Things older than history that dwell in the dark places" (Book 4, Ch10 L133). It is one of the three dominant forces beside the Network and the EOA, with a tight grip on old money (Book 4, Ch10 L127; Book 4, Ch11 L101).
- **Its official line on Jason**: Vermillion says it is the Cabal's position that Jason's liberty and independence be respected (Book 4, Ch15 L70).
- **Rivalry with the EOA**: the EOA's research into blood servants causes conflicts with the Cabal, which dislikes having its people kidnapped as research materials (Book 4, Ch11 L78).
- **Help in the monster waves**: individual factions help in places such as Siberia and the South Pacific where the Network is thin (Book 5, Ch24 L32).

### 3. Later developments, Books 5 to 6

- **Stronger in the zones**: transformation zones make Cabal members stronger, since many had hit a ceiling in a world not magical enough, and the Cabal starts to approach parity with the Network (Book 5, Ch57 L8).
- **The vampires' problem**: vampires hit a different ceiling and fall into torpor, so their leaders must hibernate. They rush to wake their ancient ones, and the rest of the Cabal "reluctantly went along due to the need to compete with the Network" (Book 5, Ch57 L9-10). Lord Willoughby has been hibernating in a sarcophagus since 1794 (Book 5, Ch57 L16).
- **The attack on Sydney**: the Cabal makes an open assault on the Network building in the Sydney CBD, after which authorities cordon off the area (Book 5, Ch59 L32).
- **Splinters**: Craig Vermillion leads a splinter faction that does not work with the old vampires (Book 5, Ch62 L60), and by Book 6 the Cabal has fragmented. Some have formed a non-vampiric new Cabal and others have joined the Global Defence Network (Book 6, Ch25 L61).
- **The Arisen**: William Spencer, an ancient vampire and one of the Arisen (the vampires' own name), says not all of them want global dominion, and some are looking to carve out a place for themselves after the vampires lose (Book 6, Ch25 L60-64).

### 5. Using this in a story

The Cabal is the face of old power that has chosen a side. Use its members as grim, courteous, long-lived allies with their own agendas, and remember that the Cabal's help comes with a favour. A character who is "Cabal" can be a vampire, a fae, or a human with old-money ties, and a character who has gone over to the vampire lords is no longer one of them.

---

## The Network

### 1. Summary

The Network is Earth's largest magical organisation: a federation of old secret societies, now national and city branches, that uses essence magic, controls most of the world's spirit coin supply, and maintains the Grid that finds and contains incursions. It went public as the Global Defense Network when the monster waves began, and later fractured into four blocs. Earth_Powers.md covers it beside the other factions; this file is the full account.

### 2. Structure

- **A federation, not a hierarchy**: "the Network isn't one large hierarchy. It's an organisation of old secret societies and the International Committee is more like a United Nations than an overlord. The branches are members, not subordinates" (Book 4, Ch35 L117).
- **The International Committee (IC)**: it holds local offices, such as the Canberra office (Book 4, Ch44 L82), and global executives; Asya represents it in Sydney (Book 4, Ch43 L3). Only the agreement of a majority of member branches can act punitively against a branch; the IC itself has no such authority (Book 4, Ch51 L8).
- **Branches**: the books name Sydney, Lyon, Fiji (as a joke about living on a beach, Book 4, Ch19 L56) and Berlin, where Cleary and Gerling work at the branch with its own grid monitoring station and Steering Committee (Book 5, Ch48 L18, L50, L57). The IC also has offices in Brussels, Shanghai, New York, Johannesburg and Cairo (Book 5, Ch8 L73).
- **Steering committees**: each branch has one, which directs the agenda and takes high-level decisions; the Sydney committee has eight members and Anna is promoted into it (Book 4, Ch19 L48; Book 4, Ch59 L11).
- **Divisions**: a branch has an Operations Director (Adrien Barbou in Lyon, Book 4, Ch43 L58), a Director of Tactical Operations (Koen in Sydney, Book 4, Ch40 L9, L32), and a Harvest Division headed by Eustace Brown (Book 4, Ch43 L3). The Research Division has been "divorced from specific branches and brought under the umbrella of the International Committee" so that breakthroughs are shared across the Network (Book 4, Ch46 L80).
- **Tactical teams**: the Sydney tactical presence is two platoons of three nine-person sections, with five category threes (Book 4, Ch40 L3-4). Nigel Thornton was a section leader there before leaving (Book 4, Ch40 L32).
- **Spirit coins**: the stronger branches in the US, China and Russia are the primary source of spirit coins and resist placing tactical forces under the IC (Book 4, Ch37 L78). The Sydney branch has none of its own and depends on the IC (Book 4, Ch59 L4).

### 3. Categories and cores

- **Ranks**: Network categories zero to five match the ranks on Pallimustus (Book 4, Ch11 L8, L19).
- **Who gets what**: all executive-level personnel are given essences to reach category one; cores are mostly saved for lower-ranked enforcement members on the front line, and only committee members like Keith go to category two with cores (Book 4, Ch36 L77).

### 4. The Grid

- **What it is**: the Grid exists to identify and monitor dimensional spaces, and every branch has access to it (Book 4, Ch51 L35).
- **A concealed sector**: in 1948 the Lyon branch's committee hid an incursion space that had become permanent near Saint-Étienne by disabling the Grid in that area, and the sector has been offline for seventy years (Book 4, Ch51 L30-37).

### 5. Going public and splitting

- **The public name**: the Network "had become known to the public as the Global Defense Network in the weeks since the monster waves began", presented as the government response through "supernatural task forces" (Book 5, Ch17 L7).
- **The EOA's campaign**: the EOA tried to swing public opinion so strongly that governments would give it a seat at the table (Book 5, Ch17 L8-9).
- **Anger at the IC**: branches resent resources being allocated to fighting alongside the Americans, and Berlin is furious at losing people to the IC's order (Book 5, Ch51 L60).
- **Four blocs**: by Book 6 the Network is split into the US branches and the old leadership faction (still calling themselves the Network), the Chinese branches, the True Network (the former IC, which keeps many government links and powerful members), and the Global Defence Network (rank and file with ex-EOA and Cabal members) (Book 6, Ch1 L15-16, L25-27). The US branches staged a secret coup of the US government (Book 6, Ch1 L25).

### 6. Using this in a story

Treat the Network as a federation of old institutions with rivalries and long memories. Local branches act on their own interest, the IC persuades more than commands, and a branch can hide something for seventy years. A story set before the split can lean on politics and spirit coins. A story set after it should check which bloc a character belongs to.

---

## The Church of Death

### 1. Summary

The church of Death is the Pallimustus institution for the passage of the dead: it certifies who has died and returned, helps with registration, and has long watched against necromancy. It works alongside the Cult of the Reaper, which serves as its hidden dagger.

### 2. Role

- **Certification**: before Jason registers with the Magic Society, he is told to visit the Temple of Death for formal confirmation that he is who he claims, "returned from death" (Book 7, Ch7 L64). The paperwork helps with the Magic Society (Book 7, Ch11 L83) and a certification from the Church of Death serves as identification (Book 7, Ch10 L55).
- **The certificate**: Jason's certification says he has died four times, "so far" (Book 7, Ch17 L104).
- **The temple**: it looks like a memorial centre, with white stone, manicured gardens and clergy in white robes (Book 7, Ch9 L88, L102).
- **Verification of deaths**: it is needed to confirm that neither of two missing adventurers had made a miraculous last-minute escape (Book 9, Ch11 L44).

- **The cult**: "The cult often works with the Church of Death... as they are closely aligned. The church does more public-facing things for the cult, while the cult can be the church's hidden dagger" (Book 9, Ch27 L87; Book 6, Ch47 L590).

### 3. Not in the text

The church's hierarchy and leaders are not named in the lines read.

---

## The Church of Dominion

### 1. Summary

The church of Dominion is the church of rulership and the exercise of authority. It is the outlier among churches in keeping low-ranking essence users under its thumb. The god is often seen; his priests rarely are.

### 2. Facts

- **Temple**: in Greenstone it is "the strange temple" on the Divine Square, called the temple of Dominion (Book 1, Ch45 L15).
- **Control of clergy**: other churches make little call on adventuring clergy; Dominion's is the most prominent outlier in keeping low-ranking essence users under its thumb (Book 3, Ch69 L859).

- **Absent priests**: a character thought power and control were the church's remit, but has "never even met a priest of Dominion" (Book 9, Ch49 L28-29).
- **The god's description**: Dominion tells Jason he is "oppression-friendly", not an oppressor (Book 8, Ch56 L47-48).

### 3. Not in the text

No hierarchy, temple network or numbers are given. The god's own comments are in Dominion.md.

---

## The Church of Knowledge

### 1. Summary

The church of Knowledge keeps the libraries, checks the assessments and has its acolytes at hand where information matters. From Book 7 it is also quietly building a fighting force across the world, which at the end of Book 9 stops hiding.

### 2. Role and structure

- **Assessors**: the Adventure Society's assessments are checked by a priest of the church of Knowledge: "Don't try to slip anything past them, because there isn't any point" (Book 1, Ch38 L68).
- **Temple**: in Greenstone the Temple of Knowledge is the plain, blocky building on the Divine Square (Book 1, Ch45 L55). The temple library is used by researchers (Book 2, Ch3 L22; Book 2, Ch25 L37) and the goddess "always welcomes seekers of truth" (Book 2, Ch21 L129).
- **Acolytes and dress**: its members wear recognisable regalia (Book 1, Ch101 L72-73; Book 6, Ch47 L780).
- **Answer room**: the Vitesse temple has an "answer room" where the goddess manifests (Book 6, Ch47 L614).
- **Rimaros**: the main Temple of Knowledge there stands on a sky island apart from the temple district and has a "symbiotic relationship" with the Magic Society branch, with a chamber for incoming portals (Book 7, Ch9 L3-4).
- **Authentication**: it is reluctant to hire out clergy to authenticate valuables (Book 7, Ch15 L40).
- **Education**: it "does what it can" for education, but meets pushback from nobility (Book 9, Ch29 L61).

### 3. The military build-up

- **Quiet muster**: "The Church of Knowledge has been building up fighting forces around the globe. Slowly but steadily, over the last fifteen years or so"; no one knows why, even its own people (Book 7, Ch56 L62-66).
- **Cover blown**: the force is mobilised on a single day, after which it no longer cared about being noticed (Book 9, Ch42 L69-72). The god War alone knew its scale (Book 9, Ch42 L71).
- **Reaching out**: a priest of Knowledge contacts Jason's team because of Jason's relationship with the goddess (Book 9, Ch42 L57-60).

### 5. Not in the text

Ranks beyond acolyte and priest(ess), and the church's command structure for its forces, are not stated in the lines read.

---

## The Church of Purity and the Lasalle Family

### 1. Summary

The church of Purity is the church of cleansing: it supplies the healers who treat curses and taint, and it first developed the techniques for removing star seeds. In Book 3 its leadership vanishes when its involvement with the Builder cult comes to light, and the churches later learn it has been operating outside its dictates for some time.

### 2. Role and members

- **Healers and cleansers**: the church sends a healer, a girl from the Lasalle family, with Rufus's party (Book 1, Ch65 L130). Anisa Lasalle is a priestess of Purity (Book 1, Ch20 L80).
- **Contracts**: it makes noise about the completion bonus after Anisa leaves, but the contract was through the Adventure Society and was paid (Book 1, Ch41 L87).
- **Rituals**: the archbishop prepares a ritual circle of silver-rank spirit coins and orbs; such rituals "draw on the power of ambient magic but are infused with the glorious might of the divine. My god's will moves the magic and not the other way around" (Book 2, Ch19 L79-80). He is emotionless and cold at the death of Jonah Geller (Book 2, Ch19 L99).
- **Star seeds**: the church first developed the extraction techniques (Book 3, Ch22 L35) and the archbishop holds its records on them (Book 2, Ch21 L34, L127).
- **The archbishop**: the book calls him Nicolas Hendren (Book 2, Ch19 L73; Book 3, Ch31 L57) but also "Herston, the archbishop of Purity" in one scene (Book 2, Ch21 L21), and Book 3 spells it "Hedron" once (Book 3, Ch1 L106). The text is inconsistent; this compendium uses Nicolas Hendren.

### 3. The fall of the Greenstone leadership

- **Vanishing**: following the revelation of Purity's apparent involvement with the Builder cult, the archbishop vanishes along with other key members on "previously scheduled sabbaticals" (Book 3, Ch31 L57; Book 3, Ch1 L106). The Healer's new Chief Priest takes his seat at the war council (Book 3, Ch31 L57).
- **The Lasalle family**: Anisa and "almost the entire Lasalle family" vanish with the archbishop (Book 3, Ch1 L106; see Anisa_Lasalle.md). The vanished include Anisa, who joins the Builder's cult.

### 4. The ecumenical council and the false god

- **Council**: the churches convene an ecumenical council to judge the church's behaviour; it finds the church "had been operating well outside their own dictates" (Book 8, Ch48 L32). People from the church are detained under its authority (Book 3, Ch5 L67).

### 6. Not in the text

The church's rank system and the Lasalle family's size are not stated; the Lasalle family is named for its healer and for Anisa.

---

## The Church of Undeath

### 1. Summary

The church of Undeath serves the god of undeath and raises the unliving. It is stamped out wherever it is found, lies dormant for generations, and strikes only when given a chance. Its priests are unliving things led by Garth Larosse.

### 2. Facts

- **A past plague**: a zombie plague had "a Temple of Undeath at the heart of it all", with the local mayor as high priest (Book 7, Ch9 L99).

### 3. Not in the text

No rank system apart from "high priest" and "priests" is stated.

---

## The Church of the Healer

### 1. Summary

The church of the Healer is the institution through which most healing on Pallimustus is done, with its priests drawing their abilities from the god's essences and awakening stones. It is strongest in isolated areas, and its Greenstone branch is the subject of the first great church scandal in the books.

### 2. Role and structure

- **Healing service**: "Most healing was done through the church of the Healer"; the god supplies the essences and awakening stones; its priests can be sought out for a fee and send people to other healers (Book 1, Ch35 L23).
- **Local reach**: it is "really important in isolated areas", though "more than one church leader has been known to go a bit power mad" (Book 1, Ch38 L34). Members are found in multiple towns and villages on the road to Greenstone (Book 1, Ch96 L16).
- **Hierarchy**: the Greenstone temple stands on the Divine Square (Book 1, Ch94 L3), led by a Chief Priest who arrives with bronze-rankers, iron-rankers and lesser clergy behind. The basic robe is simple brown, though the Greenstone group wore white-and-gold silks with brown embellishments (Book 1, Ch95 L8). High Priestess is the title at city level (Book 9, Ch74 L43).
- **Adventuring clergy**: like most faiths, the church makes little call on the time of adventuring clergy (Book 3, Ch69 L859). Neil Davone is a long-time churchman (Book 3, Ch69 L859).

### 3. The Greenstone scandal

- **Replacement**: the Healer replaces the excommunicated Greenstone clergy. A replacement priest, Donal Lange, arrives through a portal the Healer creates (Book 1, Ch102 L73). Priests then stop charging for services (Book 2, Ch16 L73).
- **The new Chief Priest**: after Purity's Greenstone leaders vanish, the new Chief Priest of the Healer takes the vanished archbishop's place at the war council (Book 3, Ch31 L57).
- **Sanctuary**: the Healer makes Jory's clinic sanctified ground, which stops others breaking in for the formula (Book 3, Ch16 L82).

### 4. Specialists and the wider church

- **Star-seed work**: "The church of the Healer has taken over from Purity in dealing with the star seeds"; the Healer provided the rituals (Book 3, Ch21 L73). Carlos Quilido, a gold-rank priest, is the specialist (Book 3, Ch22 L3, L35).
- **Mental health**: Arabelle Remore is a member specialising in mental health and trauma recovery (Book 8, Ch2 L49).

- **Mandate**: the church gives Jory a mandate and funding to spread his methods around the world (Book 5, Ch43 L94). It has its own skyships (Book 6, Ch47 L482).
- **Summons**: it can call away members such as Jory (Book 7, Ch16 L6).
- **Yaresh**: Hana Shavar is High Priestess of the Healer in Yaresh and takes personal charge of refugees (Book 9, Ch74 L43).

### 5. Not in the text

The church's structure above the city level, its relation to the other churches' councils, and the rank titles between priest and Chief Priest are not stated in the lines read.

## The Burning Violet Guild

### 1. Summary

### 2. Facts

- **Origin and links**: "an old guild, but after Rufus' grandfather became guild leader, it became more and more associated with the Remore Academy" (Book 6, Ch47 L125). Its membership is Rufus's family plus allies such as Gary and Farrah (Book 6, Ch47 L125).
- **Members**: most big-family graduates of the Academy do not join, so it is mostly graduates who do not come from power or money (Book 6, Ch47 L127). The Academy's scouting programme gives scholarships to people with potential (Book 6, Ch47 L129).
- **Symbol**: purple flames in the shape of a flower on a belt buckle (Book 7, Ch16 L3). Farrah introduces herself as "Burning Violet guild, although I don't have a guild pin on me" (Book 7, Ch7 L53).

### 4. Not in the text

Its size, ranks, fees and executive membership beyond Neiman Bassingthwaite are not stated.

---

## Guild Quotas and the Contribution Board

### 1. Summary

During a monster surge the Adventure Society assigns every adventurer a minimum action quota, and runs a contribution board as an incentive on top. Guilds are given the bulk of the quotas and compete for the board's non-material rewards.

### 2. Facts

- **Action quotas**: after clearance an adventurer is "assigned an action quota" and must report regularly to the jobs hall for contracts (Book 7, Ch10 L124). "Action quotas are the minimum requirements every adventurer has to fulfil during the monster surge" (Book 7, Ch12 L15).
- **The contribution board**: contracts taken beyond the quota carry contribution point values, tallied on weekly and overall leaderboards, with rewards each week and major rewards at the end of the surge (Book 7, Ch12 L15). A large leaderboard in the jobs hall shows the top hundred contributors by action quota (Book 7, Ch19 L62).
- **Who pays for the rewards**: societies, associations and governments give out hefty rewards; in return the Adventure Society "assigns high contribution point values to the contracts that provide that protection" (Book 7, Ch12 L17).
- **What guilds want**: the big guilds monopolise the top of the board, but want "access to civic services" and organisational benefits from the Adventure Society, Magic Society and Alchemy Association, not material rewards (Book 7, Ch12 L19). Material rewards are the lower-rung prizes, which small guilds and independents can reach (Book 7, Ch12 L19-21).
- **The numbers**: Rufus says that in the city (Rimaros) about three in ten adventurers are in a guild and one in ten in a high-end one, yet "guilds are given sixty percent of the quotas to fill" (Book 7, Ch12 L23). Guilds have the highest quotas to beat before they reach the boards (Book 7, Ch12 L21).
- **Guild independence**: the guilds manage their own affairs, largely apart from the Adventure Society's structure. The Society handles their large quotas through liaison officers, "a test for the guilds to prove they deserved the privileges they enjoyed" (Book 7, Ch38 L7).
- **Not shielded**: the royal family does not shield the Sapphire Crown from the quotas (Book 7, Ch21 L15).
- **Rules**: normal rewards are suspended in a surge and handed out weekly on the leaderboard; some contracts do not count towards the quota or leaderboard (Book 7, Ch19 L73).
- **Escalation**: after losses minimum action quotas are raised and safety standards lowered (Book 7, Ch66 L29).

### 3. Not in the text

The quota size per rank and how guild quotas are shared among members are not stated.

---

## The Sapphire Crown Guild

### 1. Summary

The Sapphire Crown is the royal guild of Rimaros: a guild that works directly for the royal family, whose members rotate through the royal guard. It carries real power, and is held to the same surge quotas as any other guild.

### 2. Facts

- **Purpose**: in many places the role of guard had become ceremonial; "in Rimaros, the solution came in the form of a guild" (Book 7, Ch21 L11).
- **Royal support**: it is one of the most powerful guilds in Rimaros because of the support of the royal family, many of whom are among its most capable members; in return all non-royal members "were required to periodically serve within the royal guard" (Book 7, Ch21 L11).
- **Membership**: stringent, mostly from guild families; loyalty is paramount. All members are examined quarterly, rising to monthly or weekly while serving in the guard (Book 7, Ch21 L12).
- **Quotas**: the royal family does not shield it from the guilds' activity quotas. Trenchant Moore, a human gold-ranker in a guild of celestines, carries a good portion (Book 7, Ch21 L13, L15).
- **Liberty**: even its gold-rankers have "a rich amount of liberty", an exception to the usual monitoring of gold-rankers (Book 8, Ch17 L19).
- **Standing**: Liara is "a figure of accomplishment and respect in the Sapphire Crown", where power, not legacy, is the ultimate authority (Book 7, Ch21 L32).
- **Operations**: it joins Amouz family members in sweeping the Builder cult's hideouts (Book 9, Ch5 L3), watches over a familiar ritual (Book 9, Ch7 L4), and uses the duelling arena as a training hall (Book 9, Ch17 L8).
- **Royal guards**: a speaker recalls being one of the iron-rankers from the guild assigned as royal guards, with no camaraderie (Book 9, Ch40 L43); a character is asked whether she has considered switching to it (Book 9, Ch18 L98).

### 3. Not in the text

Its size and the exact rank of its leadership are not stated.

---

## Team Shining Scabbard and the Mirror Kingdom's Backing

### 1. Summary

Team Shining Scabbard is the adventuring team of Prince Valdis of the Mirror Kingdom, built around the healer Sigrid Freyn. The text calls it a royal-backed team, not a guild; the plan's "backers" are the Mirror King's family and the kingdom's resources. This file records what is stated.

### 2. Facts

- **Origin**: Jason says Valdis, "Prince of the Mirror Kingdom", is "the reason their team exists" (Book 3, Ch3 L78). Humphrey says its members "were being prepared for Valdis's team before any of them ever received an essence" (Book 3, Ch3 L80).
- **Structure**: "their team is built around their healer, Sigrid. She facilitates and directs strategy" (Book 3, Ch3 L76); the strategic core is Sigrid and the political core is Valdis (Book 3, Ch3 L78).
- **The match**: in the mirage chamber fight Jason's team defeats it by going for Sigrid while feinting at Valdis (Book 3, Ch3 L81; Book 3, Ch4 L43).

### 3. Not in the text

The text names no guild patrons or corporate backers for Team Shining Scabbard beyond the Mirror Kingdom's royal family.

---

## House Amouz

### 1. Summary

House Amouz is a Rimaros noble house that controls or has a share in more than half of the mining operations in the Sea of Storms. Its heir is captured by the Builder cult in Book 8, and the family sends its people in numbers to the cleanup in Book 9.

### 2. Facts

- **Business**: House Amouz "controls or has an interest in more than half of the mining operations in the Sea of Storms, including the bulk of high-rank mineral acquisition" (Book 8, Ch18 L85).
- **Ties to the royals**: Liara's husband married out of House Amouz into the royal family, but remains a high-level manager in the family's business interests (Book 7, Ch17 L22).
- **Heir**: Gibson Amouz is "son of Lord Cassin Amouz and heir to the seat of House Amouz" (Book 8, Ch18 L76). He is captured by the cult, which wants the family's secrets (Book 8, Ch18 L89; Book 8, Ch20 L4).
- **Lord Cassin**: a gold-ranker with a goatee, who confronts Liara over his son's capture (Book 8, Ch20 L14-19). Liara suspects the son was targeted because of what he would be told on becoming gold rank and ready to assume his seat (Book 8, Ch20 L53).
- **Volunteers**: the Amouz family volunteers in numbers that surprise even Liara in the sweep of the hideouts (Book 9, Ch5 L3).

### 3. Not in the text

The family's title and its seat are not stated in the lines read.

---

## House Davone

### 1. Summary

### 2. Facts

- **Mercer ties**: "the Davone and Kettering families, with their close ties to the Mercers" sent their boys to the Mercer compound, where Neil spent time as a boy (Book 2, Ch27 L41-42).

- **Teaming**: the Davone and Mercer families "colluded to team him with Thadwick Mercer at a young age" (Book 9, Ch21 L5).

### 3. Not in the text

The family's title and size are not stated in the lines read.

## House Geller

### 1. Summary

The Gellers are an adventuring dynasty with branches across the world and a seat at an estate in the Greenstone delta. They hold only a small provincial title, train their young hard in Greenstone, and are the richest family in the city.

### 2. Facts

- **The estate**: the Geller family estate sits behind a thick high wall, so vast that monsters are as likely to be inside as out; household guards are iron-rank essence users (Book 1, Ch37 L3-4). The seat is "a sprawling estate in the delta" (Book 1, Ch97 L50), landscaped on secure ground with artful waterways and decades of defences (Book 2, Ch17 L23-24).
- **Standing**: "The Geller family stood at the peak of Greenstone society, and their local power was just a fragment of their world-spanning influence" (Book 1, Ch56 L6). The family "sprawled across continents" (Book 1, Ch66 L4), and branch families exist: one patriarch leads a branch family (Book 2, Ch10 L28).
- **Training**: Geller family tradition is to supply scions with enough awakening stones to get started, while the rest must be earned (Book 1, Ch64 L80). The family trains its young at "the south end of nowhere" so they can make their own mistakes (Book 1, Ch49 L60), with facilities including a mirage chamber and arena (Book 1, Ch66 L18, L27).
- **Why Greenstone**: other Greenstone families envy the influence the Gellers hold elsewhere and want outside connections (Book 2, Ch32 L39). Some families "simply wanted the next adventure", as the Gellers do (Book 2, Ch39 L7).
- **The academy annex**: the Remore Academy's Greenstone annex is a joint venture with the Gellers, with an internal Geller faction opposed to the agreement (Book 1, Ch88 L3, L9).

- **No title but one**: the Gellers hold only the title linked to their small provincial city of origin, refusing all others (Book 6, Ch47 L605; Book 7, Ch57 L72). They are "technically aristocracy in some inconsequential city-state somewhere" and their prestige is as an adventuring dynasty (Book 8, Ch28 L45).
- **Everywhere**: "There are Gellers everywhere, Mr Asano. You'd have to ask the Church of Fertility about that" (Book 7, Ch45 L106). Branches have a garden tower in Vitesse (Book 7, Ch24 L3-4) and a fortified estate outside Cyrion (Book 7, Ch43 L62).
- **The seat in Greenstone**: the family kept Greenstone as its seat even as its power spread, for the experience it gives the young (Book 7, Ch66 L56).
- **Training methodology**: "you don't understand just how deep Geller family training goes" (Book 9, Ch21 L18), and people scramble to be on a team with a Geller who went through the Greenstone programme (Book 9, Ch21 L20).

### 3. Not in the text

The family's size and branches are not counted; its provincial city of origin is not named in the lines read.

---

## House Kettering

### 1. Summary

The Ketterings are a Greenstone family tied to the Mercers who send their sons to the Mercer compound. They have people in Old City and supply adventurers, including Dustin and Hudson Kettering.

### 2. Facts

- **Mercer ties**: the Davone and Kettering families have "close ties to the Mercers" and send their boys to Thadwick (Book 2, Ch27 L41).
- **Adventurers**: Hudson Kettering is on Beth Cavendish's team (Book 2, Ch15 L44, L54). Dustin Kettering, a local, takes Jonah's defender role in Rick's team (Book 2, Ch29 L8).
- **Old City**: "The Ketterings have people in Old City"; Danielle coordinates with the Kettering family to mobilise them (Book 2, Ch59 L75-77).

### 3. Not in the text

The family's title, size and standing are not stated. The relation between Hudson and Dustin is not stated in the lines read.

---

## House Mercer

### 1. Summary

The Mercers are a very powerful Greenstone family, tied to the Duke through marriage, who run spirit coin farms and shape the city's other families through their patronage. Thadwick Mercer is their heir; Cassandra is his sister.

### 2. Facts

- **Power**: "Thadwick's Mercer family was very powerful in Greenstone", and more so when "the Duke of Greenstone's brother, Thadwick's father, married into it". The family ranks above many others, especially those without aristocratic title (Book 1, Ch77 L47).
- **Residence**: their main residence is a manor on the Island, five towers set in a ring connected by glass walkways, "a feat of magical engineering" (Book 1, Ch97 L50).
- **Numbers**: no family can match the Gellers' numbers, but the Mercers "still made an impressive showing" (Book 1, Ch98 L20).
- **Farms**: Humphrey says most of the spirit coin farms are operated by his family or the Mercers, under Magic Society regulation (Book 1, Ch80 L28). The Mercers do not offer part ownership as the Gellers do (Book 1, Ch80 L77).
- **Household**: the household staff are "raked over the coals" when Thadwick is provoked (Book 1, Ch82 L101).
- **Patronage**: "Thadwick had needed friends his own age and the Davone and Kettering families, with their close ties to the Mercers, had frequently sent their own boys over" (Book 2, Ch27 L41). Neil Davone grew up at the Mercer compound (Book 2, Ch27 L41-43).

### 3. Not in the text

The Mercers' title, if any, is not stated in the lines read.

---

## House Remore and the Remore Academy

### 1. Summary

The Remores are a family of educators and adventurers in Vitesse who hold no title but run the Remore Academy, one of the most respected adventurer schools in the world. Roland Remore, a diamond-ranker, is arch-chancellor; Rufus is the heir.

### 2. Facts

- **The Academy**: the family "operates the Remore Academy, which is a big deal everywhere, not just Vitesse" (Book 1, Ch30 L58). It runs a Greenstone training annex built with the Gellers (Book 1, Ch88 L3; Book 3, Ch26 L9).
- **Roland**: "Although Roland Remore was rarely spotted on the Remore Academy campus, the presence of the diamond-rank arch-chancellor was always felt" (Book 3, Ch69 L587).
- **Rufus as heir**: Rufus "was the prince of Remore Academy, the talented heir-apparent to the power and prestige of his family legacy" (Book 3, Ch69 L589).
- **Graduates**: an academy graduate should have better control of her aura, so students have standing among adventurers (Book 2, Ch38 L36).

- **No title**: the Remore family has no aristocratic title; the family's prestige comes from "generations of accomplishment", not their name (Book 6, Ch47 L605; Book 7, Ch57 L72). Like other prestige families they are offered titles and refuse (Book 7, Ch57 L72; Book 9, Ch14 L7).
- **Scholarships**: Rufus's grandfather set up a scouting programme for people with potential, with scholarships and dormitories (Book 6, Ch47 L129).
- **Students**: scions of international mercantile guilds, adventuring families, aristocrats and royalty, taught by methodology studied from across the globe (Book 9, Ch20 L26-27). The family nurtured Rufus's talent with carefully engineered opportunities (Book 9, Ch20 L57).

### 3. Not in the text

The family's size is not counted; its origin and seat besides Vitesse are not stated in the lines read.

---

## House Rimaros (the Royal House of Rimaros)

### 1. Summary

The royal family of Rimaros rules the sky-island city and its kingdom under a Storm King. It has its own guild (the Sapphire Crown), marries into the engineering and mining houses for stability, and carries sapphire hair and eyes.

### 2. Facts

- **The guild**: the royal family supports the Sapphire Crown, and many of its own are among its most capable members (Book 7, Ch21 L11). See Sapphire_Crown.md.
- **Marriage politics**: Zara, a princess, is matched with a member of House Irios, the pre-eminent producer of defensive engineering. The union would have created political stability before the surge (Book 7, Ch18 L66-72). Liara's husband was born into House Amouz (Book 7, Ch17 L22; Book 9, Ch5 L3).
- **Family traits**: Zareen has "the iconic sapphire hair and eyes" (Book 8, Ch62 L36).
- **Liara**: a gold-rank member of the royal house, in charge of screening adventurers for Builder cultists (Book 7, Ch17 L3; see Liara_Rimaros.md).

### 3. Not in the text

The royal line of succession, the king's name and the size of the family are not stated in the lines read.

---

## Greenstone

### 1. Summary

Greenstone is a colony city at the mouth of a river delta in the low-magic far south of the Africa-like continent. It is the first city Jason knows on Pallimustus and the setting of most of Books 1-3. It was founded because its conditions suit low-end spirit coin farms: stable low magic, in a place that is also livable and close to the desert (Book 1, Ch80 L42). It is a place of green marble, walled harbours, crime lords with gentlemanly manners, and a Society full of ambitious, mid-ranked people who use monster cores rather than fight for their ranks.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Setting**: a vast river delta inside a western coastal region that is mostly lifeless desert; the delta is fertile ground between dune seas. Locals and adventurers wear loose, breathable clothes because of the heat.
- **The Old City**: "the original city of Greenstone, situated on the original harbour"; the Island was meant to be a massive breakwater (Book 1, Ch35 L15). It is built mostly from the same red and yellow stone as the desert, with a massive port along its whole shoreline, and "Old City is where the money is made, but the Island is where the money goes" (Book 1, Ch35 L19, L59; Book 1, Ch36 L98). The city authorities pay little attention to it so long as the elite's interests are secure, which makes its three biggest crime lords its de facto rulers (Book 1, Ch42 L22). Districts have names from old families, such as Cavendish, whose tavern the Townhouse is the largest building there (Book 1, Ch71 L3, L38).
- **The Island**: the affluent district built entirely of green marble (Book 1, Ch36 L68). Travel to it "was restricted without a valid reason for entry": trade and work permits admit a person to the trade lanes, and aristocrats, adventurers and residents come and go freely; a day's entry costs an iron-rank spirit coin (Book 1, Ch36 L71-72). Staying past sunset needs lodgings, which earn a temporary residence permit and a tracking stone (Book 1, Ch36 L89, L95; Book 1, Ch39 L34). Vehicles on the Island are magic-driven, not animal-drawn, and prices there are twice or three times Old City's (Book 1, Ch39 L32-33). The guild district in the north-west holds the Adventure Society campus (Book 1, Ch38 L3).
- **The Divine Square**: a huge crowded square on the Island, paved even in high-grade green stone, ringed by streets of temples that show "gods had hierarchies of their own"; booths sell religious paraphernalia, and even the flagstones are high-grade green stone (Book 1, Ch45 L4-5). The Temple of the Healer is one of the central temples on it (Book 1, Ch94 L3) and all the major temples front it (Book 2, Ch10 L92).
- **The Fortress**: a huge stone building in the grimy heart of Old City's warehouse district, "older even than the city walls", once the shelter for residents during a monster surge; now neutral ground where the Big Three divide responsibility and profit, and the best place in Old City to glimpse the Island elites (Book 1, Ch42 L21-23). Fights are held there in a steel cage, as Sophie's Nightingale bouts (Book 1, Ch50 L85-91).
- **Water and tunnels**: delta water runs through an elaborate tunnel network under the Old City and drains into an artificial strait between the Old City and the Island; Adventure Society contracts clear monsters such as stone-chewer rats from it. Transit uses loop terminals (Book 1, Ch45 L4); Jason's "magic trams or something?" (Book 1, Ch37 L81) is only his guess.
- **Surroundings**: green-marble quarry villages (Quarry Village Four, a lake village fed by a waterfall from an astral-space aperture), desert, mangrove and marsh. Boko, a desert academic city, lies to the north.

### 3. Society and rule

- **Colonial status**: the city began as a colony from the wider kingdom. The Adventure Society is a major force, but the isolation makes the local branch dependent on local powers; Rufus's remark to the village mayor is that "compromises must be made."
- **The Adventure Society branch** is directed by Elspeth Arella. Vincent Trenslow runs assessments and assigns contracts.
- **The Magic Society branch** is directed by Lucian Lamprey, a silver-ranker who watches Fortress fights from a viewing box (Book 1, Ch50 L103; Book 1, Ch52 L46). His deputy is Pochard Finn, whose son Cassowary serves as Lucian's dogsbody (Book 1, Ch49 L5; Book 1, Ch50 L104).
- **The great families**: the Gellers (an estate, with a mirage chamber), the Mercers (Thalia, Cassandra, Thadwick; the Mercer estate has a gate and guards), and the Remore family's academy annex.
- **Faiths**: temples in the Divine Square, including the Healer's. Purity's Anisa Lasalle begins the books in Rufus's party.

### 4. Magic level

Greenstone is a low-magic region where "iron-rank monsters are the norm, with a good smattering of bronze-rank" (Book 1, Ch19 L40; Book 1, Ch30 L43). This shapes the city: core use is nearly universal, because fighting for advancement is slow when monsters are few and weak; a bronze-rank mirage chamber can run only for short periods; there are only "forty or so silver rankers in the whole city", most semi-retired (Book 1, Ch61 L32), and the narration notes that Greenstone's other silver-rankers show more of their age than Emir, Danielle and Thalia (Book 1, Ch108 L35). Resting spirit coins and potions matter more than in higher-magic cities. The city exports green marble and lesser spirit coins.

### 5. History

- Founded as a colony and spirit coin farm. Almost nothing is known about the region's history before the original Greenstone Colony, as Clive observes (Book 1, Ch86 L25); the Gellers' ancestors came as the region was first being settled (Book 1, Ch37 L21).
- Book 1: Jason arrives, joins the Adventure Society, helps destroy a Blood Cult branch and fights alongside Rufus and Farrah.
- Books 2-3: Sophie's indenture, Emir's contest for the Scythe of the Reaper, Mercer family intrigue, the oasis tyrant, the Builder cult.
- Later: Greenstone remains a recurring home and reference point.

### 6. Notable people

Elspeth Arella (Adventure Society director), Vincent Trenslow (official), Lucian Lamprey (Magic Society director), Clarissa Ventress, Cole Silva, Adris Dorgan, Dink, Emir Bahadir and Constance, the Gellers (Danielle, Humphrey), the Mercers (Thalia, Cassandra, Thadwick; the Mercer estate has a gate and guards), Jory Tillman (alchemist), Clive Standish, Beth Cavendish (water healer), Hiram (aperture guard at Quarry Village Four), Arash (runic juice vendor), Jamar (bartender), the Berts (Bertinelli octuplets, clerks).

### 7. Customs and daily life

Aristocrats and the wealthy live on the Island; ordinary people in the Old City. Adventurers cluster in the Society campus and its taverns. Temples hold public services in the Divine Square. Fights at the Fortress are a major entertainment; recording crystals broadcast and preserve them. Heat makes loose dress universal. Local food and drink include runic juice from Arash's stall.

### 8. Dangers and opportunities

- **Dangers**: marsh hydras, tidal trolls, shabs and other delta monsters; desert bandits; astral-space expeditions; the underworld's sudden violence; Society politics.
- **Opportunities**: contracts from the Society, spirit coin farms, ancient complexes and ruins under the delta, trade in green marble.

### 9. Hooks

- A tour of the Fortress's neutral ground as a meeting place for the Big Three.
- A contract at a quarry village where an old aperture has started leaking monsters.
- An adventurer candidate's field assessment run by the Society.
- Magic Society politics around the Nightingale.
- (Level 1) Greenstone as the place Sophie's indenture ends, and the starting point for the Reaper contest.

### Open questions

- Exact founding date and the identity of the founders.
- Distance and travel times to Vitesse and Cyrion.

---

## Rimaros (the Storm Kingdom)

### 1. Summary

The Storm Kingdom is a nation of islands in the Sea of Storms, founded by Soramir Rimaros, a diamond-ranker who was its first king. Its principal city, Rimaros, spans three islands and is protected by storm accumulators. Its monarch, the Storm King, must be gold-rank (and, per Book 7, Ch5 L67, royal heirs raised on cores are disqualified); the successor is chosen by the previous king after a contest. Its royal guard is supplied by the Sapphire Crown guild, whose members are examined quarterly, and more often in the guard (Book 7, Ch21 L12). It is the first place Jason lands when he returns to Pallimustus in Book 7, just as a monster surge begins.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Sea of Storms**: the Caribbean analogue (Book 7, Ch3 L44-47, with Jason's map at L45). The city of Rimaros comprises three islands; the largest concentrations of the kingdom's people live on a central landmass made up of what would be Cuba, Haiti and the Dominican Republic.
- **The three public islands** are Livaros, Arnote and Provo, in an east-west line (Book 7, Ch7 L70).
- **Livaros**: "the primary destination for adventurers... the adventurer island", with the Adventure Society campus and most services for essence users (Book 7, Ch7 L78; Book 7, Ch10 L5), and the skyport. Havi Estos owns property there and hires people to poke at Jason's aura (Book 7, Ch43 L47; Book 7, Ch62 L3, L43). A Builder fortress-city destroyed above it in Book 7.
- **Arnote**: the westernmost island, home to many wealthier citizens, with a relaxed lifestyle that is part of Rimaros's cultural identity and a minor branch of the royal family (Book 7, Ch7 L70-72); it is the least populous and least defended (Book 7, Ch64 L21). Palisaros is a little town on it, where Dawn stays and where Team Biscuit settles and of Jason's pagoda, built when authority infuses his Cloud Flask.
- **Provo**: the easternmost and most populous island, the general trade hub and home to most of the non-magical citizenry (Book 7, Ch40 L4).
- **The Royal Sky Island**: the palace.
- **Jaitari**: "the largest and most populous city" of the Storm Kingdom, on the largest island of the Sea of Storms (Book 9, Ch3 L81-82).
- **Defences**: windmill-like storm accumulators stand offshore, both shielding islands and powering their infrastructure (as on the island bought by the Sky Link Company). House Irios designs defences; Book 7 shows Irios family members controlling Livaros's defences, which fire back at attackers, behind a dome barrier. Irios designs and maintains the defences of Rimaros itself, the royal palace lake and many fortress towns.

### 3. Society and rule

- **The Sapphire Crown**: the royal guild; non-royals serve in the royal guard and take quarterly exams. Commander of the royal guard: Trenchant Moore, introduced as "Commander Trenchant Moore of the royal guard" and called "the siege sword" (Book 7, Ch52 L73; Book 8, Ch44 L50).
- **House Irios**: "the pre-eminent producer of defensive engineering" whose defences protect Rimaros and many fortress towns (Book 7, Ch18 L70); the family is "known as the shield of" the kingdom (Book 7, Ch37 L64). The text of its founder being Soramir's "brother" is not checked here.
- **The Magic Society** holds the second-largest sky island in Rimaros, after the palace (Book 7, Ch5 L67).
- **The Adventure Society** in Rimaros registers Jason and Farrah and questions the irregularities in his record.
- **Politics**: Jason's relationship with the throne is called the Storm Kingdom's most important political relationship by Soramir and Zila.

### 4. Magic level

High. Far above Greenstone's mid-iron. The Sea of Storms is saturated with storm and water magic; cyclones form as a matter of course, hence the accumulators. The Book 7 surge brings monsters from the deep.

### 5. History

- Founded by Soramir Rimaros.
- Book 7: Jason and Farrah arrive in a tropical cyclone as the surge begins; Zara's public claim that Jason proposed to her to escape an arranged match; the royal family "takes his measure"; bone feasters at Arcazitlan; Builder invasion platforms; the One Day War.
- Books 8-9: Jason's pagoda on Arnote; the Order of Redeeming Light and its mountain stronghold; the Storm Kingdom's allies and ambassadors.

### 6. Notable people

### 7. Customs and daily life

Storm-watching, airships and sea-skimmers are everyday things; the islands rely on accumulators and magical defences. The Sapphire Crown's exams and royal ceremonies set the social calendar.

### 8. Dangers and opportunities

Cyclones, sea monsters (a kraken and a smoke dragon are among those named in the books), the surge's weekly monster quotas, Builder invasion platforms during the war, and the politics of an ambitious, powerful royal house.

### 9. Hooks

- A contract on Livaros that leads into the underworld of Havi Estos.
- An Irios engineer wants a favour.
- A cyclone that accumulators cannot manage.
- A royal contest to name a successor.

### Later developments (spoilers)

### Open questions

- Exact structure and number of islands.
- How the "Caribbean" comparison relates to the placement of Estercost and Yaresh.

## Vitesse

### 1. Summary

Vitesse, in the kingdom of Estercost, is the City of Flowers, "located in what Jason Asano would know as the French Riviera" (Book 6, Ch47 L603; Book 1, Ch30 L48). Its skyline is made of the **garden towers**: huge towers with flowering vines spilling down the exterior, "most had every third or fourth floor dedicated to gardens using water, light and plant magic", which are residences for the wealthy elite and headquarters of key organisations (Book 6, Ch47 L603; Book 7, Ch24 L3). It is a high-magic, wealthy city (the social programmes and labour laws described below are not confirmed in the lines read). It is the home of Remore Academy and of Burning Violet, and the place Rufus, Gary and Farrah come from.

### 2. Location and layout

- Estercost's southern coast, on the equivalent of the French Riviera.
- Garden towers, most with every third or fourth floor given over to gardens (Book 6, Ch47 L603). The Adventure Society branch is in one of them, with a meeting room opening onto a balcony (Book 7, Ch43 L18); the Adventure Society and Magic Society each hold an entire building, and the royal family keeps a tower as a palace (Book 6, Ch47 L604).
- Trees line every street, heavy with blossom.

### 3. Society and rule

- Governed within the kingdom of Estercost. (Rakesh is also called the home of a continental council, Book 6, Ch47 L623; the two may be councils for two continents, see `Minor_Places/Rakesh.md` and Open_Questions 22.) The books disagree on where the Adventure Society's continental council sits: Book 6 says "the continental council for the Adventure Society sat in Vitesse, rather than the capital city, Cyrion" (Book 6, Ch47 L604), while Book 7 calls Cyrion "home to the Adventure Society's continental council" (Book 7, Ch43 L58).
- Strong labour protections and social programmes mean an unusually large comfortable middle class.

### 4. Magic level

### 5. History

Little is given directly. Vitesse is the origin of the Remore family's influence in the Adventure Society, and the base from which the Society mounts expeditions in northern Estercost. In Book 6 the Vitesse branch sends an expedition to attack a Purity church stronghold in northern Estercost; it is described as "more than they bargained for" and a "mess", with Jason involved.

### 6. Notable people

### 7. Customs and daily life

Flowers pervade daily life. Dwellings and shops are built around vertical gardens. Academy students are a visible population; adventurers who wear Burning Violet's violet-flame flower pin are respected.

### 8. Dangers and opportunities

Political rather than monstrous: the factional struggle over Remore control of Burning Violet, rivalries between Academy graduates, and Purity and Builder-cult activity in the north. Opportunities include the Academy's teaching, the guild's contracts, and access to better training facilities.

### 9. Hooks

- A newcomer applies to Burning Violet and learns the factions behind the interview.
- A Remore Academy graduation and the contest over who gets sponsorship.
- A garden tower's flower magic goes out of control.

### Later developments (spoilers)

### Open questions

- Whether the "French Riviera" comparison implies the neighbouring geography.
- Whether Roland is Rufus's maternal or paternal grandfather.

## Yaresh

### 1. Summary

Yaresh is a rainforest city with "only a few tens of thousands" of people, spread over "the footprint of a much larger centre" because its design deeply accommodates the natural environment (Book 9, Ch42 L26). Its walls are "a line of massive trees with walls of glossy black stone filling the gaps", with tunnels through the trunks (Book 9, Ch43 L33). It is the centre of the Battle of Yaresh in Book 9 and the gateway to the natural array beneath it.

### 2. Location and layout

- Rainforest setting; the outer walls are a line of enormous trees joined by black stone, with tunnels passing through the trunks to link wall-top sections.
- Black land skimmers queue at the gates for inspection (Book 9, Ch43 L33). Outside the central area most buildings have living magic trees as a core, shaped and supplemented with brickwork; the heart of the city has polished metal and glass towers (Book 9, Ch79 L5; Book 9, Ch81 L20).
- Towers stand at the heart of the city; the battle at the centre was "a dozen kilometres away" from the outskirts and the titanic figures could be seen looming over the towers (Book 9, Ch80 L3).
- The Yaresh mirage-chamber arena hosts team exhibitions; the Duke of Yaresh watches from a ballroom-sized VIP box.

### 3. Society and rule

- Two diamond-rank adventurers live here: **Allayeth** and **Charist**, both elves.
- Hana Shavar is "the High Priestess of the Church of the Healer for the city of Yaresh" (Book 9, Ch74 L43, L114); she runs relief for refugees, and after the messenger attack much of Yaresh's population is homeless and housed at Jason's cloud palace (Book 9, Ch103 L84).
- Adventure Society presence is strong; Rufus stays in Yaresh during part of Book 9.

### 4. Magic level

High, shaped by the natural array's elemental magic. The array imbues elemental magic and makes messengers mutate with devolving intelligence; its changes "cause intelligence to rapidly and precipitously devolve" and cost the messengers most of their initial silver-rank force (Book 9, Ch104 L84).

### 5. History

- Before the series: the natural array and the Smoulder city of Cardinas lie beneath Yaresh.
- Book 8: Jason's team learns of Yaresh while dealing with Builder and messenger activity.
- Book 9: messengers besiege Yaresh; a naga genesis egg (an apocalypse beast) is triggered when Argrave Mericulato, an Adventure Society functionary whose aura mask hides his seething anger (Book 9, Ch77 L13), tosses an egg-like sphere at a guard (confirmed); the Battle of Yaresh ends the siege with the help of Jason's allies; Marek explains the natural array.

### 6. Notable people

Allayeth and Charist (diamond elves), the Duke of Yaresh, Marek Nior Vargas (defecting messenger), Tera Jun Casta and Kaal (messengers), Argrave Mericulato, Prince Valdis and Sigrid Freyn (visiting Mirror Kingdom team). Rufus is with Jason's group in Book 9: "Mostly doesn't," Rufus qualified (Book 9, Ch42 L34).

### 7. Customs and daily life

Residents live among the trees; buildings and wards are woven into the forest. The city is proud of its adventurers and of its mirage arena, whose exhibitions are commentated and sponsored.

### 8. Dangers and opportunities

Elemental monsters from the array, messenger incursions, and Builder-cult activity below. Opportunities: the arena, the diamond-ranked mentors, and the array itself, which attracts researchers.

### 9. Hooks

- An arena exhibition with a visiting team.
- A messenger defector asks for sanctuary.
- Digging teams of enslaved essence users need rescue.
- A researcher wants a safe passage down to Cardinas.

### Later developments (spoilers)

The Smoulder city is evacuated; its brightheart survivors, led by Lorenn, are sheltered in Jason's soul realm and later settle Rexion. The Battle of Yaresh makes the city a symbol of resistance to the messengers.

### Open questions

- The full extent of the array under the city.

---

## The City of Fallen Echoes (Book 2)

### 1. Summary

An ancient, circular, overgrown city in an astral space with a sea around it, entered through the drowned fortress at Sky Scar Lake. It is the site of Emir Bahadir's contest for the Scythe of the Reaper, a trial gauntlet that tests wisdom, courage, capability, intellect and resolve.

### 2. Layout

- Twelve gate towers; each person who enters arrives at a random tower.
- A circular city with streets and ruins, overgrown by the astral space.
- Monsters: siege golems, margolls, a sloth demon, grizzards, vorger swarms, flesh abominations and a blood root vine. A sloth demon is a gold-rank monster with a soporific power that cripples speed (Book 2, Ch45 L7); the space's overall density is not stated in the passages I checked.

### 3. The trials

Shade administers five trials in order, with a hidden sixth at the end (Book 2, Ch44 L39-41):
1. **The first trial** (Book 2, Ch44 L47): a choice between wisdom and courage that "cannot be failed"; it decides which of the two virtues is tested at the third trial. Choosing wisdom gives a Medallion of Escape (silver-rank, uncommon; projects the aura to evacuate, works only in the city) and a Lesser Miracle Potion (Book 2, Ch44 L119-128).
2. **The second trial, capability**: the City of Fallen Echoes itself, the longest of the five; the objective is to reach the centre (Book 2, Ch44 L41-45; completed Ch57 L29). Reward: a random magic item.
3. **The third trial** (Book 2, Ch57 L68): those who chose courage first now face the test of wisdom; those who chose wisdom face courage, each alone behind a chosen door, where each meets a nightmare hag (diamond-rank astral entities that cannot harm directly; Jason faces an evil future self, Humphrey his family, notes). Failing wisdom means removal; failing courage means death.
4. **The fourth trial, intellect** (Book 2, Ch58 L9-24): a time-limited test of calm under pressure; failure means death. Jason receives a Summoner's Die: Form (an iron-rank growth item of legendary rarity) after the constellation puzzle.
5. **The fifth trial, resolve** (Book 2, Ch58 L70-74): the invigilator asks Jason to execute the Order's captive enemies; he refuses ("Not a chance") and the reward is an Immortal Crest (Book 2, Ch58 L100-102). Reward: a random magic item.
6. **The hidden trial, insight** (Book 2, Ch60 L54-56): the "ability to see beyond appearances"; reward unknown at the outset.

Participants may withdraw before each later trial through a gate (Book 2, Ch44 L57-59; Ch57 L70).

Nineteen adventurers pass all the trials. About four hundred and nineteen time-displaced priests and holy warriors are freed.

### 4. Dangers and hooks

Time displacement, hags that read the mind, and the contest's politics. Hooks: a freed priest with centuries-old knowledge; a trial reward whose use is unclear.

### Open questions

- The rewards of the trials beyond those listed (check Book 2 Ch57-60 per team).

---

## The Desert Astral Space (Book 1)

### 1. Summary

A water-and-islands astral space reached from the desert by a submerged aperture. A large Greenstone expedition goes in at the climax of Book 1 to find what is disrupting it. They are ambushed by a construct army.

### 2. Layout

- Entered through a submerged aperture; Danielle Geller surfaces in "a turquoise lagoon, under a clear cerulean sky", mostly bounded by rocky rises with scrubby trees (Book 1, Ch100 L8).
- Islands, searched one by one by the expedition. On the third island the party finds "a five-sided column, about as tall as a person and covered in magical engravings" (Book 1, Ch107 L3); several are later packed away (Book 1, Ch113 L45). The Magic Society's researchers recognise them as part of the disruption.
- The expedition is supported from the desert: Emir's cloud palace, and his portaler Hester, who can open portals only to places she has previously visited (as is normal for long-range teleports). Boko is where the Adventure Society returns the freed Ustei slaves and women and children who wanted out of the tribe (Book 1, Ch109 L8).

### 3. The ambush

A construct army of "wooden puppets, awkward but numerous", stone golems two or three times human size, clockwork creatures such as a giant steel-cog tiger, and a huge steel spider carrying a robed figure (Book 1, Ch108 L90, L97; Book 1, Ch107 L76), led by sandy-robed bronze and silver masters and silver-rank abominations (metal grafted into flesh, extra metal arms), attacks the expedition. Farrah Hurin is killed; Henry Geller dies; Jonah and Thadwick and three others are found injured; Rufus loses an arm. Two captives take their own lives by exploding crystals.

### 4. Aftermath

The incident ties the Builder cult to the region and sets up Book 2.

### 5. Hooks

- Return to the islands to retrieve the remaining columns.
- A survivor of the ambush wants answers.

### Open questions

- Whether all the columns were recovered.

---

## The Reaper-Trials Boarding Vessel (Book 3)

### 1. Summary

An artificial astral space that turns out to be a giant Builder boarding vessel. The Order of the Reaper repurposed its delivery system into a portal system powered by Jason-like power. The team enters it around the middle of Book 3.

### 2. Layout

- Twelve portal towers about eight kilometres apart, each holding a dormant world engineer golem, and a central tower. Jason calls them "a dozen diamond-ranked super-golems" that must not be allowed to wake.
- A giant stone ring gate about a hundred metres across floats horizontally above the crater and rises toward a sky anomaly. Shade identifies it as the anchor point of a physical bridge across the astral between this space and Jason's world.

### 3. Events

Clive plans to carry an inversion device up the central tower to burn out the world engineers and power a portal home. The cult's leader Zato says their people planted a beacon inside the astral space, which let them target it and begin establishing an astral bridge; once the portal opens, everyone goes through (confirmed). The Builder's forces include converted clergy with clockwork cores, spyder drones and an energy ghoul.

### 4. Dangers and hooks

World engineers waking, cultists, and the vessel's drift. Hooks: an expedition to salvage the portal system; a cult remnant that escaped.

### Open questions

- How the vessel's delivery system compares to the Builder's door.

---

## Jason's Spirit Vault and Astral Kingdom

### 1. Summary

Jason's personal space grows over the series: a dimensional inventory that only he can physically enter (Book 4, Ch2 L70: "others cannot be invited or forcibly intrude") becomes the Spirit Vault, which those who trust him completely can enter (Book 4, Ch57 L59), then a spirit domain, a soul realm that travels along the world link, and finally an astral kingdom once he possesses an astral throne and gate. Places inside it are described below.

### 2. Evolution

### 3. The vault

Seen first as a floating obsidian gazebo of black-and-white marbled obsidian, circular with four archways and open sides, floating in a rain-filled void (Book 4, Ch30 L49). The garden form is confirmed in the text as a largely hanging garden of flower-wreathed bamboo trellises over flagstone paths, which does not take more space as it grows (a function of rank). The same passage describes a blood section of red flowers and blood-red flagstone like walking through an artery, a sin section of black, red, white and gold beds, a dark section in an underground cave with luminescent fungus and silver grass, and a doom section of trees and bushes, with a creek that runs through all of them to a waterfall and an underground fairy grotto (Book 4, Ch57 L3-10). In Book 8 Clive tours the same garden estate, where the essence sections intermingle, a subterranean network of fungus-lit tunnels runs under it, the sin garden is a manicured plot of black and white flowers and the doom areas are wild jungle (Book 8, Ch2 L57-63). Jason's cloud house, in Book 8, becomes a pagoda of smoky crystal flecked with gold, silver and blue light, the same design as the pagodas at the heart of his Earth domains; in the soul space the pagoda is larger than those and stands at the edge of a large public square. Inside, others cannot use abilities; Jason and his familiars can affect them. Emi and Farrah are the first to enter.

### 4. The domains

- **Slovakia (Nitra)**: the territory renamed the Arrival Pagoda, whose portals connect to places in the astral space and which any non-hostile may use by default (Book 6, Ch5 L41; Book 6, Ch19 L62). The arrival building is rebuilt as an octagonal pagoda (Book 6, Ch5 L53); the portal area holds ten portal arches in dark crystal (Book 6, Ch19 L21); the central city, about eighteen kilometres across, has a semi-industrial, steampunk feel, with a ring of about a hundred and sixty kilometres around it and roamable land about seven hundred kilometres in each direction (Book 6, Ch23 L48, L56). Hostile intruders suffer ability-ignoring afflictions; those who venerate Jason have afflictions converted to Integrity.
- **France (Saint-Étienne)**: a cloud city. See the Earth files.

### 5. The astral kingdom

### 6. The soul realm tour (Book 9-11)

### 8. Dangers and hooks

Jason's moods shape the landscape; intrusions from the great astral beings; the Flesh Pit. Hooks: a guest who trusts him enough to enter the vault; a refugee settlement dispute; a visit to the Colin greenhouse.

### Open questions

## Transformation Zones and Genesis Space

### 1. Summary

### 2. Mechanics of the world

- **The seal**: "forty-three hours after the sphere moved into place, it vanished" at Kostroma, the first zone (Book 5, Ch44 L48).
- **Sealing**: inside a zone Jason's essence abilities are sealed (Book 6, Ch3 L5) while his racial powers remain (Book 6, Ch1 L83); a normal transformation stays sealed about two days (Book 6, Ch3 L39); the first zone message is Book 6, Ch2 L15. That aura and perception are sealed is not found.
- **Anomalies**: shape-shifting monsters (silver or gold) born from the strongest presence and memories in the zone.
- **Cores**: defeated anomalies yield Stable Genesis Cores (used to expand a spirit domain), Unstable Genesis Cores (dangerous; they transformed Guo and Tran), orbs that unseal an essence ability, and the Genesis Reclamation Core.
- **Stabilisation**: claiming territories shows a "severity reduction" like a loading bar; at one hundred percent the zone becomes a permanent astral space, the dome vanishes, and a reality core remains. If the borders are not resolved the zone collapses inward.

### 3. Known zones

- **Nitra (Slovakia)**: Book 5 end to Book 6; becomes Jason's first Earth domain. Territories include Arrival Pagoda, Soul Haven and Tranquil Shadow Woods.
- **Saint-Étienne (France)**: Book 6; a second zone triggered inside the old Dimensional Fortress.
- **Kostroma (Russia)**: the first transformation event; the whole city is sealed in a sphere and Jason, Farrah and Dawn enter after it drops.

### 4. Dangers and hooks

Zones shape their enemies from the minds of those inside. Hooks: a zone opens under a settled city; a cult exploits the Genesis Reclamation Core; refugees from a collapsed territory.

### Open questions

- How the "about nine months" Cardinas duration reconciles with the zone's "five to six month" collapse.
- Whether territory minimum size differs between Earth and Pallimustus zones.

---

## Casselton and Asano Village

### 1. Summary

Casselton Beach is Jason's hometown, a tourist beach town on the Mid-North Coast of New South Wales where he was born (Book 4, Ch1 L39). It is where he wakes in Book 4 after dying on Pallimustus, and it is the model for the head-shaped fortress he later builds in a transformation zone. Asano Village is the family compound on clifftop land nearby, built by the family after the magic arrives.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Casselton region**: the old West Regional Hospital (abandoned; Jason wakes naked in its fourth-floor maternity theatre, looking out over the semi-industrial part of West Casselton), the boardwalk, the Surf Club and the marina matter across Books 4-6.
- **Casselton Beach**: the town of Jason's childhood, with a long, quiet beach.
- **Asano Village**: eco-village buildings on clifftop land, a medical centre, a pub and spa, and a secret tunnel system (Book 5, Ch5 L83-89). Clifftop excavations served as a front for a network of tunnels linking the compound's nodes and carrying the magical infrastructure, with a two-way tramway; a separate, longer passage runs from the main residence out into the ocean and becomes a glass tunnel along the seafloor, with sea creatures drawn by subtle magic. Only Ken, Hiro, Emi and Farrah knew of the tunnels; the Network and the rest of the family did not. The long passage ends at Jason's cloud house, which sits at the bottom of the sea about two kilometres out, reached by a hidden tram (Book 5, Ch5 L106-113).
- **Family home**: the house where Jason's mother Cheryl and father Ken live, with a rebuilt gazebo and fence that Jason notices have changed.

### 3. Society and rule

An ordinary Australian coastal community until magic becomes public. The Asano family, led by Yumi, treats the village as the base of a clan. The Network and, later, the Australian government take an interest in it.

### 4. Magic level

Low. Earth has very little magic in Book 4: Jason uses spirit coins to normalise his recovery (Book 4, Ch3 L87). After the Grid fails, ambient magic rises (see `../../03_Magic_and_Adventuring/Earth_Magic.md`).

### 5. History

- Book 4: Jason wakes in the hospital; contacts uncle Hiro through the Stone Wall bar in Sydney's Kings Cross (Book 4, Ch4 L8); reveals magic to his niece Emi, then to the family.
- Books 5-6: the family evacuates to Asano Village when the Grid is sabotaged.

### 6. Notable people

Jason's parents Ken and Cheryl, siblings Kaito and Erika, Kaito's wife Amy, Erika's husband Ian, niece Emi, uncle Hiro, matriarch Yumi, administrator Ketevan Arziani, and Jason's school friends including Greg and Michael Aram.

### 7. Customs and daily life

Coastal Australian life: beach, surf club, pub. After the magic arrives the village adds training facilities and a medical centre, and secrecy matters less.

### 8. Dangers and opportunities

Incursions from nearby sites, vampire and EOA attacks, and legal disputes over land. Opportunities: the village's medical centre, training with Jason's methods, a safe place to settle.

### 9. Hooks

- A local incursion near the beach town.
- A land dispute between rival Asano clans.
- A returning outworlder finds home changed.

### Later developments (spoilers)

Kaito and Greg die in Book 5 at the hands of the American gold-ranker Gerling; Amy is mayor in the later books, preparing in Book 5 to commandeer accommodation across the tourist towns of Casselton Beach, Castle Reach and Casselton North. The Australian land seizure, Noriko's hosting of the pirate captain Jota, and the 2038 return form the Earth endgame.

### Open questions

- Exact location and layout of the compound.

---

## Incursion Sites (Dorrigo, Makassar, Broken Hill and others)

### 1. Summary

An incursion is a proto-astral space that opens on Earth and spills monsters into the surrounding area. Spaces are rated by category and last "forty-three hours, as a baseline" (Book 4, Ch37 L70). This file records the sites where the story takes place.

### 2. Dorrigo and South Australia (Book 4)

- **Dorrigo National Park** (Book 4, Ch39 L28):  the team meets silver-rank jungle trolls (Book 4, Ch41 L18, L32 confirms roaming jungle trolls, whose regeneration is shut down by fire; Nigel's team burns the bodies with bronze-rank everburn oil). Jason joins the Network team, kills anomalous dimensional entities (ADEs) and shows them adventurer tactics.
- **South Australia**: Jason and Farrah are flown from Sydney to join the Adelaide branch's tactical team (Book 4, Ch60 L18). Inside, flat scrubland holds a herd of grotesque animals: "three gigantoads, two yowies, and a yara-ma-yha-who", with barb-tailed mantas overhead (Book 4, Ch60 L59-65); the team's projection is that the space "might last as much as sixty hours" (Book 4, Ch62 L23).

### 3. Makassar (Book 5)

- **Setting**: Makassar, South Sulawesi, a city of one and a half million people (Book 5, Ch25 L82). A category-three proto-space starts dumping monsters into it; it later escalates to category four with a gold-rank anchor.
- **Dino-men**: silver-rank intelligent lizard-men lead dinosaur monsters; their king says "I, like you all, am less than two days old" (Book 5, Ch26 L17).
- **Outcome**: Jason enters, dies, and takes the form of a star phoenix (Book 5, Ch28 L86). The Makassar death toll "officially crossed a hundred and fifty thousand" with bodies in piles (Book 5, Ch30 L71), early estimates being one to two hundred thousand (Book 5, Ch31 L170), and a necromancer later animates Makassar's victims as a zombie horde (Mr North says (Book 5, Ch32 L11) it looks like an extension of long-discarded experiments from his own organisation's past; Jason says the necromancer who animated them got his start in North's house, and Book 6, Ch39 L25 names Todd "The Necromancer" Halverson).
- **Aftermath**: Sulawesi later declares independence.
- **The Butterflies of Makassar**: the name news broadcasts give to a public spectacle. Against the zombie horde Jason's familiar Gordon uses his affliction orbs to start the Harbinger of Doom affliction: it drains mana from each victim to conjure incorporeal blue-and-orange butterflies with the eye pattern on their wings, which seek out nearby enemies, detonate with disruptive force when destroyed, and carry every non-holy affliction of the host to the next victim. The swarm spreads from zombie to zombie until the sky is thick with butterflies. Filmed from high in the air it looks beautiful; once the wave passes only black-stained bones remain. Broadcasters credit Jason with it, and in Book 5 Amy cites it as a reason to fear what he might do to "regular people".

### 4. Broken Hill (Book 5)

- A New South Wales outback town whose population had grown from fewer than twenty thousand to almost thirty-five thousand as isolated outback residents were gathered there (Book 5, Ch17 L3); under Network protection with a support team and a nine-person tactical section.
- The EOA's League of Heroes infiltrates the town, cuts communications and a category-three proto-space opens. Jason estimates "somewhere between ten and fifteen thousand people had died" from the bodies and his portal count (Book 5, Ch18 L83), putting survivors at fewer than twenty thousand (Book 5, Ch18 L82).
- On camera Jason confronts the EOA media team and gives a speech. The Network's rebranding as the Global Defense Network follows.

### 5. Other sites

Sydney's Kings Cross (the Stone Wall bar), the Network's Sydney clinic where Jason secretly works as the "Starlight Angel" (Book 4, Ch29 L43), and the Starlight Rider episodes belong to Book 4: the media dub the figure the Starlight Rider after earlier stories of a starlight angel (Book 4, Ch15 L12). They are not full place files.

### 6. Dangers and opportunities

Monsters of category three or four, anomalies, political fallout from civilian deaths. Opportunities: loot, spirit coins and reputation.

### 7. Hooks

- A new incursion in a town with no Network team.
- A survivor of Broken Hill with a story the news missed.

### Open questions

- Exact categories and durations of the later transformation events in Books 5-6.

## Nitra (Slovakia)

### 1. Summary

Nitra, in western Slovakia, is a city that became "a major centre for the Network after the organization had been pushed out of Bratislava by vampires" (Book 5, Ch68 L69). It is too small to host ancient vampires but large enough to warrant Network protection. At the end of Book 5 a transformation zone forms there; in Book 6 Jason claims it and it becomes his first Earth spirit domain.

### 2. The city before the zone

Blessed by its size, it weathered the monster waves well and became a major Network centre. Jack Gerling is found sitting at a café while the zone forms.

### 3. The transformation zone

A glimmering dome several kilometres across, "a giant rainbow under glass" (Book 5, Ch68 L68). Inside is a genesis space in which essence abilities, aura and perception are sealed (racial gifts remain). Jason claims territories (Arrival Pagoda, Soul Haven, Tranquil Shadow Woods, Steamforge Circuit and others; the renamings are at Book 6, Ch5 L41; Ch12 L41, L59; Ch16 L60), stabilises the zone into a permanent astral space, and earns the title Reality Hegemon (Book 6, Ch15 L45).

### 4. Nitra as Jason's domain

### 5. Notable people

Jack Gerling, the Network gold-rankers Chen, Guo and Tran, Yumi Asano and the clan, Craig Vermillion, Carlos Quilido.

### 6. Dangers and hooks

Zone anomalies, vampires, and the strain on the domain when Jason is away. Hooks: an expedition into the zone; a vampire quarter dispute; a domain-wide rust crisis.

### Later developments (spoilers)

### Open questions

- Whether a Quilido Centre exists in Nitra at all.

## Saint-Étienne (France)

### 1. Summary

Saint-Étienne, France, is the site of the Dimensional Fortress, a permanent astral space found by the Network's Lyon branch in 1948. In Book 4 Jason frees Farrah from it; in Book 6 it becomes a second transformation zone, which Jason claims and later turns into a whimsical cloud city of refugees.

### 2. The Dimensional Fortress

- Discovered in 1948 when local Network operatives found "an incursion space dimensionally coterminous with an area near Saint-Étienne" with multiple apertures, as Paul Abreo of the Lyon branch Steering Committee explains (Book 4, Ch51 L28-30).
- Heightened magic; anomalous entities manifest directly; the Lyon branch used it as a monster-hunting reserve and spirit-coin farm and hid it from the wider Network for about seventy years by faking the Grid.
- It looks like dilapidated interconnected manors and crumbling castles in fog and drizzle.
- Adrien Barbou is the Lyon Operations Director, working out of the black site (Book 4, Ch43 L58); Paul Abreo confesses that many of the decisions behind it were "at least in part, mine" (Book 4, Ch51 L28). Barbou holes up in the fortress having sealed the apertures from the inside, and ritual specialists try to breach the seals (Book 4, Ch51 L60-68; Book 4, Ch52 L22-23). Jason passes the seals using Nirvanic Transfiguration.

### 3. The transformation zone and cloud city

### 4. Society

Refugee city for transformed non-humans; the Asano clan lives here; Erika and Ian host picnics. Nigel Thornton's families have joined. Gary Sharpton, a teenage inventor and the reincarnated Gary, lives nearby.

### 5. Dangers and hooks

### Open questions

- How the zone's second claim relates to Jason's time away.

## Arcazitlan

### 1. Summary

Arcazitlan is a fortress town dug into the wall of a rocky gorge in jungle country, where Jason arrives alone at the start of the surge and fights bone feasters (Book 7, Ch25 L77-78). Its region is not named in the lines checked.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Setting**: a fortified town "dug high into the wall of a rocky gorge", reached by adventurers because airships would be crushed against the wall by the gorge's gusts (Book 7, Ch25 L77).
- **Interior**: stone chambers and claustrophobic corridors, favouring defence over comfort; civilians sheltering from the surge are packed in (Book 7, Ch28 L3).
- **Defences**: magical sensors throughout the gorge (Book 7, Ch26 L48) and wind-blade runes (Book 7, Ch31 L42); a military command post watches the fight below (Book 7, Ch26 L48).
- **Roads**: a well-maintained network runs through the surrounding jungles and hills (Book 7, Ch29 L8).

### 3. Society and rule

A militia commander runs the fort (Book 7, Ch26 L48). Adventurers make deliveries (Book 7, Ch25 L77).

### 4. History

- Book 7: Jason lands nearby after dropping from the sky, reaches the fort during a night attack by bone feasters (emaciated, eyeless humanoid monsters with large mouths) and helps defend it (Book 7, Ch25 L77-78); he leaves by road with Shade as a land skimmer (Book 7, Ch29 L8).

### 5. Dangers and opportunities

Surge monsters in the gorge and jungle (Book 7, Ch25 L78); the fort's inaccessibility is both defence and difficulty (Book 7, Ch25 L77).

### Open questions

- Which kingdom holds it, and how far it lies from Rimaros.
