## Cartise

### 1. Summary

Cartise was a river city and the major hub of trade and travel in its area, destroyed just before the great surge when the Builder cult claimed a nearby astral space and a diamond-rank monster then overwhelmed the city (Book 9, Ch29 L17-18). Jason's team clears monsters from its ruins in Book 9.

### 2. Facts

- **Before**: "the river city of Cartise", the major hub for trade and travel in the area (Book 9, Ch29 L17-18). It had many adventurers who now protect the fortress towns (Book 9, Ch35 L62).
- **The fall**: the Builder cult claimed a nearby astral space, which separated from the world with widespread destruction; a diamond-rank monster manifested shortly after and overwhelmed the city; most people were evacuated to towns along the river (Book 9, Ch29 L17-18).
- **After**: the ruins look like London after the Blitz, with blocks reduced to fist-sized rubble (Book 9, Ch30 L59). Refugees overpopulate the surrounding towns and the loss strains logistics (Book 9, Ch29 L18-19; Book 9, Ch35 L60).
- **Clearing the ruins**: Jason's team sweeps a route guided by an Adventure Society functionary named Vestine, as one of several teams (Book 9, Ch31 L11).

### Open questions

- Which river and region; the text names the city but not the land.

---

## Casallini

### 1. Summary

Casallini is a small border city of Girlano, close to the coast where Girlano comes nearest to the Sea of Storms. It is where Sophie's Book 8 delivery contract leads, and where a gang is hired to ambush her on behalf of Purity loyalists (Book 8, Ch24 L25; Book 8, Ch25 L119).

### 2. Location and layout

- **Position**: the border city of Girlano, which lies inland and south of the Storm Kingdom (Book 8, Ch24 L22, L25). The region is the part of Girlano that comes closest to the coast, near enough that a tall hill gives a view of the Sea of Storms (Book 8, Ch24 L26).
- **Border station**: Storm Kingdom border guards run a station at the city gates (Book 8, Ch26 L43-45).
- **Streets**: generously spaced, with rooftops used by Jason and Sophie for a private talk (Book 8, Ch26 L39, L54).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Gangs and cartels**: the surrounding country is run by cartels producing heavily controlled or banned substances from rare plants and minerals (Book 8, Ch24 L22). The eight men who ambush Sophie are members of a small gang from the nearby city, one of many satellite groups to the local cartel, mostly strongmen who keep low-end officials in line (Book 8, Ch24 L21).
- **Hired muscle**: they are silver-rank in their forties, "raw garbage" with no training and reeking of cores, hired by Purity loyalists to bring Sophie in (Book 8, Ch25 L127-130).
- **Adventure Society presence**: the Society ignores borders and keeps monsters out of population centres; its neutrality is why Sophie heads this way, and Liara says "we have a large force in Casallini because it's a border city" (Book 8, Ch24 L24-25; Book 8, Ch25 L136).
- **Smuggler's paths**: semi-hidden paths through jungle-covered hills avoid border checkpoints, which have no patrols during the surge (Book 8, Ch24 L26-27).
- **Character**: "a lawless town full of crappy guys" in Sophie's words (Book 8, Ch26 L66).

### 4. History

Book 8: the gang is hired and the ambush fails (Book 8, Ch24 L21-30; Book 8, Ch25 L117-130). Jason and Liara judge the tip to be a trap, a "worm on a hook" from the Order of Redeeming Light, and Liara cancels Sophie's contract and sends Storm Kingdom civic forces to sweep the gang up (Book 8, Ch25 L133-138; Book 8, Ch26 L7). Sophie, meanwhile, scouts the city on her own and hands her prisoners to the border guards (Book 8, Ch26 L39-72).

### Open questions

- Whether the sweep captured the Purity loyalists; the lines read do not say.

---

## Girlano

### 1. Summary

Girlano is a landlocked inland nation south of the Storm Kingdom, ostensibly a kingdom but controlled by cartels that produce heavily controlled or banned substances, with a puppet government (Book 8, Ch24 L22-23). It is a smuggling region on the Storm Kingdom's border.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Position**: inland, to the south of the Storm Kingdom, whose coast it comes close to; from a tall hill the Sea of Storms can be seen (Book 8, Ch24 L22, L26).
- **Border city**: Casallini (Book 8, Ch24 L25).
- **Border country**: jungle-covered hills with many semi-hidden smuggling paths between the border and the shore (Book 8, Ch24 L26).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Cartels**: the illicit leadership keeps its position through under-the-table deals with powerful groups in neighbouring countries, which prop up the puppet government while their own governments pay lip service to suppression (Book 8, Ch24 L23).
- **Trade**: the rare plants and minerals of the region are what the cartels exploit, and strong public sanctions and border controls surround the nation (Book 8, Ch24 L22).
- **Adventure Society**: it ignores borders and politics and keeps monsters out of population centres, so long as its work is not interfered with (Book 8, Ch24 L24-25).
- **Reputation**: Liara calls it "a stain full of drug dealers and smugglers" (Book 8, Ch25 L120); Belinda finds it "famously sketchy" and tempting (Book 9, Ch26 L3).

### 4. History

- Book 8: Sophie heads toward Girlano and Casallini on a specialised delivery (Book 8, Ch24 L25).
- Book 9: the team turns east at the coast, not south toward Girlano (Book 9, Ch26 L3).

### Open questions

- Its ruler and capital; only the border region is described.

---

## Hornis

### 1. Summary

Hornis is a port city on the coast south and around from Greenstone, reached overland through the delta, the veldt and then south (Book 1, Ch102 L11; Book 3, Ch24 L17). It is Greenstone's nearest rival port, used for smuggling and escapes, and a stop on the team's route home in Book 9.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Location**: "a port city, like Greenstone, south and around the coast"; the overland route runs east into the veldt and then south (Book 3, Ch24 L17).
- **Port**: smuggled goods can come in "through the port in Hornis or the one here in Greenstone" (Book 2, Ch14 L16).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Adventure Society**: Hornis has a branch that mounts large searches (Book 2, Ch22 L76).
- **Magic Society**: Hornis has its own Magic Society people (Book 2, Ch17 L61) and a mirage chamber that Danielle helped rebuild and maintains (Book 2, Ch17 L60).
- **Underworld**: the Big Three's influence is a factor in Greenstone; Sophie plans to buy her way past it in Hornis (Book 1, Ch102 L11).

### 4. Magic level

Not stated.

### 5. History

- Book 1: Sophie plans to flee through the delta and the veldt to Hornis with the spirit coin vault's money (Book 1, Ch102 L11).
- Book 2: smuggled materials are traced to shipments into Hornis before being moved to private vessels (Book 2, Ch27 L4); Jonah's team is found in Hornis on a boat bound for distant shores (Book 2, Ch14 L116; Book 2, Ch15 L25).
- Book 3: Lucian Lamprey hopes to escape to Hornis through a contact (Book 3, Ch24 L12-17).
- Book 9: the team's route home runs south, across the Great Southern Continent, then north to Hornis on the way to Greenstone, Vitesse and Cyrion (Book 9, Ch25 L4).

### 6. Notable people

None named in the lines checked.

### Open questions

- Distance and travel time to Greenstone (the overland route is only described, not measured).

## Isart

### 1. Summary

Isart is named once: a city on Jason's procession south where Emir asks to meet him to discuss a job and bring the person who made the cloud flasks (Book 9, Ch39 L12-26).

### 2. Facts

- Emir says there is "a city called Isart that you should arrive at on your procession south" (Book 9, Ch39 L12), will meet Jason there when the detour is done (Book 9, Ch39 L16), will arrive in about two weeks and bring the maker of the cloud flasks (Book 9, Ch39 L26).
- No later scene in the chapter files shows Jason reaching Isart, and nothing describes the city.

### Open questions

- Where Isart is, whether the meeting happens, and what the job is.

## Jayapura

### 1. Summary

Jayapura is a port on the northern coast of Pranay, the large southern land equivalent to Sri Lanka, and the birthplace of Hester (Book 2, Ch69 L65-66). Jason's team stays several days after the Reaper trial to awaken abilities, summon familiars and get tattoos (Book 2, Ch74 L14).

### 2. Location and layout

- **Setting**: one of the important sea-trade ports on Pranay's northern coast, in a world with no Arabian Peninsula (Book 2, Ch69 L65-66).
- **Arrival**: Hester opens a portal into the city; Jason and Sophie, with astral affinities, are unaffected by the transition (Book 2, Ch69 L66).
- **Mirage chamber complex**: larger and more sophisticated than the Geller chamber in Greenstone, with concurrent chambers, because the magic is denser (Book 2, Ch73 L3).
- **Mystic Quarter and markets**: floating discs for sale in the Mystic Quarter (Book 3, Ch5 L83); markets the team visits (Book 3, Ch32 L65).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Mirage Chamber Association**: owns the complex, has close ties to the Magic Society, Adventure Society and local government and holds significant power (Book 2, Ch73 L4-5).
- **Entertainment**: essence users fight one another or illusions for a paying audience (Book 2, Ch73 L4).

### 4. Magic level

Higher than Greenstone; it allows advanced dimensional effects (Book 2, Ch73 L3).

### 5. Customs and daily life

Those who can afford them ride platter-sized floating discs (Book 2, Ch70 L3); the team finds new customs and food (Book 2, Ch74 L14); Jason remembers kulfi-like ice cream there (Book 7, Ch14 L52). A nondescript tattoo parlour run by Tilly is where Jason gets his crest (Book 2, Ch73 L62).

### 6. History

Book 2: the visit after the Reaper trial (Book 2, Ch69-74). Book 3: the team's mirage-arena memories inform their confidence (Book 3, Ch45 L100).

### Open questions

- Its rulers, and how large it is; only the Mirage Chamber Association's role is described.

## Kurdansk

### 1. Summary

### 2. Location and layout

- **Nation**: the People's Holy Federation of Dreisil (Book 6, Ch47 L390). Humphrey remarks that the more a nation calls itself free and righteous, the more tyrannical it is (Book 6, Ch47 L392).
- **Entertainment quarter**: a former warehouse district by the Kurdan River docks, now the most viable site for large theatre halls, "a heady mix of pleasure, criminality and money" (Book 6, Ch47 L436).
- **Skyport**: busy, with towers where airships dock; Emir's cloud ship recovers its full function there (Book 6, Ch47 L459).
- **Houses**: narrow row houses wedged together (Book 6, Ch47 L508).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Peoples**: celestines with dark skin and silver hair are common (Book 6, Ch47 L437, L508).

### 4. History

- Book 6: Emir tells Sophie she was born in Kurdansk (Book 6, Ch47 L386-390); the team searches the city, and Marta Fries, a silver-rank celestine and friend of Melody Jain, escapes through a hidden panel Melody once used to fake her death, about two decades earlier (Book 6, Ch47 L508-510).
- Book 9: Melody Jain's story begins in Kurdansk about twenty-five years before; she and Sophie's father were both secret members of organisations, the Order of the Reaper among them (Book 9, Ch27 L24-26).

### Open questions

- Its size and ruler, and the Federation's structure.

## Leust

### 1. Summary

Leust is one of the largest and richest towns in the delta, with paved roads and stone buildings instead of mud brick (Book 1, Ch93 L5). Jason stays there while investigating a fergax contract in the nearby lumber region.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Town**: mostly yellow desert stone, with some green stone buildings; the water affinity of green stone keeps interiors cool, and Jason stays at a large green stone inn (Book 1, Ch93 L5-6).
- **Lumber region**: on the south side of the river in the eastern delta, the furthest part from the city; more solid ground than marsh, laid out like a forestry reserve with trees in neat rows, saplings and cleared fields (Book 1, Ch93 L3, L22). Wagons carry lumber to Leust (Book 1, Ch93 L22).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Industry**: lumber; the land rights are heavily regulated (Book 1, Ch92 L63), and the lumber region is one of the few places in the delta where lumber-worthy trees grow (Book 1, Ch92 L61).
- **Mill owners**: Kyle Lindover, a leonid larger than Gary, owns the Lindover Lumber Mill and shut it after repeated fergax sightings (Book 1, Ch93 L24, L28); his family stays with her parents in Greenstone (Book 1, Ch93 L29).

### 4. History

Book 1: Jason takes an unusual contract to hunt a fergax that is never found (Book 1, Ch92 L59-65), stays in Leust (Book 1, Ch93 L5-8), finds the Clementson property and the adventurer Dean Tuckell summoning monsters, and learns that Thadwick Mercer set it up (Book 1, Ch94 L8-13, L58).

### Open questions

- Its exact position in the delta relative to Greenstone.

---

## The Lindover and Clementson Properties

### 1. Summary

Two neighbouring lumber properties in the eastern delta, near Leust: Kyle Lindover's idle mill and the busy, ostentatious property of Eustace Clementson. In Book 1 a "fergax" sighting contract Jason takes turns out to be a land-grab staged by Thadwick Mercer, and the properties are where he finds the summoner behind it.

### 2. The Lindover property

- **Mill and house**: a still, empty lumber mill, past which Jason finds a "sizeable farmhouse" (Book 1, Ch93 L23). The mill is reached through a wooden archway bearing its name on a road through neat rows of trees (Book 1, Ch93 L22-23).
- **Owner**: Kyle Lindover is a leonid larger than Gary, "the most lumberjack-looking man Jason had ever seen" (Book 1, Ch93 L24). He shut the mill after repeated fergax sightings so no worker would be hurt; his wife and children are staying with her parents in Greenstone (Book 1, Ch93 L28-29).
- **Stakes**: his reserves are falling short, most workers have taken jobs elsewhere, and he fears a black mark on his record (Book 1, Ch93 L28-29).
- **Tracks**: bear tracks are almost identical to a fergax's, so other adventurers dismissed his reports (Book 1, Ch93 L81).

### 3. The Clementson property

- **Layout**: laid out like the Lindover property but in full operation, with magical saws audible from the mill and a farmhouse "both larger and more ostentatious" (Book 1, Ch93 L84).
- **Owner**: Eustace Clementson, who sweats and tries to divert Jason, then falls in behind him under his aura (Book 1, Ch93 L88-99).
- **Guest house**: a stone-and-wood building apart from the main residence, where the iron-rank adventurer Dean Tuckell lays low between visits (Book 1, Ch93 L99; Book 1, Ch94 L70).

### 4. History

Book 1: Jason takes the contract at the Adventure Society, stays at an inn in Leust, and tracks the monster's aura from Lindover's land to Clementson's property (Book 1, Ch93 L19-83). Dean says Thadwick Mercer set up the "land-grab deal": every couple of weeks Dean spooked Lindover with his summon and then hid in Clementson's guest house (Book 1, Ch94 L58-72). In the settlement Jason negotiates with Thalia Mercer, the Mercer family compensates Lindover for the months of stalled production and Clementson's takeover preparations are rolled back (Book 1, Ch97 L97-124).

### Open questions

- How Clementson was recruited, and what the Mercer family planned to gain from the land beyond "you'd probably end up with the land, anyway" (Book 1, Ch97 L97).

## Oleyu

### 1. Summary

Oleyu is an unremarkable border city on a river, "pleasant, prosperous, and moderately sized", built on river trade with a mid-range level of magic (Book 6, Ch47 L840).

### 2. Facts

- **Position**: Clive's route follows a river to "the border city of Oleyu", where the Rakesh Magic Society branch's area of influence ends (Book 6, Ch47 L838).
- **Standing**: not as big or important as Rakesh or Vitesse, or unusual like Greenstone (Book 6, Ch47 L840).
- **Magic Society building**: Clive lodges a protest here over his treatment by the Rakesh branch, without optimism (Book 6, Ch47 L841-842).

- Which border it lies on, and which river.

---

## Palisaros

### 1. Summary

Palisaros is a small laid-back town on the island of Arnote in the Storm Kingdom, arranged along a lagoon and the clifftop above it, where Jason settles his cloud house (Book 7, Ch13 L33; Book 8, Ch28 L59; Book 8, Ch52 L20).

### 2. Location and layout

- **Island**: Arnote, the quiet residential island of Rimaros's three public islands (Book 7, Ch7 L70; Book 8, Ch28 L59).
- **Lagoon and cliff**: the town lies along the lagoon shore and the clifftop bordering it, with a waterfall and a river spilling over the cliff (Book 8, Ch28 L59).
- **Houses**: colourful, two or three storeys, with open space, covered walkways, balconies and awnings, and hidden magical amenities (Book 7, Ch13 L35).
- **Jason's house**: his cloud house stands on a clifftop near the river, with an invisible barrier along the cliff edge (Book 8, Ch52 L20).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Residents**: mostly normals, irons and bronzes using cores, with a few silver and gold auras, and many caramel-skinned celestines (Book 7, Ch13 L34).
- **Pace**: small-town, with none of the residents in a rush, calmer than busy Livaros (Book 7, Ch13 L33).
- **Mayor**: Pelli, a member of the royal family but separate from them, living a relatively humble life as a core user (Book 7, Ch61 L59; Book 8, Ch62 L60).
- **Guide**: Argy shows visitors the saloon, eateries and market (Book 7, Ch13 L35).

### 4. History

- Book 7: Jason meets Argy and Pelli in the town and settles there (Book 7, Ch13 L20-35; Book 7, Ch41 L61); during the surge only Jason and Pelli remain to defend it, and Jason leaves shortly after his team (Book 7, Ch61 L60).
- Book 8: Vidal rides into the lagoon to find Wexler's team staying in a house by the waterfall (Book 8, Ch28 L59).

### Open questions

- Population and layout beyond the clifftop and lagoon.

---

## Rakesh

### 1. Summary

Rakesh is a populous, caste-divided city on the north coast of the Africa-like continent, home of the Adventure Society's continental council and of the continent's largest Magic Society and Adventure Society strongholds (Book 6, Ch47 L623). It is where the Magic Society holds Clive in all but name and where Danielle meets Carlivexistrix.

### 2. Location and layout

- **Setting**: on the north coast; a desert gulch with a hidden Builder-cult portal lies a few dozen kilometres south (Book 6, Ch47 L623, L651).
- **Buildings**: desert stone, plastered and painted in large, coordinated murals, each district's dominant colour grading into the next (Book 6, Ch47 L709).
- **Campus**: one sprawling campus joins the largest Magic Society and Adventure Society strongholds on the continent (Book 6, Ch47 L623).
- **Link to Vitesse**: Danielle teleports Humphrey and Sophie across the Gramid Passage from Vitesse to Rakesh (Book 6, Ch47 L703).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Caste system**: society is divided into castes, and the caste system justifies holding someone seen as lowly against their will (Book 6, Ch47 L658); it dismisses Belinda as unimportant (Book 6, Ch47 L660).
- **Culture**: busy but quiet streets, a local culture that values calm decorum, most people on foot or in animal-drawn carriages (Book 6, Ch47 L710).
- **The Magic Society branch**: one of the most powerful on the continent; officials there have power and connections, so consequences come from the Adventure Society, not other branches (Book 6, Ch47 L841). The campus prevents portals outside designated zones (Book 6, Ch47 L656).
- **The Adventure Society**: the continental council sits here (Book 6, Ch47 L623). Vitesse is also described as the seat of a continental council (Book 6, Ch47 L604); the text reads most simply as one council per continent, with Rakesh serving the Africa-like continent and Vitesse the continent of Estercost, but it never states this.

### 4. Magic level

Enough to support floating platforms, a common sight, unlike low-magic Greenstone (Book 6, Ch47 L707).

### 5. History

- Book 6: Clive is recruited from Greenstone by Lorelei Grantham, Vice-Dean of the astral magic research department, and finds himself a prisoner of the campus; he and Belinda spend about two months planning an escape through the Builder cult's portal network (Book 6, Ch47 L656-662). He later lodges a protest in Oleyu about his treatment by the Rakesh branch (Book 6, Ch47 L841-842). The Rakesh Magic Society wants the cult's astral magic, not the fight against it (Book 6, Ch47 L865).

### 6. Notable people

Lorelei Grantham (Vice-Dean), Lady Ajus (an official interested in Clive's research) (Book 6, Ch47 L662); Carlivexistrix, whom Danielle will not keep waiting (Book 6, Ch47 L704-706).

### 7. Customs and daily life

Floating platforms for essence users, from standing discs to floating palanquins (Book 6, Ch47 L710); calm public manners (Book 6, Ch47 L710).

### Open questions

- Whether the continental council at Rakesh and the one at Vitesse are two bodies for two continents (see section 3).

---

## Zartos

### 1. Summary

Zartos is a subterranean city built around an underground river, largely populated by celestines, and the home of the diamond-rank smith Virid, Gary's mentor (Book 7, Ch2 L71, L75). It is named early as a celestine centre and appears on the page only when Gary and Virid reforge Jason's sword there.

### 2. Location and layout

- A city built around an underground river (Book 7, Ch2 L75). Its region is not stated in the lines checked.
- Virid keeps a personal smithy in the city, "the best place to forge a great work" (Book 7, Ch2 L71-72).

### 3. Society and rule

- **Peoples**: "Zartos has a large celestine community" (Book 1, Ch16 L57); the city is "largely populated by celestines" (Book 7, Ch2 L75).
- No ruler or institution is named in the lines checked.

### 4. Magic level

Not stated. A diamond-ranker lives and works there (Book 7, Ch2 L71).

### 5. History

- Book 1: Gary and Farrah mention Zartos on the road; Rufus has not been there, though his brother recommended it (Book 1, Ch16 L53-58).
- Book 7: after Jason's death the team waits in Zartos while Gary and Virid examine and reforge Dread Salvation, a collaboration that Jason's soul-bond guides (Book 7, Ch2 L71-75); the sword returns as Hegemon's Will (Book 7, Ch54 L108-120).

### 6. Notable people

Virid Martine (Book 6, Ch47 L270), diamond-rank weaponsmith and Gary's mentor (Book 7, Ch1 L30; Book 7, Ch2 L71).

### 7. Customs and daily life

Nothing further is established; the team's stay is described only as a reunion while the smiths work (Book 7, Ch2 L75).

### Open questions

- Where Zartos lies, and who governs it.

---

## The Africa-like Continent

### 1. Summary

The continent that Jason would call Africa holds Greenstone, where Books 1-3 are set. In the text it is separate from Eurasia (see the World Primer), is far more populous in the north than the south, and has its continental Adventure Society and Magic Society strongholds at Rakesh on the north coast (Book 6, Ch47 L623). This file sets out the places the compendium already files here, from south to north.

### 2. The far south: Greenstone and its delta

- **Magic level**: Clive rates Greenstone's density at about mid-iron, "which is very low" (Book 3, Ch34 L60). The cities of the south are low-magic and sparsely populated, while the north is far more populous (Book 6, Ch47 L623 in the Greenstone file).
- **The delta and the veldt**: the overland route from Greenstone runs through the delta, east into the veldt and then south to Hornis (Book 1, Ch102 L11; Book 3, Ch24 L17).
- **The lumber region**: on the south side of the river in the eastern delta, farthest from the city, is Leust with the Lindover and Clementson lumber properties (Book 1, Ch93 L3, L22; see ../Minor_Places/Leust.md and ../Minor_Places/Lindover_and_Clementson_Properties.md).

### 3. Ports and cities of the south and coast

- **Hornis** is a port "south and around the coast" from Greenstone, Greenstone's nearest rival port, used for smuggling and escape routes (Book 3, Ch24 L17; Book 2, Ch14 L16).
- **The Ustei**: a desert people whose fashion is less calm than Boko's (Book 1, Ch113 L21).

### 4. The north: Rakesh and the border country

- **Rakesh** is a populous, caste-divided city on the north coast, with a desert gulch and a hidden Builder-cult portal a few dozen kilometres south (Book 6, Ch47 L623, L651). The Gramid Passage links it with Vitesse (Book 6, Ch47 L703).
- **Oleyu** is a pleasant, prosperous border city of moderate size on river trade, where the Rakesh Magic Society branch's area of influence ends (Book 6, Ch47 L838-840).
- **Zartos** is a celestine-populated underground city around an underground river; its continent is not stated (Book 7, Ch2 L75).

### 5. Councils and authority

- The continental council of the Adventure Society sits at Rakesh (Book 6, Ch47 L623), while Cyrion and Vitesse are also described as seats of a council; the text reads most simply as one council per continent (see ../Minor_Places/Rakesh.md).

### 6. The Great Southern Continent

The only mention is the team's route plan in Book 9: move south down the continent, cross to the Great Southern Continent, cross north again to Hornis, then on to Greenstone, Vitesse and Cyrion (Book 9, Ch25 L4). Nothing describes it.

### 7. Open questions

- Where Zartos and Sadi Andali lie, and which of them sit on this continent.
- Which river and region Cartise and Arcazitlan belong to.
- The relationship between the continental councils at Rakesh and in Estercost.

---

## Estercost and Its Continent

### 1. Summary

Estercost is the kingdom whose capital Cyrion is the largest city in the world and whose Vitesse sits on the equivalent of the French Riviera (Book 6, Ch47 L603). The text places it on the Europe-like continent and calls it the home of Greenstone's founders. It is the setting of most of Books 6 and 11 and the base of Remore Academy and the Burning Violet guild.

### 2. The kingdom

### 3. Links to other regions

- **Rakesh** is reached from Vitesse across the Gramid Passage (Book 6, Ch47 L703).
- **Kurdansk** is in the People's Holy Federation of Dreisil, Sophie's birthplace; the files do not say it is on this continent (Book 6, Ch47 L390).

### 4. Magic and culture

The books give no numeric density figure for Vitesse, but its monsters run to silver with frequent gold (Book 1, Ch30 L44; see ../Vitesse.md). For customs and dress see ../../04_Peoples_and_Cultures/Estercost_Culture.md and Vitesse_Culture.md.

### 5. Open questions

- Exact geography between Cyrion, Vitesse and Kazlahk (travel times only).
- Whether Dreisil lies on this continent.
- The monarch's name.

---

## The Storm Kingdom's Islands

### 1. Summary

The Storm Kingdom is a nation of islands in the Sea of Storms, the Caribbean analogue (Book 7, Ch3 L44-47), founded by Soramir Rimaros. Its best-known city, Rimaros, spans three public islands; its largest and most populous city is Jaitari (Book 9, Ch3 L81-82). Beyond the islands lies Girlano, the cartel country along its southern border.

### 2. The three public islands

The three public islands of Rimaros run east to west: Livaros, Arnote and Provo (Book 7, Ch7 L70).

- **Livaros**: "the adventurer island", with the Adventure Society campus, the skyport and most services for essence users (Book 7, Ch7 L78; Book 7, Ch10 L5).
- **Arnote**: the westernmost island, home to wealthier citizens and a minor branch of the royal family, the least populous and least defended (Book 7, Ch7 L70-72; Book 7, Ch64 L21). Palisaros is a laid-back town on it, where Jason's cloud house stands (Book 8, Ch52 L20).
- **Provo**: the easternmost, most populous island, the trade hub with most of the non-magical citizens (Book 7, Ch40 L4).
- **The Royal Sky Island**: the palace, above the islands.

### 3. Beyond the three islands

- **The central landmass**: the largest concentration of the kingdom's people lives on a central landmass made up of what would be Cuba, Haiti and the Dominican Republic (see ../Rimaros.md); the largest city there is Jaitari (Book 9, Ch3 L81-82).
- **Defences**: windmill-like storm accumulators stand offshore, shielding islands and powering infrastructure; House Irios designs and maintains Rimaros's defences (see ../Rimaros.md).
- **The coast and Girlano**: the Storm Kingdom controls the coast, and inland Girlano lies to its south, close enough that the Sea of Storms is visible from a tall hill (Book 8, Ch24 L22, L26). Casallini is Girlano's border city (Book 8, Ch24 L25).
- **Surges and forces**: the Storm Kingdom provides border-station guards and civic forces beyond the Adventure Society (Book 8, Ch26 L43-45; Book 8, Ch25 L136).

### 4. Rule

The Storm King must be gold-rank; the previous king picks a successor after a contest (see ../Rimaros.md). Soramir Rimaros, the founder, is still alive and deferred to even by the king (Book 9, Ch14 L4). Royal guard duties fall to the Sapphire Crown guild (see ../../05_Organisations/Guilds/Sapphire_Crown.md).

### 5. Open questions

- The Storm Kingdom's full island list beyond the three public islands and the central landmass.
- How far the Caribbean comparison is intended to be literal.

## Adrien Barbou

### 1. Summary

The Network's Lyon Operations Director, who imprisons Farrah in the dimensional fortress, rises to be the second "Mr East" and later follows Gerling.

### 2. Facts

- Operations Director of the Network's Lyon branch, working out of the black site (Book 4, Ch43 L58); one of the secretive staff who knew of and worked in the dimensional fortress, and has been cultivating loyalists (Book 4, Ch51 L54).
- He leaves the dimensional space by another aperture just before it is sealed, alone, with no EOA and no prisoner (Book 4, Ch52 L94).
- In Book 5 he takes the seat of Mr East, speaking of "the previous Mr East" and of vetting his people (Book 5, Ch7 L96; Book 5, Ch8 L35). Mr North says his own minions were disappointing before Barbou came along (Book 5, Ch40 L86).
- Anna says Barbou is not hers to give; Farrah says "Barbou belongs to me" (Book 6, Ch26 L9-20).
- Gerling hits him and demands help reaching the Saint-Étienne dimensional space (Book 6, Ch30 L40-43). In the zone he is the only one not attacked by the soul attacks (Book 6, Ch42 L22) and Jason declines to take him (Book 6, Ch43 L75).

### 3. Timeline of involvement

- Book 4: dimensional fortress (Book 4, Ch43-52).
- Book 5: Mr East (Book 5, Ch7-8).
- Book 6: Gerling's prisoner and ally (Book 6, Ch26-43).

### 4. Roleplay notes

Calculating and self-protective; he stays at the edge of a fight and tries to look insignificant (Book 6, Ch42 L22).

### Open questions

## Amos Pensinata

### 1. Summary

A gold-rank adventurer with a soul-damaged aura who teaches Jason aura and soul techniques and is lost to an orb in Cardinas.

### 2. Facts

- Trenchant asks whether anyone has worked with Amos Pensinata (Book 7, Ch44 L30). Dawn names him as a gold-ranker whose experiences with soul trauma left him with a more capable aura than most; she has convinced him to travel with Jason to teach soul manipulation (Book 9, Ch6 L47).
- Jason knows him by reputation as the man who defeated the same forces that had forced Jason to flee the underwater complex (Book 9, Ch6 L48).
- Clive turns on him at the Yaresh camp: "You were brought on to help Jason, not make things worse. But your baggage..." (Book 9, Ch42 L90).
- His nephew is Orin Pensinata (Book 7, Ch47 L39; see `Orin_Pensinata.md`); he joins Jason's journey partly because Orin insisted on coming (Book 9, Ch24 L35).

### 3. Roleplay notes

Calm and wry, with a teacher's patience and a soldier's scars.

### Open questions

## Anisa Lasalle

### 1. Summary

Anisa Lasalle is a zealous elf priestess of Purity from the Lasalle family who guides Rufus's party in Book 1, is thrown out for killing a captive, and dies serving the Builder's cult alliance in Book 3. Cold, righteous and quick to anger, she treats mercy as weakness.

### 2. Appearance

- An elf, platinum-haired and fair-skinned, in fitted white adventuring gear (Book 2, Ch10 L92; Book 3, Ch41 L53).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Zealous**: Rufus calls her "Zealous girl. Dangerously committed" (Book 1, Ch65 L132).
- **Ruthless**: Farrah says "Anisa killed him before he could get a word out" (Book 1, Ch30 L26).
- **Defiant of correction**: Gary tells her "You don't get a say in what we do anymore, Anisa... You're out" (Book 1, Ch30 L86-93).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- In the Book 3 fight she is "something of a perfect weapon against Jason's powers", able to protect herself and cleanse afflictions (Book 3, Ch58 L30).

### 5. Background

- A temporary addition to Rufus's party at the start of the books (Book 1, Ch19 L74).
- After Purity is implicated in the cult she vanishes with the archbishop and much of the Lasalle family (Book 3, Ch1 L106).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: Rufus's party (Book 1, Ch19-30).
- **Book 2**: runs the temple's response to a star seed incident ("We are evacuating no one", Book 2, Ch10 L102); leads church militants to claim an abandoned site (Book 2, Ch37 L105-114).
- **Book 3**: meets the cult's Timos at the Vane estate (Book 3, Ch41 L51-76); stands near Archbishop Hendren (Book 3, Ch54 L23-24); dies in the battle (Book 3, Ch58-60 L5).

### 7. Relationships

- **Rufus and Farrah**: former party leaders (Book 1, Ch30 L26).
- **Nicolas Hendren**: her archbishop (Book 3, Ch54 L23-24).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Jason notes most of the people he first met in this world are dead (Book 3, Ch60 L5).

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Cold, righteous and quick to anger.

## Annabeth "Anna" Tilden

### 1. Summary

### 2. Personality and background

- **Family**: wife Susan; brother Terrance, the Network's media director (Book 4, Ch5 L5-7; Book 5, Ch8 L69).

### 3. Timeline

- **Book 4**: meets Jason and negotiates with him; her group's agreement template for his family is "the template we intend to use for your entire family" (Book 4, Ch28 L48; Book 4, Ch36 L35; Book 4, Ch59 L35).
- **Book 6**: Anna is asked to be go-between for a meeting of Network, EOA and Cabal members held in Jason's spirit domain, and arrives with Craig Vermillion and Taika Williams (Book 6, Ch23 L15; Book 6, Ch24 L5); the meeting takes place outside the city of Nitra, where a magical dome has appeared (Book 6, Ch1 L4; Book 6, Ch24 L4), and the GDN is the Network's name after going public (Book 6, Ch1 L27); Anna is asked to be go-between for a meeting arranged by the GDN (Book 6, Ch23 L15), and arrives at the GDN camp in a party of eight that includes former EOA members who joined the GDN (Book 6, Ch24 L3, L5 to L8); that she belongs to the GDN delegation is not stated.

Jason, Ketevan, Susan, Terrance, Boris, Taika, the Asano clan.

### 5. Roleplay notes

Measured, political, quick to move sides when the facts change.

## Argy

### 1. Summary

Argy (Emmett Dillivan) is a fruit grower in Palisaros who takes Jason, Rufus and Farrah into town and to the mayor, Pelli. Easygoing and proud of his town, he treats visitors as guests.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read.

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Laconic**: Jason "happily fell into Argy's laconic pace" (Book 7, Ch13 L31).
- **Hospitable**: he greets everyone he passes in the sealed streets (Book 7, Ch13 L32).
- **Friendly nickname**: "Emmett Dillivan, but folks just call me Argy" (Book 7, Ch13 L18-20).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Not an essence user in the lines read; his family has grown the fruit "longer than most can remember" (Book 7, Ch13 L20).

### 5. Background

- Named for the fruit he is eating, which looks like a large tangerine (Book 7, Ch13 L18-20).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 7**: leads Jason to town (Book 7, Ch13 L28-32); walks them to the cliffs and waterfall cave (Book 7, Ch13 L75-99).

### 7. Relationships

- **Pelli**: local mayor; he says "You'll want to talk to Pelli" (Book 7, Ch13 L28).
- **Jason**: calls Palisaros "a genuine paradise" (Book 7, Ch13 L24).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Easygoing and proud of his town. Cue: "folks just call me Argy."

## Asya Karadeniz

### 1. Summary

Asya Karadeniz is a school friend of Jason's from the Mid North Coast who works in Network magitech administration. She joins Jason's side in Book 4, crews Kaito's helicopter with Greg, enters Jason's spirit vault as their relationship deepens, and dies in the Berlin attack in Book 5. Her family stays in the Asano clan afterwards.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read.

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Dry about herself**: "I was always fancy" (Book 4, Ch36 L37).
- **Modest about her job**: "I'm just an administrator there... My job is to keep the people doing the actual work happy and funded" (Book 4, Ch36 L39).
- **Deliberate in contact**: she asked for the Sydney slot because she went to school with Jason (Book 4, Ch36 L41).
- **Shaken by the gap between boy and man**: the recording of Jason's power unnerved her, set against "the boy she had known" with his "sexy, impish grin" (Book 4, Ch43 L24-25).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- An executive-level Network administrator given an essence to reach category one, without monster cores (Book 4, Ch36 L77).
- She operates the helicopter's weapons when a winged lizard enters her sights (Book 5, Ch7 L21-23).

### 5. Background

- Went to school with Jason and Greg (Book 4, Ch36 L41; Book 4, Ch66 L59).
- Her mother Rabia sits on the Asano Village committee for the non-Asano families and helps introduce the village to magic (Book 5, Ch13 L75).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 4**: she arrives with Anna and speaks with Jason's circle (Book 4, Ch36 L37-41); follows Hector at the cloud lodge (Book 4, Ch52 L83); visits the Network's local headquarters with Jason, Farrah and Jason's sister (Book 4, Ch59 L7).
- **Book 5**: on the plane and in the helicopter crew with Greg (Book 5, Ch7 L12; Book 5, Ch25 L54); sits beside Jason on the motorboat (Book 5, Ch39 L110); the core of the new combat team with Greg and Kaito (Book 5, Ch44 L17); dies in Berlin (Book 5, Ch51 L40-52).

### 7. Relationships

- **Jason**: old school friend; she enters his spirit vault "as her relationship with Jason deepened" (Book 5, Ch44 L45).
- **Greg and Kaito**: teammates (Book 5, Ch44 L17).
- **Anna**: "The bastards killed Asya" (Book 5, Ch51 L52).
- **Rabia and the Karadeniz family**: mother and relatives (Book 5, Ch13 L75; Book 5, Ch56 L24-26).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Brisk, wry and unafraid of Jason's reputation; modest about her desk job. Cue: "I'm just an administrator there."

## Baseph, Dara, Zareen and Joseph

### 1. Summary

Liara Rimaros's husband and their three silver-rank children.

### 2. Facts

- Baseph is Liara's husband, silver rank, met leaving her townhouse (Book 7, Ch17 L20). In Book 8 he hammers a pipe to draw raiders (Book 8, Ch33 L64), fears what lies below because it is people who died because of what he did (Book 8, Ch37 L77) and is saved by Jason (Book 8, Ch62 L51). He holds Liara's hand under the table (Book 8, Ch63 L3).
- Dara is the eldest, a one-star adventurer happy to hunt monsters, with Liara's height and Baseph's dark copper hair and eyes, a muscular frontline combatant (Book 8, Ch62 L39).
- Zareen is the middle child, physically smaller, with the royal family's sapphire hair and eyes, a politics enthusiast close to Vesper and a three-star adventurer (Book 8, Ch62 L36).
- Joseph is the youngest and only son, an administrative official with the Amouz family business interests like his father (Book 8, Ch62 L32); he wants to meet the man who saved his father (Book 8, Ch62 L51).
- Dara's later remark about "the one from up north" with the elf twins refers to Rick (Book 9, Ch11 L60).

### 3. Roleplay notes

A warm family dinner: Zareen sharp and strategic, Dara blunt and cheerful, Joseph mild.

## Belinda Callahan

### 1. Summary

Belinda "Lindy" Callahan is Sophie's oldest friend, a thief from Greenstone's Old City who becomes Clive's assistant and, in time, the team's planner, infiltrator and master of shapeshifting.

### 2. Appearance

### 3. Personality and voice

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Essences**: Magic, Trap and Adept, which she traded for in Book 2 (Book 2, Ch32 L67-72), producing the Charlatan confluence (Book 2, Ch32 L67-72).
- **Abilities**: Echo Spirit is her first charlatan ability (Book 2, Ch68 L39-45); Bait and Switch, Rune Trap, Force Tether, Lightning Tether and Pit of the Reaper (wiki); shapeshifting (notes, Book 8).
- **Storage**: a personal storage space that even the Magic Society cannot peek into "without killing her first" (Book 6, Ch47 L660).

### 5. Background

Grew up in the Old City with Sophie (notes, Book 2); began working with Clive at the Magic Society after she was caught (Book 2, Ch12 L6); the notes add that in Rakesh she works as the off-campus errand-runner while the caste system dismisses her as unimportant (notes, Book 6); she tracks a signal in the desert south of Rakesh (Book 6, Ch47 L651).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Books 1-2**: a thief; joins Clive.
- **Book 6**: helps Clive escape the Magic Society in Rakesh.
- **Book 8**: infiltrates the Order of Redeeming Light.

### 7. Relationships

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Her infiltration identities are levels 2-3.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Delighted by her own cleverness, and teasing. Cues: "Charlatan," she says with glee (Book 2, Ch32 L72); she urges Jason to find an excuse to head up on deck where Sophie can see him (Book 7, Ch6 L42). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

Mischievous, observant, quick to play a role.

## Benella

### 1. Summary

Benella is a servant of the messengers who works as Zolit's aide, brings Jason to the messengers' attention and is given one chance to prove herself valuable. Careful, watchful and afraid, she hides what she thinks behind a servant's neutrality.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read; she carries a duffel of fight outfits (Book 9, Ch46 L44).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Neutral by necessity**: she is relieved but not fool enough to thank the messenger, as showing that his consideration mattered would get her punished (Book 9, Ch55 L34).
- **Self-possessed**: "Benella," she corrected with an annoyed shake of her head (Book 9, Ch46 L40).
- **Uncertain under scrutiny**: she is unsure why Kaal is interested in her thought process (Book 9, Ch56 L22).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Not an essence user in the lines read; she is Zolit's aide, silent until the priest leaves (Book 9, Ch47 L49).

### 5. Background

- Her master is Fal Vin Garath (Book 9, Ch56 L5); he scolds her and demands she explain the servant races' different way (Book 9, Ch52 L20-29).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 9**: offers Jason fight outfits (Book 9, Ch46 L44-51); Zolit's quiet aide at the fights (Book 9, Ch47 L25, L49); has "one chance to prove herself still valuable" by presenting Jason as a threat (Book 9, Ch56 L4); presents to Voice Kaal (Book 9, Ch56 L20); Kaal probes her emotions and deduces more (Book 9, Ch56 L22-26).

### 7. Relationships

- **Fal Vin Garath**: her abusive master (Book 9, Ch56 L5).
- **Jes Fin Kaal**: the Voice she presents to (Book 9, Ch56 L20).
- **Zolit**: the fighter she aids (Book 9, Ch46 L51).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Careful, watchful and afraid; she hides her thoughts behind servant neutrality. Cue: correcting a mispronounced name.

## Beth Cavendish

Beth Cavendish is a bronze-rank Greenstone adventurer and water healer who leads a team in the Ustei campaign and the City of Fallen Echoes. Competent and brisk, she gives orders without ceremony.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read.

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Brisk**: "We're going to move on the sand barge" (Book 1, Ch89 L77).
- **Encouraging**: "Welcome to the path to bronze rank" (Book 1, Ch90 L61).

- Introduces herself to Jason at the Adventure Society as Beth Cavendish, meeting her cousin Mose (Book 1, Ch83 L23-43).

### 7. Relationships

- **Jason**: junior ally (Book 1, Ch90 L61).

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Competent and brisk; a clear team leader who gives orders without ceremony.

## Callum Morse

### 1. Summary

A gold-rank stealth assassin, former teammate of Emir and of Rufus's parents, whose long hunt for Sophie's mother Melody Jain drives his later scenes.

### 2. Facts

- Introduced by Emir as a gold-ranker who, if he does not want to be seen, cannot be seen by anyone short of diamond rank (Book 2, Ch34 L91).
- Described as "the gold-ranked assassination specialist" and the group's best scout (Book 2, Ch76 L7); his attacks on a gold-rank priest land strongly but do not kill at once (Book 2, Ch76 L41).
- He, Gabriel and Arabelle Remore have been companions for decades (Book 2, Ch76 L40). Emir, Arabelle, Gabriel and Callum were an old team (Book 3, Ch24 L35; Book 8, Ch27 L26).
- An avid monster-hunter, he is one of the few gold-rankers who obsessively raise their strength (Book 6, Ch47 L495).
- He stops Sophie and Humphrey from running off after the Order of the Reaper: "I'm stopping you until you are ready. Hate me if you like" (Book 6, Ch47 L581), and explains the order split from the cult and became self-serving assassins (Book 6, Ch47 L592).
- His fixation on Melody Jain, Sophie's mother, is because he is connected to her: she kept him as her only real connection to the order (Book 9, Ch27 L63); he lost track of her and has searched ever since (Book 9, Ch27 L91).

### 3. Timeline of involvement

- Book 2: scouts for the strike on the Builder cult's island (Book 2, Ch76 L7-46).
- Book 3: reports on the cultists' escape and the Purity evidence (Book 3, Ch1 L77-82).
- Book 6: stops Sophie and Humphrey in Kurdansk (Book 6, Ch47 L521-592).

### 4. Roleplay notes

Quiet, patient and unfazed; he lets a hard decision stand and speaks plainly. "Sensible," Callum agrees (Book 2, Ch36 L79). His grief and fixation on Melody are the one place his calm slips.

---

## Cheryl Asano

### 1. Summary

Cheryl Asano is Jason's mother, from a large Australian farming and mining family. She is the one in the family fascinated with Japan, a strong-willed realtor who shows up in Book 4 stunned at her dead son's return.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read.

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Strong-willed**: she flashes "a well-recognised look of dissatisfaction" at Jason's poke but calms herself (Book 4, Ch44 L56).
- **Stern**: Jason jokes "Mum is quite stern" (Book 4, Ch70 L86).
- **Grieving**: she looks at Jason "like she'd seen a ghost" (Book 4, Ch31 L6) and hugs him tightly once she steels her nerve (Book 4, Ch35 L84).
- **Overworked**: she hasn't been into the office in two days, unlike her (Book 4, Ch35 L52).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- She is "the pre-eminent upscale realtor in the Greater Casselton area" (Book 4, Ch24 L42).
- She is non-essence in the lines read; portal travel sickens her (Book 4, Ch44 L59).

### 5. Background

- Her family are "miners and farmers, tracing their bloodline back through bushrangers, convicts, and indigenous Australians"; she was the one fascinated with Japan (Book 1, Ch1 L88).
- Her brother is Robert (Book 4, Ch31 L3); her mother makes a miraculous recovery (Book 4, Ch35 L52).
- Jason is told his parents divorced a year ago and that his mother moved to Castle Reach (Book 4, Ch4 L105).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1-2**: Jason describes his mother's side as rural (Book 2, Ch20 L18).
- **Book 4**: the hospital waiting room (Book 4, Ch31 L3-6); the hug (Book 4, Ch35 L84); the houseboat portal (Book 4, Ch44 L54-59); the family meeting (Book 4, Ch45 L73-76).

### 7. Relationships

- **Jason**: son (Book 4, Ch35 L84).
- **Erika and Kaito**: other children (Book 4, Ch35 L53).
- **Amy**: puts a restraining hand on Cheryl's arm (Book 4, Ch45 L76).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Strong-willed and protective; her grief at Jason's disappearance colours her early scenes. Cue: the dissatisfied look she can't hide.

---

## Clarissa Ventress

### 1. Summary

Clarissa Ventress is one of the crime lords of Old City and holds Sophie's indenture. She uses Sophie to provoke the rash Cole Silva and learns what Lucian Lamprey wants in order to control him.

### 2. Appearance

- Dark cascading hair and a walk "so sultry she was almost swerving"; she looks only a few years older than the two women with her (Book 1, Ch43 L29).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Calculating**: "The key to controlling a person was finding what they wanted" (Book 1, Ch52 L57).
- **Amused**: she is "happily imagining the look on Silva's face" as she watches Sophie's match (Book 1, Ch50 L93).
- **Irritated by independence**: she is less happy with Sophie's friend Belinda (Book 1, Ch50 L93).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Rank is not stated in the lines read; she travels with a huge leonid bodyguard (Book 1, Ch43 L29).

### 5. Background

- Lives in a sprawling manor in Old City's canal district, which has its own internal city wall (Book 1, Ch53 L70).
- Her arrangement with Sophie: Sophie would help her provoke Silva into the kind of rash action his father would have prevented (Book 1, Ch50 L96).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: enters the chamber with Sophie and Belinda (Book 1, Ch43 L29); watches Sophie's match (Book 1, Ch50 L92-96); learns Lucian's wants (Book 1, Ch52 L57); summons Sophie and Belinda to her manor (Book 1, Ch53 L70).

### 7. Relationships

- **Sophie**: indentured servant (Book 1, Ch50 L96).
- **Cole Silva**: rival, newest of the Big Three (Book 1, Ch50 L92).
- **Lucian Lamprey**: someone she plans to control (Book 1, Ch52 L57).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Cool, amused and calculating. Cue: "dear Sophie."

---

## Clive Standish

### 1. Summary

Clive Standish is a tall, lanky Magic Society researcher in Greenstone, raised on an eel farm, who becomes Jason's team's ritualist and astral-magic scholar. He explains the cosmos to Jason as a bowl of dumpling soup, identifies the Reaper-trial astral space as a Builder vessel, and builds the open-access Magical Research Association.

### 2. Appearance

"Rather tall, but his narrow frame and hunched posture made him seem lanky and awkward", wearing voluminous scholarly robes that "dangled off him like they'd been hung out to dry" (Book 1, Ch42 L44).

### 3. Personality and voice

- He says he venerates, not worships, the Celestial Book: "There's a difference" (Book 1, Ch85 L51); an archbishop asks "You admit to being a heretic?" and Clive answers that he "could be considered a heretic" (Book 2, Ch21 L94-98).
- He jokes about his background: "I'm the son of eel farmers" (Book 2, Ch7 L62). A stranger accuses him of sleeping with a wife he says he does not have, which becomes a running gag (Book 2, Ch32 L106).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Essences**: Magic, Rune, Balance and the Karmic confluence (Book 2, Ch28 L56: "Clive has the magic, rune, balance and karmic essences"). A rune essence came to him as "an epic four-star essence" (Book 2, Ch9 L21).
- **Abilities**: Wrath of the Magister, a rune circle that triggers effects when attacked, the damage-reflecting buff Mantle of Retribution and the zone ability Eye of Karma (Book 2, Ch30 L53); Mana Equilibrium (Book 1, Ch103 L44).
- **Racial gift**: a blessing from the Celestial Book changed his human Special Attack Affinity into a spell affinity (Book 1, Ch85 L22-26).
- **Familiar**: Onslow (see `Familiars/Onslow.md`), a rune tortoise who sits at the prow of the airboat, carries Clive, and becomes a runic tattoo on his torso when called back (Book 1, Ch86 L115-116; Book 2, Ch22 L127-129).
- **Ritual and astral magic**: he leads the team's rituals and bridge research.

### 5. Background

Grew up on an eel farm in the delta near Greenstone (Book 1, Ch68 L92-94; wiki); recruited to the Magic Society by the Greenstone branch's then-director (wiki). His family sheltered in the Geller estate during the surge before last (Book 2, Ch18 L25-27).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: introduced as the Magic Society official; explains the deep astral and the dumpling-soup cosmology (chapter 69).
- **Book 3**: identifies the vessel nature of the Reaper-trial astral space (Book 3, Ch37 L13) and plans the inversion.
- **Book 6**: held at the Magic Society's Rakesh campus; escapes with Belinda's help.

### 7. Relationships

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

The MRA's growth and Clive's part in the bridge are level 2-3.

### 9. Roleplay notes

Speak in explanatory bursts with tangents and apologies; get animated about theory and hungry mid-lecture.

---

## Cole Silva

### 1. Summary

Cole Silva is the newest of Old City's Big Three crime lords, who inherits his father's operation and, with Lucian Lamprey, orchestrates Jason's kidnapping and star seed implantation. Arrested in Book 3, his organisation falls into chaos.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read.

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Rash**: Clarissa plans to provoke him "into the kind of rash action that his father always kept him from making" (Book 1, Ch50 L96).
- **Predatory**: his father's protection of Sophie is gone and he watches her match from a viewing box (Book 1, Ch50 L92).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

Not given in the lines read.

### 5. Background

- His father had a small operation catering to nobles with low tastes, with the money and connections to arrange expensive cleansings (Book 3, Ch18 L128).
- The father died in his sleep "a couple of months ago"; the son stepped up (Book 1, Ch51 L37).
- Cole's father killed a man who tried to rob him (Book 2, Ch9 L60).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: watches Sophie's match (Book 1, Ch50 L92).
- **Book 2**: Jason is told he put himself "in the path of Cole Silva and Lucian Lamprey" (Book 2, Ch35 L97).
- **Book 3**: the elf Palpatine-to-Vader joke (Book 3, Ch17 L56-60); held in the prison tower with Lamprey (Book 3, Ch24 L48); his organisation fights over succession after his arrest (Book 3, Ch25 L3).
- **Book 9**: Jason's kidnapping and star seed implantation are traced to him and Lamprey (Book 9, Ch2 L84).

### 7. Relationships

- **Clarissa Ventress**: rival crime lord (Book 1, Ch50 L96).
- **Lucian Lamprey**: partner in the kidnapping (Book 9, Ch2 L84).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Predatory and entitled. Cue: the rash move his father always prevented.

---

## Cressida Vane

### 1. Summary

Cressida Vane is the matriarch of the blood-cult family that holds Jason and his friends in Book 1. Beautiful, haughty and weary of her family's ambitions, she insists they are "a nice, traditional blood cult family" and she is killed in the confrontation.

### 2. Appearance

- A young-looking woman in her early thirties with olive skin and dark hair, "no older than her son Landemere" (Book 1, Ch11 L65). Standing next to the ordinary-looking high priest Darryl, she is "the beautiful Cressida Vane" (Book 1, Ch14 L17).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Haughty**: "I am Lady Cressida Vane" (Book 1, Ch11 L80); Jason mocks her high-priestess name (Book 1, Ch11 L81-83).
- **Traditional**: "This is my manor, my family, and we do things the traditional way" (Book 1, Ch9 L54).
- **Dismissive of her husband**: "I don't care what my stupid husband thinks; he married into the family" (Book 1, Ch9 L54).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Leader of the blood cult family's rituals (Book 1, Ch9 L47-54).
- Complained that astral beings were "the death" of her son (Book 1, Ch9 L47-50).

### 5. Background

- Mother of Landemere Vane (Book 1, Ch11 L65); she asks what Landemere was trying to summon (Book 1, Ch9 L47).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: the basement scene (Book 1, Ch9 L47-54; Book 1, Ch11 L65-83); her body lands on the chamber floor, not the pool (Book 1, Ch13 L55); Jason reflects on her (Book 1, Ch14 L17).
- **Book 2**: Jason asks Clive what she said about her son (Book 2, Ch37 L78).

### 7. Relationships

- **Landemere**: son (Book 1, Ch11 L65).
- **Darryl**: the high priest beside her (Book 1, Ch14 L17).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Her body ends up on the stone floor at the edge of the chamber rather than in the pool (Book 1, Ch13 L55); Jason later alludes to her head being "smashed open" (Book 1, Ch97 L122).

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Haughty, weary of her family's ambitions. Cue: "A nice, traditional blood cult family."

---

## Danielle Geller

### 1. Summary

Danielle Geller is Humphrey's mother and the head of the Geller family's household in Greenstone. She is a silver-ranker at the start of the series who looks much younger than she is, later a gold-ranker with dimension and time powers, and Jason's political adviser on Earth.

### 2. Appearance

Silver-rank and ageless-looking: she seems about thirty and is fifty in fact (notes, Book 1); Jason sees the same agelessness in Emir and Thalia Mercer (Book 1, Ch108 L35-37). In Book 1 she "may have looked too young to be Humphrey's mother" (Book 1, Ch47 L87-89). In Book 7 she is gold-rank with dark olive skin and black hair (Book 7, Ch43 L61).

### 3. Personality and voice

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Estate and training**: the Geller estate's mirage chamber can run at bronze rank only "for limited periods" because Greenstone's magic is low, and at iron rank Danielle offers it to Rufus (Book 1, Ch37 L43-55); a family member from the generation that first settled the region may still be alive (Book 1, Ch37 L21). The diamond-rank ancestor in the earlier notes was not found.
- **Rank**: silver in Book 1, gold by Book 7.

### 5. Background

Mother of Humphrey and wife of Keith Geller (Book 2, Ch62 L24). The Geller family's wall is older than the Old City (Book 1, Ch37 L21).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: Gabriel Remore asks about "this outworlder of yours" in a call (Book 1, Ch49 L42); Danielle leads the search pattern on the Greenstone expedition into the desert astral space (Book 1, Ch104 L60-61), where Henry Geller dies (Book 1, Ch110 L51).
- **Book 3**: confronts Lamprey with Humphrey.
- **Book 7**: takes the 119 outworlders (Book 7, Ch36 L86) to the Geller estate in Cyrion (Book 7, Ch43 L61-62).

### 7. Relationships

Humphrey (son), Keith (husband), Rick and the Geller family, Jason (ally), Elspeth Arella.

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Her gold rank and the Earth adviser role are levels 2-3.

### 9. Roleplay notes

Crisp, strategic, protective of her family, fond of aphorisms about power.

---

## Dawn

### 1. Summary

Dawn is the World-Phoenix's First Servant, the head of its cult and its primary vessel (Book 3, Ch69 L1036-1040 describes the post; the notes identify Dawn as holder of it, notes, Book 4). She guides Jason through Books 4-8 as a mentor and agent: she paints, takes avatar bodies, reveals the invasion plan, negotiates with the Builder and brings Jason's friends to Rimaros.

### 2. Appearance

Her true body is a celestine with ruby eyes and hair (Book 7, Ch54 L87). She appears in many avatars; Jason kills and loots one (Book 8, Ch4 L116: "I killed and looted Dawn"; the replacement avatar is described in Book 5, Ch20 L22). In Book 7 her vessel is a cottage inside a floating bubble that carries her (Book 7, Ch8 L3); in Book 9 a recording crystal projects her face over a projector (Book 9, Ch6 L17).

### 3. Personality and voice

Mentoring and slightly arch. When Jason has just punched her avatar she says "You realise that you could have asked that instead of punching my nose through my brain" and teases that she is "a little more mature than someone who just turned twenty-six" (Book 5, Ch20 L21-24).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Role**: First Servant of the World-Phoenix, head of its cult and the primary vessel of the great astral being (Book 3, Ch69 L1036-1040). Diamond-rank vessels of great astral beings can withstand the strain and keep their selves, and former vessels end up as peak existences (Book 3, Ch69 L1035-1040).
- **One intervention**: the Hierophant is allowed to intervene in Pallimustus a single time (Soramir to Jason, Book 7, Ch60 L27); in Book 7 she says she will use one full-power intervention (Book 7, Ch60 L27-30), and she destroys a Builder rolling city with a phoenix (notes, Book 7).
- **Art**: the paintings by "Dawn" that Jason buys show two planets with four pillars and "The Invasion of Pallimustus" (Book 4, Ch23 L31-39).
- **Home**: Interstice, the city-universe at the cosmos's centre (Book 5, Ch20 L65-67).

### 5. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 4**: her paintings; Dawn reveals the Earth-Pallimustus magic link and the Builder/Purity invasion plan to Jason.
- **Book 5**: arrives at Asano Village in a new avatar; explains the danger of transformation zones as dimensional ulceration; tells him the Reaper "was the one who offered" Farrah's return (Book 5, Ch20 L40-41).
- **Book 6**: arrives in her true body to negotiate with the Builder after Shako kills Jason (Book 6, Ch18 L3-9).
- **Book 7**: her full-power intervention and the arrival of Humphrey's team in Rimaros.

### 6. Relationships

### 7. Secrets and spoiler notes

Dawn's identity as First Servant and her use of avatars are level 2; her exceeding her mandate is level 3.

### 8. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Formal, with a teasing edge. Cue: "You realise that you could have asked that instead of punching my nose through my brain" (Book 5, Ch20 L21). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

Dry, informative, protective of the cosmic balance, willing to joke about her own avatars.

---

## Dustin Kettering

### 1. Summary

An ice-armoured defender from the Kettering family who starts as one of Thadwick Mercer's offsiders, joins Rick's team in Book 2 and later fights beside Neil in Cardinas.

### 2. Facts

- One of Thadwick Mercer's two offsiders with Neil: he protects the group with his powers while Neil heals them (Book 1, Ch110 L17).
- The Davone and Kettering families have close ties to the Mercers, which is how the boys came to Thadwick's side (Book 2, Ch27 L41). He and Neil are relieved to be free, having been known as Thadwick's flunkeys (Book 2, Ch27 L57).
- A local who fills Jonah Geller's defender role on Rick's team (Book 2, Ch29 L8): "a classic defender, not very mobile but very hard to go around or through" (Book 2, Ch29 L9), clad in armour forged entirely of ice (Book 2, Ch29 L59, L69).
- His cousin Hudson Kettering is his counterpart on Beth Cavendish's team (Book 2, Ch29 L9; Book 2, Ch15 L44).
- In Book 7 he is a friend of Neil's from their time on Thadwick's team (Book 7, Ch59 L65); by Book 9 he is the only non-local on Rick's team, picked up after Jonah's death (Book 9, Ch104 L54).

### 3. Timeline of involvement

- Book 1-2: Thadwick's team, then Rick's (Book 1, Ch110 L17; Book 2, Ch29 L8).
- Book 7: arrives with Rick's group in Rimaros (Book 7, Ch59 L65).

### 4. Roleplay notes

---

## Emi Evans-Asano

### 1. Summary

### 2. Facts

**Name and family**
- The System names her as the contact "Emi Evans-Asano" when Jason returns, and he senses her aura with Erika's and Ian's (Book 4, Ch3 L124, L132).
- Jason's remark on her at twelve is "twelve going on forty" (Book 4, Ch29 L103). Erika says Emi took his death hard and that Erika's own conspiracy-theory phase did not help (Book 4, Ch27 L171).

**Book 4: Jason's return**
- At twelve she stands up to a group of boys picking on another student at Jason's old school, which Jason watches through Shade (Book 4, Ch25 L60-66).
- Jason promises Erika he will tell her everything (Book 4, Ch28 L126). Emi guesses something is wrong from how her parents are acting, throws herself into his arms when he appears, and her parents nod their agreement to let her go with him (Book 4, Ch28 L135-139, L154). He takes her for a drive, and she asks whether he is "the Starlight Angel" (Book 4, Ch29 L43). At a coastal lookout he asks "Do you believe in magic, Emi?", the car dissolves into his shadow, and she says "You're him" (Book 4, Ch29 L45-54).
- He takes her through a portal arch, her first dimensional translocation, and she endures it better than expected (Book 4, Ch29 L69-73).
- She asks Jason to help Nanna, who has Alzheimer's, and he says it looks like it worked (Book 4, Ch31 L25).
- She enjoys "having this big secret" (Book 4, Ch32 L4), is introduced to Shade as "Miss Emi" (Book 4, Ch32 L19), and has seen Jason's recordings (Book 4, Ch34 L14; Book 4, Ch57 L34-36).
- She vanishes her scooter into Shade's storage in front of two police officers (Book 4, Ch50 L53-62), and leads Farrah by the hand into the bar lounge when Farrah first arrives (Book 4, Ch57 L33-39).
- She asks to be Jason's assistant "when I get magic powers" (Book 4, Ch58 L75).
- Jason plans to take her out of school for intensive training (Book 4, Ch73 L77). At his farewell barbecue she is angry that he is leaving again, then runs back through the portal to hug him (Book 4, Ch74 L32, L145).

**Book 5: the compound and the choice**
- She sends recordings to and from Jason through Shade and is "especially excited" about her ritual magic lessons with Farrah, which take the sting out of not being old enough for essences (Book 5, Ch2 L116; Book 5, Ch3 L127).
- She shows Jason round the new family compound, pointing out the training facilities and the Network office (Book 5, Ch5 L59-68).
- She tells reporter Dennier his story was "an exercise in buffoonery" (Book 5, Ch24 L4). Erika sends her to Coffs Harbour to make sure no monster waves arrive, and Jason jokes about her "dimensional membrane protrusion-precursor analysis" (Book 5, Ch24 L12-16).
- Jason wants to take her with him on more of his work so she is prepared for the other world. Erika is unwilling to put her thirteen-year-old daughter near that (Book 5, Ch31 L134-135).
- She flies with the family to Japan and goes down the sea-cave shaft with Jason (Book 5, Ch34 L3; Book 5, Ch40 L3-18).
- The essences Jason picked for her were chosen to keep her safe, and she would not be ready for essences for about a year (Book 5, Ch43 L85-90).
- After the death of her uncle Kaito ("Uncle Kai", Book 5, Ch51 L33), she tells Jason she does not want to kill things or be an adventurer, and he says he is not disappointed (Book 5, Ch53 L80-85). She adds that he took her out of school for training (Book 5, Ch53 L86).
- She chooses a specialty, formation interactivity (the interference between nearby magical formations), though Farrah advises her to see the breadth of options first (Book 5, Ch54 L8, L13).
- In Book 6 she tells Dawn that almost everyone at the cloud palace is an Asano and Dawn should call him Jason (Book 6, Ch4 L65). Jason works at being "the cool uncle", which has not restored their old closeness, but she is less ill at ease (Book 6, Ch23 L31).

**Powers and skills**

### 3. Roleplay notes
