## Emir Bahadir

### 1. Summary

Emir Bahadir is a gold-rank treasure hunter, patron of the Reaper contest and owner of a cloud ship and palace that becomes Jason's team's base. Elegant, ageless and wanderlustful, he has hunted the remnants of the Order of the Reaper for years, uses his wealth and staff for large schemes, and marries his chief of staff, Constance.

### 2. Appearance

- He "didn't look to be more than thirty years old", with an "agelessness" to him, and dresses well, largely thanks to Constance (Book 1, Ch108 L35; Book 3, Ch2 L65).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Likes grand entrances**: "I'm hosting a grand event. It requires grandeur" (Book 2, Ch49 L67). He announces a competition and a grand-prize cloud palace in a speech that throws the crowd into turmoil (Book 2, Ch40 L65).
- **Warm and wry**: he tells Rufus "Comrades behind her, enemies in front of her and friends beside her" of Farrah's apparent death (Book 1, Ch110 L56) and sighs "world-weary" when Jason storms off (Book 2, Ch34 L44).
- **Cautious in accusation**: "Making that kind of accusation against a church is no small matter and even I'm not completely convinced yet" (Book 2, Ch37 L95).
- **Not used to being the responsible one**: that is Constance's job, but he is the older and higher-ranked man (Book 9, Ch51 L14).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Essences**: he has "staff, might, magic and mystic" (Book 2, Ch5 L88).
- **Cloud ship and palace**: a cloud ship the size of an ocean liner that morphs into a palace (Book 1, Ch101 L103; Book 2, Ch40 L54; see Cloud_Constructs_and_Palaces.md). His preferred design is a five-towered castle (Book 9, Ch50 L27).
- **Staff and speed**: he conjures copies of his staff under every construct, smashing the iron and bronze ones and tossing the silvers (Book 1, Ch111 L76) and catches Danielle before she can lunge (Book 2, Ch19 L93).
- **Aura mastery**: he can block the senses of others, normally taught only to gold-rankers (Book 9, Ch51 L11).

### 5. Background

- **The Reaper search**: he has "studied the style more than most outsiders to the Order of the Reaper" and hypothesised that it was meant to be learned through skill books (Book 6, Ch44 L12). His patron's search for Order remnants is why Emir has hunted them for years (Book 9, Ch27 L65).
- **Old team**: he, Arabelle, Gabriel and Callum Morse were a team (Book 3, Ch24 L35; Book 2, Ch76 L3).
- **Family**: a son who died, whose wife died with him, leaving his granddaughter Ketis, raised by her grandmother (Book 2, Ch64 L151).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Books 1-3**: the team moves into his cloud palace (Book 2, Ch8 L168), and his chief of staff Constance is among the silver-rankers of the strike force on the Builder cult island (Book 2, Ch76 L3).
- **Books 6-9**: he waits for Constance to reach gold and marries her (Book 6, Ch47 L494, L539-542); Jason calls on him as an old ally and tells him of his changes (Book 9, Ch49-51).

### 7. Relationships

- **Jason**: he gives him a thick notebook of cloud flask knowledge (Book 7, Ch14 L21) and is startled by the change in him in Book 9 (Book 9, Ch51 L6-11).
- **Callum Morse**: old teammate (Book 6, Ch47 L494).
- **Sophie**: she and Emir used Sophie as bait for the order, which Callum resented (Book 9, Ch27 L81; Book 8, Ch27 L34).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

He knew of the Order of the Reaper's return long before the team (Book 9, Ch27 L65).

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Urbane, amused and generous, with a showman's love of timing; he turns serious quickly when someone is in danger. Cue: "I'm hosting a grand event. It requires grandeur."

---

## Fal Vin Garath

### 1. Summary

Fal Vin Garath is a silver-rank messenger master who is abusive but within the bounds his peers accept. He detests acting at the behest of Jes Fin Kaal and launches a soul attack on Jason in Book 9.

### 2. Appearance

- Fair skin and golden hair, a physical counterpart to the other silver messenger (Book 9, Ch56 L5-7).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Imperious**: he scowls at Benella and demands answers in a voice "like a command from the heavens" (Book 9, Ch52 L20-28).
- **Resentful of command**: he "detested being forced to act at the behest of the Voice of the Will" (Book 9, Ch90 L3) and sneers at his commander, astral king's Voice and Asano (Book 9, Ch91 L3).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Silver rank; he launches a soul attack on Jason through his connection to Shade and is surprised when Jason fends it off and retaliates (Book 9, Ch52 L36).
- He seizes Shade and squeezes while Shade drains his mana, until Shade's body is destroyed (Book 9, Ch52 L42-44).

### 5. Background

- Benella's master, "abusive but not outside the bounds of acceptability to his fellow messengers", free to treat the servant races as he pleased so long as it did not impinge on other messengers (Book 9, Ch56 L5).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 9**: scolds Benella (Book 9, Ch52 L20-28); soul attack and Shade fight (Book 9, Ch52 L36-44); sent on a mission by Kaal (Book 9, Ch90 L3; Book 9, Ch91 L3).

### 7. Relationships

- **Benella**: his servant (Book 9, Ch56 L5).
- **Jes Fin Kaal**: his commander (Book 9, Ch90 L3).
- **Hess Jor Nasala**: sits with him at Kaal's meeting (Book 9, Ch56 L40).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Imperious and quick to anger; he assumes servants exist to answer him. Cue: "like a command from the heavens."

---

## The Familiars: Shade, Colin, Gordon and Stash

Individual entries for the familiars with a personality are in the `Familiars/` folder: Shade, Colin, Gordon, Stash, Onslow and Shimmer. This file stays as the overview.

### 1. Summary

A familiar is a purely magical creature from the deep astral given a body by a summoning (Clive, Book 1, Ch69 L85-107). Jason has three, each very different, and Humphrey has one.

### 2. Shade

- **Incantation**: Jason's summoning chant begins "I call to the realm beyond cold and darkness, where death has no meaning, for life has no place. Let mine be the dark beyond darkness..." (Book 2, Ch72 L15).

### 3. Colin

- **Origin**: Colin comes from the border region between the realms of the All-Devouring Eye and Legion, Legion being the great astral being who administers the cosmic rules of life (Book 8, Ch55 L92-93).

### 4. Gordon

- **Who**: Jason's third familiar, named by him in Book 2 (Book 2, Ch75 L75-79). A disembodied dark cloak with a nebula in the shape of an eye in the chest, with blue and orange orbs "drifting around" (Book 3, Ch4 L28-32).
- **Powers**: two floating orbs fire sustained beams: an orange beam of resonating-force damage that penetrates armour and a blue beam of disruptive-force damage that works against magical protection and incorporeal enemies (Book 2, Ch75 L81-84). His orbs also deliver the Harbinger of Doom affliction (Book 5, Ch31 L187) and create shield orbs (Book 5, Ch31 L187). In Book 9 he draws a massive magical orrery (Book 9, Ch94 L24-26).
- **Origin**: Gordon comes from the realm of the All-Devouring Eye, as the messenger Carmen says in Book 8 (Book 8, Ch55 L92-93; wiki).

### 5. Stash

- **Who**: Humphrey Geller's familiar, whose full name is Velitraxistaasch; "He likes 'Stash,' though" (Book 1, Ch38 L58). A mirage dragon (Book 2, Ch20 L29) who takes small forms: a mouse in Humphrey's pocket, a bird on Jason's shoulder and a puppy (Book 1, Ch78 L84-85; Book 2, Ch62 L50).
- **Special attack**: Humphrey's racial ability evolves into Attack of the Mirage Dragon (Book 2, Ch61 L147).

### 6. Roleplay notes

- Shade: a formal, shadowy presence; Jason talks with him as an equal.
- Colin: hungry, direct.
- Gordon: a silent nebula-eyed cloak whose orbs speak for him.
- Stash: a small, shapeshifting dragon who likes biscuits.

---

## Farrah Hurin

### 1. Summary

Farrah Hurin (also spelt Huren) is a small, pale, strawberry-blonde ritual magician from Vitesse. She is one of the three adventurers Jason meets in the Vane cellar, teaches him aura control, perception and meditation, and treats him as an equal. She dies in Book 1, is brought back by the Reaper, and reappears on Earth in Book 4.

### 2. Appearance

Pretty, with strawberry-blonde hair, a button nose and light skin, "startlingly cute" and clearly unhappy when freed from her cage (Book 1, Ch12 L11). Physically small but of "prodigious strength": she hauls the standing stones into place in Book 7 (Book 7, Ch1 L16).

### 3. Personality and voice

A lecturer by habit: whenever she is not pointing out something specific, she is explaining (Book 1, Ch23 L15-17). She keeps her friends close: in Book 5, away from Gary, she says "I miss Gary" (Book 5, Ch30 L61).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Essences**: Earth, Fire and Potent, which form the Volcano confluence; she explains this herself in Book 1 (Book 1, Ch19 L30).
- **Abilities** (Book 1, Ch19 L1; Book 4, Ch41 L31): lava cannon (the Book 1 chapter title is "I Want a Lava Cannon"), a magma golem, lava streams and fire orbs, obsidian domes, shards and columns, and obsidian armour (Book 1, Ch108 L94).
- **Ritual magic**: she adjusts circles for ambient magic (Book 1, Ch23 L15-17) and builds the infrastructure of Asano Village and the Grid repairs (Book 5, Ch5 L83-89).
- **Aura teaching**: perception and memory games, meditation and aura control (Book 1, Ch40 L31-35).

### 5. Background

Daughter of Amelia and William Hurin of Vitesse; a member of the Burning Violet guild (Book 2, Ch38 L69; Book 7, Ch7 L53). Friends with Rufus and Gary since childhood.

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: rescued with Rufus and Gary from the cult; dies covering civilians in the desert astral-space ambush ("Farrah died covering those people", Book 1, Ch113 L41).
- **Books 5-6**: builds the family compound's infrastructure and the Grid repairs, helps destroy a vampire hub, leaves Earth with Jason; of her captor Adrien Barbou she says "Barbou belongs to me" (Book 6, Ch26 L9).

### 7. Relationships

Jason (an unusual, close bond), Rufus and Gary (friends), Travis Noble (partner), Emi (the child of the household; Farrah helped build the village for the family), Adrien Barbou (her captor).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Her return to life is level 2 (this project's spoiler scale; the event is in Book 4). The Defier choice is level 3.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: A lecturer who turns blunt when angry. Cues: Book 1, Ch23 L15-17; "Barbou belongs to me" (Book 6, Ch26 L9). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

She explains at length and corrects quickly; she is direct about danger and treats ignorance as something to be fixed.

---

## Gary (Gareth Xandier) and Gary Sharpton

### 1. Summary

### 2. Appearance

### 3. Personality and voice

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Summon**: a foundry golem, a roughly three-metre black-iron figure with a molten mouth that drips molten metal (Book 1, Ch17 L58-61).
- **Equipment**: conjured full-body dark-steel plate armour (Book 1, Ch16 L28); the notes add a hammer and shield and the runes.
- **Smithing**: he forges Dread Salvation (Book 1, Ch56 L46-51); he reforges it with his mentor Virid Martine (Book 6, Ch47 L270) as Hegemon's Will (Book 7, Ch2 L71-74; Book 7, Ch54 L108-120).
- **Rank**: bronze at the start; "almost silver" by Book 3 (Book 3, Ch21 L60); silver after using a core (notes, Book 6).

### 5. Background

A friend of Rufus and Farrah, and a Vitesse adventurer (Book 1, Ch30 L47). In Book 6 he meets the gold-rank smith Virid Martine, who becomes his master (Book 6, Ch47 L270-272).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: captured with Rufus and Farrah by the Vane cult; escorts Jason to Greenstone; stands before Dominion.
- **Book 6**: meets Virid Martine and begins his blacksmith's path.
- **Book 7**: reforges Dread Salvation with Virid.

### 7. Relationships

Jason (friend), Rufus and Farrah (friends), Virid Martine (master), Dominion and Hero, and the younger Gary (the successor).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

The Cup of Heroes, death and rebirth are level 3.

### 9. Roleplay notes

Short, growly sentences; patient with craft; the younger Gary should be written as a different person, a bright teenage engineer, not an echo.

---

## Greg

### 1. Summary

Greg is an old school friend of Jason's who joins the Network defectors on Kaito's helicopter crew and is killed in Book 5. His family is taken into Asano Village afterward. He is nerdy, self-deprecating and braver than he believed.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read; he drives a 2017 Ford Taurus (Book 4, Ch66 L59).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Self-deprecating**: he was "never much more than an adequate lawyer" and had been pushed out of his father's practice (Book 5, Ch58 L14).
- **Enjoys small comforts**: he holds a mug of hot chocolate on a chalet balcony (Book 5, Ch47 L37).
- **Teased by Asya**: "If all your friends are cool, what does that say about you?" (Book 4, Ch66 L66).
- **Sensitive to portals**: he fights portal sickness on Erika's floor (Book 4, Ch74 L140).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Bronze rank by Book 5; his body "could easily endure the cold" (Book 5, Ch47 L37).
- He crews Kaito's helicopter with Asya (Book 5, Ch7 L12).

### 5. Background

- School friend of Jason and Asya who roped Jason and Amy into hours-long board games (Book 4, Ch7 L42; Book 4, Ch66 L59).
- He says he had lived more in six months of magic than in six years before (Book 5, Ch58 L14).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 4**: meets Jason with Asya and Craig (Book 4, Ch66 L59-66); portal trip (Book 4, Ch74 L140).
- **Book 5**: part of the combat team core with Asya and Kaito (Book 5, Ch44 L17); dies in Berlin (Book 5, Ch51 L40); Jason speaks with his spectre in the cloud palace hall (Book 5, Ch58 L3-14); Jason bows his head before the family (Book 5, Ch56 L26).

### 7. Relationships

- **Asya**: friend and fellow crew member (Book 4, Ch66 L66).
- **Jason**: old friend (Book 5, Ch58 L3).
- **Kaito**: teammate (Book 5, Ch44 L17).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Jason's talk with his spectre follows his death (Book 5, Ch58 L3-14).

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Nerdy, self-deprecating and braver than he believed. Cue: "never much more than an adequate lawyer."

---

## Hannah and Claire Adeah

### 1. Summary

Twin elf sisters on Rick Geller's team: Hannah the archer and Claire the healer.

### 2. Facts

- The elf twins Hannah and Claire Adeah are the archer and the team healer, with Claire the most important member to protect (Book 1, Ch67 L15; Book 2, Ch29 L6-8).
- Hannah's arrows pin trap weavers to trees (Book 1, Ch67 L18); Claire cleanses afflictions and cuts a team mate from a web with her wand (Book 1, Ch66 L31; Book 1, Ch67 L71-95).
- In the Book 1 mock battle against Jason, Claire's body is left floating in the water after she focused her healing on the others, and they swear revenge (Book 1, Ch67 L126-128).
- Claire resented Jason's tactics in the mock battle the most (Book 2, Ch4 L10).

### 3. Roleplay notes

## Hester Maharala

### 1. Summary

Hester Maharala is Emir's portal user and head of logistics, born in Jayapura in Pranay. Warm, travelled and practical, she gets people where they need to be and keeps Emir's operations running.

### 2. Appearance

- The Asiatic-looking person Jason sees first in this world, closer to South Asian than his own Japanese features (Book 2, Ch34 L55).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Warm and practical**: Jason compares her portal range to his own shadow portals (Book 3, Ch8 L25).
- **Respected**: "Hester was one of Emir's most important staff members" (Book 2, Ch69 L64).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Portal user: she traces a circle in the air to open a portal (Book 1, Ch109 L6); she can only open portals to places she has visited and the time before her next use depends on range (Book 1, Ch109 L7-9).
- She portals Clive back to the city (Book 3, Ch30 L21).

### 5. Background

- Born in Jayapura, a port city of Pranay (Book 2, Ch69 L65-66).
- Has a clifftop house with grotto rooms and a swim-access bar (Book 2, Ch69 L69-72).
- A shopkeeper calls her "Hester Maharala... The lady with the house on the hill" (Book 2, Ch70 L70).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: opens the portal for the Boko rescue at Emir's direction (Book 1, Ch109 L6).
- **Book 2**: head of logistics (Book 2, Ch69 L64).
- **Book 3**: Emir plans to ask her to portal Callum back (Book 3, Ch69 L186).
- **Book 6**: Gary imagines Hester arriving to portal him to a memorial service (Book 6, Ch47 L172).

### 7. Relationships

- **Emir**: employer (Book 2, Ch69 L64).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Warm, travelled and practical.

---

## Hudson Kettering

### 1. Summary

Hudson Kettering is the huge earth-essence front-liner of Beth Cavendish's team and cousin of Dustin Kettering. He is a stone-shielding bruiser who meets Jason on the sand barge assault, fights Humphrey in a Geller-versus-Cavendish contest, and joins the Reaper trial expedition.

### 2. Facts

- **Size**: "One of the members of Beth's team was a huge human named Hudson. He looked like Humphrey with twenty percent bonus person, to the point of being almost as large as Gary" (Book 1, Ch89 L54).
- **First meeting**: he tells Jason "You don't seem evil" (Book 1, Ch89 L60).
- **Team role**: Beth puts him "front and centre" in the assault on the sand barge (Book 1, Ch89 L77), and a large flat block of sandstone rises under the team to carry them forward like a raft (Book 1, Ch89 L89).
- **In the fight**: a bronze-rank captain's charge shatters his stone shield and sends him staggering (Book 1, Ch90 L11); he ploughs up the ramp "crashing into the people inside and making space for the others" (Book 1, Ch90 L16). Beth tells him "No slacking" (Book 1, Ch90 L49).
- **Family**: he is cousin to Dustin, "the only one I can think of who could stack up to Jonah" among defenders, and cannot be pried from Beth's team (Book 2, Ch15 L44). Dustin sits beside him with their teams around them (Book 2, Ch28 L13).
- **Humphrey's offer**: Jason brings Humphrey to talk to Hudson about his cousin joining a Geller team; Hudson says a Geller name is good "but so was Mercer", and the family wants real adventurers for Dustin after Thadwick (Book 2, Ch15 L56-59).
- **Memorial**: his team attends the memorial because "Her standing strong is the reason my team all got out alive" (Book 2, Ch15 L63).
- **Powers**: his body takes on the colour of the desert stone, flesh transmuting into living rock (Book 2, Ch29 L48), and he conjures a huge stone shield at the front of a stone-raft run (Book 2, Ch29 L65). Two wall abilities spend him: "That's both my wall abilities. I won't have them again for a while" (Book 2, Ch29 L54).
- **Elemental pair**: he shields his team with earth powers while Dustin is clad in ice armour (Book 2, Ch29 L59); Humphrey notes that Rick's team has Kettering where "we have Hudson" (Book 2, Ch29 L18).
- **Down**: Humphrey smashes him after a switch-teleport with Beth leaves him prone (Book 2, Ch30 L4), and Hudson is soon "scrambling on the ground and conjuring shield after shield for Humphrey to smash through" (Book 2, Ch30 L15).
- **Reaper trial**: in the astral city he stays quiet while Beth plans; his earth powers are the team's most prominent (Book 2, Ch54 L85). He receives a vine-looped wrist band that adds effects to his earth conjuration (Book 2, Ch56 L41). He appears in the party contact list (Book 2, Ch54 L18).

### 3. Roleplay notes

Large, quiet and steady: he lets Beth talk, gives his team a wall to stand behind and says little. He is protective of his cousin.

---

## Humphrey Geller

### 1. Summary

Humphrey Francis Eugene Geller is the leader of Team Biscuit, a tall, broad-shouldered, handsome young man from a family that has been adventuring for sixteen generations. He begins the series a shy teenager and becomes a steady leader and one of the team's strongest fighters.

### 2. Appearance

Tall, broad-shouldered and handsome; sixteen or seventeen in Book 1 (Book 1, Ch38 L114; Book 1, Ch66 L58). Rick Geller, a family member, shares his tall, broad-shouldered build (Book 1, Ch66 L58-62).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Formal in a crisis**: in Book 3, facing the Builder's forces, he announces "My name is Humphrey Francis Eugene Geller. My family have been adventurers for sixteen generations. We aren't alchemists or weaponsmiths on the side... For hundreds of years we have done one thing, and one thing only: protect our world from people like you" (Book 3, Ch68 L61).
- **Shy in personal matters**: when Jason asks in front of his mother whether he has spoken to Gabrielle, he turns white and shakes his head (Book 1, Ch47 L88-89).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Racial gift**: his human gift Special Attack Affinity evolves into Attack of the Mirage Dragon (an illusory double of his attacks, Book 2, Ch61 L147) and later into Hero's Drive, with the racial gift Hero's Sacrifice (Book 2, Ch61 L147).
- **Familiar**: Stash (Velitraxistaasch), a bonded baby mirage dragon (Book 1, Ch38 L56-63); see `Familiars/Stash.md`.

### 5. Background

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: introduced alongside Stash; Jason nudges him toward Gabrielle Pellin; confronts Jason about the arena ambush.
- **Book 2**: breaks up with Gabrielle, who is dogmatic about Knowledge (Book 2, Ch28 L25-30); Humphrey says "I care for Gabrielle and I've enjoyed our time together. That time is just coming to an end" (Book 2, Ch28 L32).
- **Book 3**: the speech to the Builder's forces.
- **Book 9**: Hero's Drive and Hero's Sacrifice appear.

### 7. Relationships

Danielle Geller (mother), Sophie Wexler (fiancée), Jason (friend), Stash, Rufus (mentor), Gabrielle Pellin (a first romance, ended in Book 2), Rick and Phoebe Geller (family).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

His race-gift evolution is level 2. His engagement is level 3.

### 9. Roleplay notes

Formal when serious, shy in personal matters, steadfast in a fight.

---

## Jason Asano

### 1. Summary

### 2. Appearance

- **On arrival**: he wakes bald and naked in a hedge maze (Book 1, Ch1 L8-10). Seeing himself later, he notes his Japanese facial features, inherited from his father, look even sharper than usual, his face is gaunt, and his bald head and absent eyebrows make him look "manic and unhinged" (Book 1, Ch8 L37). His skin is pale and taut.
- **Why**: his body ceased to exist in the deep astral; his soul, being magical, was given a new body in the way a summoned familiar is (Clive, Book 1, Ch69 L85-107).
- **Clothing**: from Book 3 he buys a Vitesse-style wardrobe from the tailor Gilbert Bertinelli (Book 3, Ch2 L59-64).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Self-deprecation**: "I'm only twenty-three but I've been pretty efficient about squandering my opportunities" (Book 1, Ch25 L43). He does not celebrate his birthday, which falls on 1 April: "It's not a celebratory time. I never liked my birthday anyway" (Book 5, Ch20 L25).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Essences**: Dark, Blood, Sin and the Doom confluence. His attributes are bound as Power (Blood), Speed (Dark), Spirit (Doom) and Recovery (the fourth essence) (Book 1, Ch99 L67-70).
- **Weapons**: a sword, Dread Salvation, an iron-rank growth item of legendary rarity made by Gary (Book 1, Ch56 L49-52), later reforged by Gary with his mentor Virid and with Jason's soul-bond guiding the work (Book 7, Ch2 L73-74), becoming the sword [Hegemon's Will] with the scabbard Hegemon's Dominion (Book 7, Ch54 L108-120); Virid's surname Martine is Book 6, Ch47 L270. Way of the Reaper martial arts (Book 1, Ch52 L7-11). The set Regalia of the Dark Hegemon includes the Amulet of the Dark Guardian (Book 6, Ch4 L29-36).
- **Abilities seen in the text**: Path of Shadows (a shadow-to-shadow teleport at iron, Book 1, Ch22 L120; at bronze a conjured shadow gate on a regional scale to a place already visited, Book 3, Ch29 L22-35; "a range of roughly two hundred kilometres" when he plans to reach Sydney from Casselton, Book 4, Ch1 L67), Midnight Eyes (awakened Book 1, Ch8 L35), Castigate (Book 1, Ch87 L85) and Verdict (Book 1, Ch99 L24), Punition (Book 1, Ch78 L54), Feast of Absolution (Book 1, Ch22 L84) and Gordon's Harbinger of Doom (Book 5, Ch31 L187).
- **Outworlder racial abilities** (Book 1, Ch1 L76-81): Interface, Quest System, Inventory, Map, Astral Affinity and Mysterious Stranger. Mysterious Stranger gives language adaptation, absorption of essences, awakening stones and skill books, and immunity to identification and tracking effects. Astral Affinity increases resistance to dimension effects and astral forces and increases the effect of dimension abilities and transcendent damage.
- **Familiars**: Shade, Colin and Gordon (see `Familiars/Shade.md`, `Familiars/Colin.md` and `Familiars/Gordon.md`, and the hub `Familiars.md`).

### 5. Background

- **Home**: Casselton Beach on the Mid North Coast of New South Wales (Book 4, Ch3 L100). His father Ken is a landscape architect (Book 2, Ch11 L41); his mother Cheryl comes from a large family of mostly rural people (Book 2, Ch20 L18); his older sister Erika and her husband Ian live nearby with their daughter Emi; his brother Kaito and Kaito's wife Amy live next door to his parents' house (notes, Book 4). His uncles are Hiro, the black sheep with minor involvement in organised crime, and Shiro, the favourite (Book 4, Ch1 L66-67).
- **Before Pallimustus**: "a brief stint at university" studying political science (Book 1, Ch7 L26), then Melbourne "the last couple of years" (Book 1, Ch36 L87), seeing little of his family apart from his sister's household (Book 1, Ch52 says only that he had little contact with his family after they fell out), where he is "the cheapest childcare in town" (Book 1, Ch52 L27). He worked as an assistant manager at a retail bulk office supplier (Book 2, Ch14 L35).
- **Birthday**: 1 April (Book 5, Ch20 L24-26).
- **Arrival**: pulled from his bed in late November by Landemere Vane's failed summoning (Book 4, Ch3 L112).

### 6. Timeline of involvement (summary)

- **Books 1-3 (levels 0-1)**: arrives in Greenstone, joins the Adventure Society, forms the team that becomes Team Biscuit, gains Shade, Colin and Gordon, reaches bronze, and dies in the Builder's tower.
- **Books 4-6 (level 2)**: wakes in the old Casselton hospital in early June 2019, reveals magic to his family, rescues Farrah, ranks to silver after the Makassar events, claims transformation zones, is killed by Shako and leaves Earth in autumn 2021.
- **Books 7-9 (level 2)**: returns to Pallimustus as the great surge begins; the One Day War; becomes an astral king (Book 8, Ch65 L85-93); the Battle of Yaresh; takes in messengers.

See the Master Timeline for dates.

### 7. Relationships

- **Mentors and early friends**: Rufus, Gary and Farrah; Rufus runs his field assessment (Book 1, Ch38 L45) and guides his first meditation (Book 1, Ch31 L120-133); Farrah trains his perception and aura projection with memory games and other exercises (Book 1, Ch40 L31, L50-52; Book 1, Ch41 L35).
- **Team Biscuit**: Humphrey, Sophie, Belinda, Clive and Neil.
- **Family**: Emi, Kaito (dies in Book 5), Amy, Erika.
- **Familiars**: Shade (a shadow, butler and driver), Colin (an apocalypse leech swarm) and Gordon (an Avatar of Doom); full entries in `Familiars/`.
- **Others**: Dawn, Zara, Anna Tilden, Boris, Knowledge, the Builder.

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Dry, sarcastic and quick with modern references; polite to rank only when forced. Cue: "Do I have to pay a wizard to identify items?" (Book 1, Ch1 L108). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

- Keep the humour first and the substance second; pop-culture references and self-mockery are constant.
- He speaks at length when explaining, then plainly when serious.
- Leave out any claims about his inner moral life that the text does not show; the books show his actions and jokes.

---

## Jeni Kavaloa

### 1. Summary

Jeni Kavaloa is the gold-rank leader of the mixed-force surge expedition in Book 7. She is irritated by the roster, suspects Vesper Rimaros of assembling it, and enlists Jason's astral magic to seal the astral aperture.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read; she checks a pocket watch (Book 7, Ch47 L33).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Impatient**: unhappy about being "saddled with the mixed group" (Book 7, Ch47 L33).
- **Suspicious of politics**: the roster is like "a mad alchemist throwing volatile ingredients in a pot" (Book 7, Ch47 L60).
- **Practical**: she plans for the worst and does not slow when she senses the clearing's oddity (Book 7, Ch49 L39).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Gold rank (Book 7, Ch47 L33); she senses Jason's defences pop an opponent's aura like a soap bubble (Book 7, Ch47 L41-49).
- Sets up a device to seal the astral aperture, roping in Jason as astral specialist (Book 7, Ch51 L5).

### 5. Background

- She knows Orin Pensinata chiefly through his famous uncle Amos (Book 7, Ch47 L39).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 7**: the marshalling yard (Book 7, Ch47 L33-60); the clearing (Book 7, Ch49 L39); communication with the group (Book 7, Ch48 L21); sealing the aperture (Book 7, Ch51 L5).
- **Book 8**: minor mention (Book 8, mentions 2).

### 7. Relationships

- **Vesper Rimaros**: suspected of rigging the roster (Book 7, Ch47 L60).
- **Jason**: recruited for his astral skill (Book 7, Ch51 L5).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Brisk, suspicious and practical; she plans for the worst of a bad roster. Cue: checking her pocket watch.

---

## Kaito Asano

### 1. Summary

Kaito Asano is Jason's older brother, a year his senior, married to Amy, with two daughters, Hana and Jace. Easy-going and socially skilled, he is the "well-received original" Jason grew up in the shadow of. In Book 4 he reconnects with Jason, in Book 5 he flies a helicopter with Network defectors against the vampires, and he dies in Berlin.

### 2. Appearance

- Jason's face is more angular than his brother's; Kaito has a "signature easy-going smile" that Jason spent years learning to copy (Book 4, Ch25 L44).
- At a party he wears a pale suit and pastel shirt (Book 4, Ch26 L45).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Socially adroit**: Jason says he never had "Kaito's skill for getting along" (Book 2, Ch11 L61).
- **The favourite**: their parents "got Kaito, so they were willing to put up with me" (Book 4, Ch7 L11).
- **Casual**: he greets the family meeting with "G'day... We're arrived for the mysterious family meeting" (Book 4, Ch27 L55).
- **Dry on pop culture**: his knowledge of classic pop culture "appeared to begin and end with Miami Vice" (Book 4, Ch26 L45).
- **Warm with his children**: he leads Hana by the hand and carries Jace (Book 4, Ch24 L101).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- After Jason's return he joins the Network-defector combat team with Asya and Greg (Book 5, Ch44 L17).
- He flies drones to sweep the ruins of a town (Book 5, Ch18 L83) and owns the helicopter Jason and Akari ride in (Book 5, Ch14 L37).

### 5. Background

- A year older than Jason; the "well-received original" at school, so Jason felt like "a disappointing rehash" (Book 2, Ch11 L65).
- He and Amy bought her parents' house next door to Jason's family (Book 4, Ch3 L133).
- He and Amy's relationship caused the fallout that left Jason dropping out of university; Jason did not want details (Book 4, Ch26 L47-50).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 4**: Jason sees his contact appear on the interface (Book 4, Ch3 L126) and sees the home he shares with Amy (Book 4, Ch3 L133); Erika and Kaito discuss Amy and Jason (Book 4, Ch24 L101-126); the family reunion (Book 4, Ch27 L55-70); the helicopter check with Erika (Book 4, Ch71 L62).
- **Book 5**: the strike team and helicopter crew (Book 5, Ch14 L37-49); dies in Berlin with Asya and Greg (Book 5, Ch51 L40); Jason at the funeral: "died for something that truly matters. To give his children a future" (Book 5, Ch52 L20).

### 7. Relationships

- **Jason**: brother; Jason says Kaito made his own choice to stand up for his world and that Amy knows it (Book 7, Ch1 L15).
- **Erika**: sister who keeps the family together (Book 4, Ch71 L62).
- **Hiro**: his uncle, who spoke Japanese with his mother behind their father's back (Book 4, Ch34 L131).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

His death is why Amy's children grow up without a father "because he followed me" (Book 7, Ch1 L15).

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Warm, teasing, effortless and brave without announcing it. Cue: "G'day... We're arrived for the mysterious family meeting."

---

## Ketevan Arziani

### 1. Summary

Ketevan Arziani is an analytical Network officer in Sydney, Anna Tilden's assistant, who later becomes Yumi Asano's chief of staff. Brisk and sceptical, she tests leaps of logic.

### 2. Appearance

- Jason guesses she is about thirty, athletic, with short brown hair (Book 4, Ch46 L82).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Analytical**: Anna places a lot of trust in her analytical abilities (Book 4, Ch5 L10).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Formal title: Assistant to the Director of Operations, Sydney branch (Book 4, Ch46 L82).
- Investigates a hospital event up the coast (Book 4, Ch8 L83).
- Jason saves her life with one of Shade's bodies forming a parachute (Book 4, Ch47 L28; Book 4, Ch59 L13).

### 5. Background

- She leads the Network defectors' intake and sits on calls with the Sydney branch (Book 5, Ch9 L67; Book 5, Ch36 L33).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 4**: Anna's assistant (Book 4, Ch5 L10; Book 4, Ch8 L83); parachute rescue (Book 4, Ch47 L28).
- **Book 5**: tells Jason's side "We've got a bunch of people coming in from the EOA looking to defect" (Book 5, Ch9 L67).

### 7. Relationships

- **Anna Tilden**: her director (Book 4, Ch5 L10).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Brisk and sceptical. Cue: testing leaps of logic.

## Knowledge

### 1. Summary

### 2. Appearance and manner

In Book 1 a statue is replaced by a woman with colourful clothes and Mediterranean features, beautiful yet "detached and untouchable", not holding the statue's book (Book 1, Ch46 L16). She knows everything anyone in her world knows, including Jason, so she knows Mario Kart shortcuts (Book 1, Ch77 L10). A melodious voice behind Jason says "Such a shame. I was hoping to be your first" (Book 1, Ch45 L40).

### 4. Relationships

### 5. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Detached and untouchable in manner. Cue: "And you left. You have the same option here." (Book 1, Ch46 L15). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

Warm, detached and omniscient; she answers questions honestly in a way that serves her.

## Landemere Vane

### 1. Summary

Landemere Vane is the Vane heir whose failed summoning pulls Jason to Pallimustus. He is the first person Jason meets and the first he kills, which leaves Jason shaken for weeks.

### 2. Appearance

His mother looks to be in her early thirties "no older than her son Landemere" (Book 1, Ch11 L65); no other description appears in the lines read.

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Ambitious and out of his depth**: Dougall says Landemere summoned "one of them entities from beyond the void" (Book 1, Ch9 L47-50).
- **Traditional family**: his mother says "this is my manor, my family, and we do things the traditional way" (Book 1, Ch9 L54).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Clive explains that Landemere's summoning went wrong, possibly because he reached beyond his ability or a dimensional event interfered (Book 7, Ch55 L64-79).
- He was holding more than a thousand coins when killed (Book 1, Ch13 L13).

### 5. Background

- His mother, who looks no older than her son, is among the cultists who greet Jason (Book 1, Ch11 L65).
- The Vane family treats the summoning as a family matter: "This is my manor, my family" (Book 1, Ch9 L54).
- Familiar: Jason's first encounter with a native creature was Vane's familiar, which tried to avenge him and fell victim to aging masonry (Book 5, Ch40 L81).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: the summoning in the Vane basement (Book 1, Ch9 L47-54); Jason reflects on the "very first person Jason met" and first killed (Book 1, Ch61 L41).
- **Book 2**: Clive regrets losing his notes (Book 2, Ch37 L67).
- **Book 4-7**: the first kill shakes Jason (Book 4, Ch2 L28); Clive's later explanation (Book 7, Ch55 L64).

### 7. Relationships

- **Cressida Vane**: his mother, who calls him "poor Landemere" (Book 1, Ch9 L54).
- **Jason**: accidental killer (Book 4, Ch2 L28).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Clive says random dimensional events connecting worlds are rare but more frequent because of the link between the worlds (Book 7, Ch55 L64).

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Not heard on the page; remembered as ambitious and out of his depth.

---

## Liara Rimaros

### 1. Summary

Liara Rimaros is a gold-rank Adventure Society special-operations officer and a lesser member of the Rimaros royal house. She screens adventurers for Builder cultists in Book 7, leads the Book 8 expedition into the Builder's complex, handles Jason for the royal family and stays a brisk, protective ally.

### 2. Appearance

- Tall (her eldest daughter has "Liara's height") and gold-rank fast, flitting through streets unnoticed (Book 7, Ch17 L20; Book 8, Ch62 L39).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Brisk and official**: she is working without rest as the city fills with adventurers to be filtered for cultists (Book 7, Ch17 L3).
- **Wary of politics**: she "disdained" the politics she "could never seem to escape" (Book 9, Ch11 L53).
- **Self-critical but resolute**: after Gibson Amouz's capture she "allowed shame to flash over her expression before schooling it" (Book 8, Ch20 L3); she stands up to his father, "Get out of my office, Lord Amouz" (Book 8, Ch20 L57).
- **Protective**: "Don't even think about it", she tells Zareen of Jason (Book 8, Ch62 L59).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Rank**: a gold-ranker, leader of the Book 8 expedition with her former teammate Jana Costi as second (Book 8, Ch7 L4).
- **Investigator**: her search for the cult's portal users is a process of elimination through portal schedules, market tracking and field reports (Book 8, Ch20 L11).
- **Not for rescues**: she understands why she was told not to take part personally in the mining-complex rescue: "Her abilities were ill-suited to the task and her emotional investment would not be an asset" (Book 8, Ch40 L29).

### 5. Background

- **Royal branch**: "from one of the more remote branches of the royal family", not close enough to be called a princess, but entitled to a home on the royal sky island and to take part in the contests for the throne in her youth (Book 7, Ch17 L14).
- **Career**: "a long and prestigious career in Adventure Society special operations" (Book 7, Ch17 L15).
- **Marriage**: in her youth she married a local lordling, Baseph, "to secure royal influence"; they live largely separate lives but remain friends (Book 7, Ch17 L19-20). Baseph is born into House Amouz (Book 9, Ch5 L3).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 7**: she has been "working without rest" as adventurers are filtered for potential cultists (Book 7, Ch17 L3); Jason is told "Pelli and Liara Rimaros will be your contact points" (Book 7, Ch18 L95). She and Keel watch Jason and Farrah through a seeing stone (Book 7, Ch11 L65), and takes Jason to a private meeting room in the administration building (Book 7, Ch38 L91).
- **Book 8**: she leads the expedition on the Builder complex (Book 8, Ch7 L4), faces Lord Amouz (Book 8, Ch20 L14-57), re-collates her files (Book 8, Ch26 L15) and, assigned to handle Jason for the royal family, meets him with an agenda rather than gratitude (Book 8, Ch62 L30).
- **Book 9**: she tells Jason's group not to roam like a pack (Book 9, Ch14 L5), sits with him at the confrontation (Book 9, Ch16 L3) and goes to the cloud house (Book 9, Ch6 L73).

### 7. Relationships

- **Baseph and the children**: Baseph, Dara, Zareen and Joseph (Book 8, Ch62 L28-32); see Baseph_and_the_Rimaros_Children.md and Zareen_Rimaros.md.
- **Amouz family**: Cassin Amouz is her husband's family; she calls him "Uncle Cassin" when angry (Book 8, Ch20 L21).
- **Trenchant Moore**: stands beside her in the royal guard crisis (Book 7, Ch52 L71).
- **Jason**: she asks Farrah whether he knows about the Adventure Society liaison and says loyal, consistent people are to be treasured (Book 9, Ch6 L73-79).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Brisk, official and not unkind; she says what she needs and trusts competence. Cue: "Don't even think about it."

---

## Lucian Lamprey

### 1. Summary

Lucian Lamprey is the corrupt director of Greenstone's Magic Society branch, an elf with a fighter's physique "banished to Greenstone for previous improprieties". He treats the Fortress as his office, orchestrates Jason's kidnapping with Cole Silva, and is cut down by Danielle Geller and imprisoned.

### 2. Appearance

- An uncommonly muscular elf who "would not look out of place in the fighting pits himself", in expensive clothes (Book 1, Ch42 L26).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Smug and indulgent**: he arrives at his viewing room "with a contented sigh" (Book 1, Ch43 L47).
- **Handsy**: he strokes his fingers over a face in the viewing window (Book 1, Ch43 L69).
- **Quick to blame**: his fury threatens to erupt when pressed (Book 2, Ch38 L19).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Danielle outpaces him in a duel and cuts him (Book 3, Ch24 L23).
- His handling of a star seed for the Church of Purity comes under scrutiny (Book 3, Ch1 L107).

### 5. Background

- "Banished to Greenstone for previous improprieties"; he is not a member of the local elf community (Book 1, Ch42 L25-26).
- His deputy Pochard maintains the city campus so he can use the Fortress as his office (Book 1, Ch42 L30).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: the fighting pits viewing rooms (Book 1, Ch42-43; Book 1, Ch50 L104).
- **Book 2**: Clive's racks discussion (Book 2, Ch7 L62).
- **Book 3**: held in the prison tower (Book 3, Ch24 L48).
- **Book 9**: the kidnapping traced to him and Cole Silva (Book 9, Ch2 L84).

### 7. Relationships

- **Pochard Finn**: deputy and friend (Book 1, Ch50 L104).
- **Cole Silva**: co-conspirator (Book 9, Ch2 L84).
- **Danielle Geller**: opponent (Book 3, Ch24 L23).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Smug, indulgent and quick to blame. Cue: the contented sigh.

---

## Neil Davone

### 1. Summary

Neil Davone is an elf of Greenstone's Island families, a healer who grows from the Mercers' hanger-on into the heart of Jason's team. He is a priest of the Healer, and by 2038 he uses his growth magic to reclaim deserts.

### 2. Appearance

Muscular (Sophie and Belinda comment on his "muscular body", Book 2, Ch71 L14), though the notes describe him as "chubby for an elf" (Book 2, Ch71 L14).

### 3. Personality and voice

He grew up in the Mercer family's orbit: he started spending time at the Mercer compound as a boy because Thadwick needed friends his own age and the Davone and Kettering families had close ties to the Mercers (Book 2, Ch27 L41-43). At thirteen he had a crush on Cassandra Mercer, four years older (Book 2, Ch27 L41-48). He makes a stand in the Temple of the Healer (Book 1, Ch94 L3-7), and after being rejected by the Mercers' circle the guards treat him as "just another iron-ranker" (Book 2, Ch27 L59). He is uncertain about joining Jason's team (Book 2, Ch27 L46) and shows a nervous expression when pressed (Book 2, Ch27 L112).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Essences**: Shield, Growth and Renewal, producing the Prosperity confluence (Jason's notebook, Book 2, Ch28 L44): short-lived shields that intercept attacks, healing and cleansing.
- **Abilities**: size-growth power (Book 2, Ch30 L52-54) and Armour of Renewal, which reduces damage taken and gives healing over time (Book 2, Ch64 L55); the Reaper's Redoubt (wiki).
- **Summon**: a chrysalis golem "like an ogre carved out of diamond" with runes appearing on its facets as it is hit (Book 3, Ch4 L29-32).

### 5. Background

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: stands in the Temple of the Healer; joins Rufus, Gary and Jory at the clinic reopening.
- **Books 2-3**: joins Jason's team; the chrysalis golem and shields.

### 7. Relationships

Jory Tillman, Dustin Kettering (friend, tank), the Mercer family, Cassandra Mercer, Nik, the Church of the Healer.

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

His fall from the Mercers' favour is level 1.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Tentative before committing. Cue: Asano's visit left Neil uncertain, since the only real attraction to joining the team was Humphrey Geller (Book 2, Ch27 L46). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

Calm and humane; the healer who patches everyone up.

---

## Nicolas Hendren

### 1. Summary

Nicolas Hendren is the archbishop of the Church of Purity, a silver-rank barrier-and-force specialist who flees the inquiry and joins the Builder cult's camp before Jason's team kills him. Stiff, proud and rule-bound, he wants Purity's dignity even as it is dragged into the cult's plans.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read.

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Disgruntled and proud**: he is summoned to the cult camp and refuses to taint the church for "short-term power" (Book 3, Ch54 L21; Book 3, Ch56 L57).
- **Moral authority absent god**: his god's voice is silent, making him the highest moral authority in the realm he stands in (Book 3, Ch56 L71).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Silver-rank barrier and force specialist: his barrier shields his group from a falling building (Book 3, Ch58 L3); a force wave power (Book 3, Ch59 L103); "always the true danger" (Book 3, Ch58 L47).

### 5. Background

- Archbishop of the Church of Purity, introduced at a Greenstone meeting (Book 2, Ch19 L73). One Book 3 line spells the name Hedron (Book 3, Ch1 L106).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 2**: enters the ritual room with Danielle Geller, Thalia Mercer, Elspeth Arella and Emir Bahadir (Book 2, Ch19 L73).
- **Book 3**: vanishes on a "previously scheduled sabbatical" (Book 3, Ch31 L57); joins the camp (Book 3, Ch54-56); the battle (Book 3, Ch58-59); killed, with a large part of Purity's funds in his storage (Book 3, Ch60 L12); the Builder says losing Hendren's power is a blow though he was a reluctant ally (Book 3, Ch60 L32).

### 7. Relationships

- **The Builder cult**: reluctant ally (Book 3, Ch60 L32).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

He took a large part of Purity's funds with him (Book 3, Ch60 L12).

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Stiff, proud and rule-bound.

---

## Padma Parsell

### 1. Summary

Padma Parsell is an adventurer from Remore Academy and Farrah's half-disciple, who leads a Vitesse team in the City of Fallen Echoes. Keen, grieving and quick to speak her mind, she treats Jason as a rival after Farrah's death.

### 2. Appearance

- Onyx skin and fiery eyes typical of her people; unaffected by heat (Book 2, Ch39 L13).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Keen and grieving**: Farrah was her idol and mentor (Book 2, Ch38 L40-41); she lets Jason vent her frustration (Book 2, Ch38 L43).
- **Obedient to instructors**: Instructor Gabriel's voice triggers conditioned obedience (Book 2, Ch38 L66).
- **Ignores interruptions**: when Lance calls out under his breath, she ignores him (Book 2, Ch38 L35).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Leader of a Vitesse team of Rufus's juniors (Book 3, Ch1 L87).

### 5. Background

- Trained at Remore Academy a few years behind Rufus (Book 2, Ch38 L40).
- She had waited for Farrah's return to Vitesse and, when Farrah died, joined Emir's call for adventurers (Book 2, Ch38 L42).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 2**: cadet Padma Parsell (Book 2, Ch38 L65); Farrah's parents seek her out (Book 2, Ch38 L84); her team joins the trial late (Book 2, Ch55 L69).
- **Book 3**: wants to test itself against Jason's team; standoffish at first, warming up (Book 3, Ch1 L87-88); her team leader builds a rivalry with Valdis (Book 3, Ch1 L89).

### 7. Relationships

- **Farrah**: mentor (Book 2, Ch38 L40-41).
- **Jason**: friend and rival (Book 2, Ch39 L42).
- **Valdis**: sword rival (Book 3, Ch1 L89).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Keen, grieving and quick to speak her mind.

---

## Pelli

### 1. Summary

Pelli is a sharp-tongued core-using gold-ranker who keeps the town of Palisaros quiet and safe, is "Aunt Pelli" to the royal family, and is a contact for Jason in the Storm Kingdom. She fights a pirate captain outside Kasilaro and organises civilians at the mining facility.

### 2. Appearance

- Walks up the path from town with two celestines with emerald hair and eyes (Book 7, Ch18 L28).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Blunt**: "The name's Pelli and I'll thank you to use it" (Book 7, Ch13 L51).
- **Teasing and self-deprecating**: "I'm just an old core user"; she shakes her head at "an abundance of experience, dear gods" (Book 7, Ch15 L67-71).
- **Protective**: she fights outside the town to spare Kasilaro's residents (Book 7, Ch64 L25).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- A gold-ranker and core user; she fights a pirate captain outside a town (Book 7, Ch64 L25; Book 8, Ch41 L18).
- She had a magic barrier put on a cave behind the waterfall (Book 7, Ch13 L99).

### 5. Background

- "Aunt Pelli" to Vesper from when she was a girl (Book 7, Ch15 L67).
- Mayor-level authority in Palisaros: Argy tells Jason "You'll want to talk to Pelli" (Book 7, Ch13 L28).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 7**: meets Jason and Rufus (Book 7, Ch13 L28-99); greets Vesper (Book 7, Ch15 L65-71); tells Zara where Jason is (Book 7, Ch17 L86); the pirate fight (Book 7, Ch64 L25).
- **Book 8**: organises civilians and mining facility evacuees (Book 8, Ch41 L18).

### 7. Relationships

- **Argy**: local friend (Book 7, Ch13 L74).
- **Vesper**: calls her "Aunt Pelli" (Book 7, Ch15 L67).
- **Rufus**: she asks about his father and the sword (Book 7, Ch13 L51).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Blunt and watchful; protects the town's quiet life from outsiders. Cue: "The name's Pelli and I'll thank you to use it."

---

## Rufus Remore

### 1. Summary

Rufus Remore is a bald, dark-skinned swordsman from Vitesse, grandson of the diamond-ranker Roland Remore. He is one of the three adventurers Jason meets in the Vane cellar, runs Jason's field assessment, and remains his friend for the rest of the series.

### 2. Appearance

Dark skin, a bald head and "stupidly handsome"; when Jason first sees him, grimy from his ordeal, he looks like an action hero heading into act three (Book 1, Ch11 L49). His voice is "like dark chocolate" (Book 1, Ch11 L51). He is "tall and lean" (Book 7, Ch9 L6) and a "tall, leanly muscular adventurer" (Book 9, Ch3 L59); no height in numbers is given.

### 3. Personality and voice

- He is calm under pressure: when Anisa demands that Jason be burned as tainted, he answers "I did [know]" and refuses her any say (Book 1, Ch30 L88-93).
- He feels responsible as the leader of his group: Farrah tells Jason "Rufus is in charge, so he blames himself" for their capture, and says the pressure on him from his family is enormous (Book 1, Ch30 L57-60).
- After Farrah's death he is driven by obsession, which strains his relationships (Book 3, Ch33 L11-12).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Swordsmanship**: an elegant scimitar of golden light with red-gold inscriptions that he conjures in his hand (Book 1, Ch16 L31-36); silver and golden swords (Book 1, Ch16 L31-36).
- **Other powers**: boots that let him walk on water (Book 1, Ch16 L38) and a "speed of light" power that makes the world "seem to freeze in time" for "two seconds of accelerated time" (Book 1, Ch108 L99; Book 1, Ch109 L23).
- **Kingdom of Eclipse**: a sun-and-moon power invoked by incantation ("Darkness and light, sun and moon; be mine to awaken and move at my command...", Book 2, Ch76 L71).

### 5. Background

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: captured with Gary and Farrah by the Vane blood cult; frees them with Jason; runs Jason's field assessment; present when Farrah dies.
- **Books 2-3**: uses his Kingdom of Eclipse power in the strike on the Builder cult camp (Book 2, Ch76 L71).
- **Book 7**: reunites with Jason in Rimaros.

### 7. Relationships

Jason (closest friend), Gary and Farrah (childhood friends), Roland, Arabelle, Gabriel and Gwydion (family), Humphrey Geller, Taika Williams, Hassan Dukhtar. The books only imply a relationship with Vincent Trenslow, so it is not recorded here (project decision).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Courteous and formal, quick to apologise for discourtesy. Cue: his apology to Priestess Pellin (Book 7, Ch9 L18). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

Speak calmly and courteously; refuse unreasonable demands without raising your voice; make quiet jokes about the family school.

---

## Sendira

### 1. Summary

Sendira is Melody Jain's second in command in the Order of Redeeming Light's local enclave: a silver-rank zealot and the person Melody trusts most. She commands the Purity forces on the island in Book 8, carries the aura beacon and the clockwork kings, and holds the order's discipline together while suspecting others.

### 2. Facts

- **Rank in the order**: Melody leads the enclave and Sendira, her second, reports on what the scouts have found (Book 7, Ch58 L38). The overall leadership is the same silver rank as the individual cell leaders, so there is "a constant tension" (Book 8, Ch9 L17).
- **Reliability**: she is "ever-reliable"; like another cell leader, Kelleigh, she is not ambitious, which places her as Melody's second (Book 8, Ch33 L16).
- **Doctrine**: she tells the converted, "Only Purity is worthy of our devotion, not his servants... We are all unworthy, seeking to cleanse the taint that lingers within us" (Book 8, Ch9 L21), and "Distraction and frivolity taint pure dedication of purpose" (Book 8, Ch9 L14).
- **Command on the island**: she is "the commander of the Purity forces on the island", separating from most of her group to take a smaller team underground (Book 8, Ch13 L3).
- **Control of subordinates**: she wants Fila and Ramona "under her thumb instead of causing trouble for Melody's plans" (Book 8, Ch13 L6). She snaps at Ramona that "What Melody wants with the target is no concern of yours. Your place is to obey, not criticise in ignorance" (Book 8, Ch13 L54).
- **The beacon**: when the grab team seizes Melody's daughter, "Sendira couldn't activate the beacon fast enough" (Book 8, Ch13 L51). She launches a flare that scatters motes of light (Book 8, Ch14 L59), and the beacon's pervasive aura drenches the island until it is cut off, at which she shows "an expression of uncertainty" (Book 8, Ch14 L70-72).
- **Clockwork kings**: when the Builder cult delivered the first clockwork king, it came with a control device; she explains that the order purified both (Book 8, Ch14 L67). She orders the kings through a portal after taking out a silver pyramid (Book 8, Ch16 L85-92).
- **Doubt among her people**: Jaime says "Sendira is using us as decoys" (Book 8, Ch16 L29).
- **Assessment**: "The likelihood of their having realised our objective is high" (Book 8, Ch16 L82).
- **At the leaders' meeting**: she stands next to Melody as hostile cell leaders glare (Book 8, Ch17 L55); angry at a remark, she is silenced by Melody's gesture (Book 8, Ch18 L8). Melody asks whether she has been telling people they were going after Melody's daughter (Book 8, Ch18 L80).
- **Search**: she crouches with the others to feel faint vibrations through the floor (Book 8, Ch33 L22).

### 3. Roleplay notes

Rigid, loyal and humourless: she speaks in doctrine, obeys Melody absolutely and treats questions as faults. When her plan fails she falls quiet rather than doubting.

---

## Sophie Wexler

### 1. Summary

Sophie Wexler is a celestine fighter known as the Nightingale in the Greenstone fighting pits. She is a thief and debt-slave when Jason meets her, has her indenture taken over by Jason in Book 2, and becomes the team's fastest fighter and, later, Humphrey's fiancée.

### 2. Appearance

Chocolate skin, shining silver hair and sharp eyes that exactly match the hair, "the matching metallics a giveaway trait of the celestine race"; normally shoulder length, tied back in a simple, practical ponytail (Book 1, Ch43 L18).

### 3. Personality and voice

Suspicious of kindness at first: "Are you trying to tell me that Jory is such a good friend to you that you'd go this far over some girl he likes?" (Book 2, Ch1 L77-80). She fights through pain without complaint; in Book 2, one-armed and struggling to her knees, she waves off the lesser miracle potion Jason offers (Book 2, Ch45 L69-72). Her laugh is "free" (Book 9, Ch48 L12).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- **Essences**: Swift, Balance and Wind, producing the Mystic confluence: Clive answers "Mystic" when Rufus asks what that combination produces (Book 2, Ch5 L82-87). Before Jason's help she had only the Swift essence and its single ability for more than half a dozen years (Book 2, Ch5 L37-41).
- **Abilities**: Eternal Moment, a massive acceleration power in which "time seemed to freeze" as she produced wind blades (Book 3, Ch49 L15); Between the Raindrops (from the Awakening Stone of the Rain; notes, Book 2); Mirage Step and Wind Blade (Book 2, Ch8 L170-173; Ch7 L129); unarmed Way of the Reaper strikes.
- **Racial gifts** (celestine): Mana Integrity evolves into Mana Wellspring (Book 2, Ch61 L146), Astral Affinity into Boundary Breaker, which removes the damage reduction from rank disparity (Book 3, Ch68 L14-15), and Celestial Swiftness into Princess of the Firmament (Book 8, Ch22 L37-38).

### 5. Background

Born in Kurdansk, in the People's Holy Federation of Dreisil (Book 6, Ch47 L390, Emir). Her father's failed expedition left her with a huge debt at sixteen or seventeen, and she worked as a pit fighter; Cole Silva, whose father her father had worked for, wanted her (Book 1, Ch51 L26; Book 1, Ch50 L92). Her mother is Melody Jain (Book 9, Ch19 L3-6), who faked her death some twenty years earlier (Book 6, Ch47 L510-513).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 1**: caught as a thief, sentenced to indenture; fights in the pits as the Nightingale.
- **Book 2**: Jason takes her indenture; she joins the Adventure Society.
- **Books 3-6**: key fighter; the team's accelerator.
- **Book 7**: Jason learns that her mother leads the Order of Redeeming Light in Rimaros (Book 7, Ch58 L38-46).

### 7. Relationships

Humphrey (fiancé), Belinda (oldest friend), Jason (the man who took her indenture), Melody Jain (mother), Danielle Geller.

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Her mother's identity and fate are levels 2-3.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Wary of kindness and silent about pain. Cues: she asks why Jason would do so much for them (Book 2, Ch1 L77-80); she grimaces silently through the pain of a broken arm (Book 2, Ch45 L69-72). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

Quick and sharp; she distrusts kindness at first and stays in the fight through pain.

---

## Timos

### 1. Summary

Timos is a Builder cultist embedded in Greenstone for years who regrets going along with Thadwick's self-recruitment, kills Frank, and stands with Zato in the Vane estate ritual. He is careful, patient and horrified by amateurs.

### 2. Appearance

Not described in the lines read; he wears dismissive sneers until Thadwick wipes one off (Book 2, Ch41 L89).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Regretful**: he "quickly came to regret" Thadwick's aggressive self-recruitment (Book 2, Ch59 L87).
- **Cautious**: every mistake "threatened to snowball into disaster" (Book 2, Ch59 L89).
- **Compartmentalising**: he facilitated the cult's insertions and ran operatives in compartmentalised groups (Book 3, Ch34 L10).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- He kills Frank with a conjured spear (Book 2, Ch59 L106-110).
- He takes part in a ritual with Zato and Dougall (Book 3, Ch51 L85).

### 5. Background

- "Timos had been operating in Greenstone for years" (Book 2, Ch59 L87).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 2**: Thadwick presses him about flashes of memory (Book 2, Ch41 L88-89); the Frank murder (Book 2, Ch59 L87-110).
- **Book 3**: led with Thadwick across the Vane estate by Zato (Book 3, Ch46 L55); the ritual (Book 3, Ch51 L85); Zato notes the marks on his neck (Book 3, Ch41 L73).

### 7. Relationships

- **Zato**: his leader (Book 3, Ch46 L55).
- **Thadwick**: an inconvenient recruit (Book 2, Ch59 L87).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Careful, patient and horrified by amateurs. Cue: a dismissive sneer.

---

## Trenchant Moore

### 1. Summary

Trenchant Moore is a gold-rank human sword specialist of the Sapphire Crown royal guard who escorts Jason's group in Rimaros. Watchful and reserved, he reads places and people for danger.

### 2. Appearance

- A human in a guild of mostly celestines: pale-skinned and dark-haired, lean and angular with icy blue eyes (Book 7, Ch21 L13-14).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **Reserved**: he drops to one knee before the royals (Book 7, Ch46 L74).
- **Cautious about politics**: he warns that Vesper's world is political (Book 7, Ch58 L53).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Gold rank sword specialist who has served as a guard on and off for decades (Book 7, Ch21 L13-18).
- He boards ships checking society contracts (Book 7, Ch22 L93).

### 5. Background

- The royal family does not shield the Sapphire Crown from surge quotas (Book 7, Ch21 L15-18).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 7**: summoned to a low-key palace meeting where Vesper Rimaros lets him keep his sword (Book 7, Ch21 L21-25); escorts Jason's group to the cloud house (Book 7, Ch53 L102); Jason tells him to hear his debrief (Book 7, Ch35 L72); stands with Liara when Zara confronts them (Book 7, Ch46 L81).

### 7. Relationships

- **Liara**: stands beside her (Book 7, Ch46 L81).
- **Vesper Rimaros**: the royal who gives him leave to keep his sword (Book 7, Ch21 L21-25).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Watchful and reserved; reads places and people for danger. Cue: dropping to one knee.

---

## Valdis Volaire

### 1. Summary

A swordmaster prince of the Kingdom of Mirrors whom Clive meets in the City of Fallen Echoes; he is cheerful, reckless and a rival and friend to Jason's team.

### 2. Facts

- Introduces himself as "Valdis" and praises Clive's reputation (Book 2, Ch48 L77); the system names him Valdis Volaire (Book 2, Ch51 L56).
- A reckless enthusiast who leads his three teammates up a tower in search of treasure (Book 2, Ch51 L9-10); he has a light-step power, a slow-fall power and a wall run (Book 2, Ch51 L17, L28, L32).
- He chooses the path of courage in the trial and is the first to step forward (Book 2, Ch51 L42; Book 2, Ch57 L63).
- A sword master, mobile striker and the stronger team's leader (Book 3, Ch4 L3, L7); his team is a highly efficient cohort trained since before they had essences (Book 3, Ch3 L80).
- His father collects him from Jason's ship, to Valdis's embarrassment (Book 3, Ch5 L42-50); he portals in for Jason's memorial (Book 3, Ch69 L895).

### 3. Timeline of involvement

- Book 2: Clive's team-mate in the City of Fallen Echoes; party and trials (Book 2, Ch48-61).
- Book 3: friendly rival team; mock battles (Book 3, Ch4; Book 5, Ch64 L24).

### 4. Roleplay notes

---

## Zara Rimaros (Zara Nareen)

### 1. Summary

Zara is a celestine princess of the Storm Kingdom, the "Hurricane Princess". Jason meets her in a tent during the contest for the Scythe of the Reaper, when she is nineteen and he is iron-rank. Years later the scandal of her false engagement claim, her loss of the succession, and her adoption into her mother's house bring her to Team Biscuit and, in 2038, into a romance with Jason.

### 2. Appearance

### 3. Personality and voice

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

### 5. Background

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 2**: meets Jason in a tent at the Scythe contest; the Hurricane Princess at the farewell feast.
- **Book 7**: Zara had been matched with a member of House Irios, a political marriage the royals wanted for stability before the surge (Book 7, Ch18 L66-72).
- **Book 9**: she is a former princess of the Storm Kingdom by Book 9 (Book 9, Ch40 L21); she says "I'm Zara Nareen now" (Book 9, Ch40 L18) and that she adopted into her mother's house, whose colouration she wears (Book 9, Ch40 L13), to signal her withdrawal from the contest for the crown (Book 9, Ch40 L72).

### 7. Relationships

Jason, Vesper and Liara Rimaros, Emiliano, Team Biscuit, Zareen Rimaros.

### 8. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Courtly, with a threat in the smile. Cue: "Might I introduce my aunt, Vesper Rimaros?" (Book 2, Ch69 L17). See `../10_Roleplay_Support/Tone_and_Voice.md`.

Courtly with a streak of mischief; storms for emphasis.

## Zato

### 1. Summary

Zato is the silver-rank leader of the Builder cult's local forces in Book 3, a metal-bodied shapeshifter who recruits Thadwick, drives the cult's survivors to the Vane Estate and is the final opponent of Jason's team. He is competent, unfazed and sure of victory.

### 2. Appearance

- A silvery metal body that flows "like quicksilver", reshaping itself to produce combat abilities (Book 3, Ch67 L69).

### 3. Personality and voice

- **On top of things**: Jason says "He does seem pretty on top of things... I actually kind of like him" and invites him to join team "Hopelessly Outmatched" (Book 3, Ch57 L119).
- **Convinced of victory**: "Zato knew that his victory was imminent. He knew that the adventurers were no match for his power" (Book 3, Ch68 L60).
- **Quiet in command**: he stands with his back to the door, eyes lingering on Timos's neck marks, saying nothing (Book 3, Ch41 L73).
- **Recruiter**: "I see your true potential" (Book 3, Ch46 L60).

### 4. Powers and skills as world facts

- Silver rank, with a metal body of shifting abilities (Book 3, Ch67 L69).
- Immovable in the final fight: no damage seems to impair him, even Humphrey's strikes (Book 3, Ch68 L46).
- Trump card: after baiting out Sophie's absorb power, his body explodes into a mass of shrapnel (Book 3, Ch67 L100).

### 5. Background

- "The last silver-ranker left in the Builder cult's local forces"; tasked with leading the evacuation when the island stronghold was invaded, he escaped with a good number of people, though none of the other silver-rankers survived (Book 3, Ch7 L88-89).

### 6. Timeline of involvement

- **Book 3**: leads the evacuation (Book 3, Ch7 L88-89); stands before the inert archway (Book 3, Ch41 L73); recruits Thadwick (Book 3, Ch46 L60); asks what to do with bronze-rankers the cult cannot convert with clockwork cores (Book 3, Ch60 L37); falls before Jason and Sophie flee to the gate (Book 3, Ch66 L45); the climax with Humphrey and Sophie (Book 3, Ch67 L69-100; Book 3, Ch68 L46-61).

### 7. Relationships

- **Thadwick Mercer**: his recruit (Book 3, Ch46 L60).
- **Humphrey and Sophie**: opponents in the climax (Book 3, Ch68 L58-60).
- **Timos**: his follower, whose neck he notices (Book 3, Ch41 L73).

### 8. Secrets and spoiler notes

Nothing hidden in the lines read.

### 9. Roleplay notes

**Voice (condensed)**: Measured, zealous and confident until the very end. Cue: "I see your true potential."

---

## Colin

### 1. Summary

Jason's first familiar: a swarm of apocalypse leeches that lives in his blood, sprays out over enemies to bite and drain them, and can take the shape of a red-eyed blood clone of Jason. Colin begins as a hungry, wriggling swarm and grows into a being who talks back, waves from his bowl of leeches and calls Jason "smarmy".

### 2. Facts

- **Named**: Jason says, "I've decided to call him Colin. And his friends. Team Colin" (Book 1, Ch30 L85), and calls the swarm "Leechy" in the same fight (Book 1, Ch29 L23).
- **Lives in Jason's blood**: Jason pricks his finger and the leeches crawl into his hand and vanish into his blood (Book 1, Ch72 L32). His rapid regeneration only works while the familiar is in his bloodstream (Book 1, Ch72 L32).
- **Method**: Jason sprays him out over a target like a fire hose and Colin crawls over armour to find flesh (Book 3, Ch8 L76; Book 7, Ch26 L22). Colin wiggles happily when pleased (Book 3, Ch21 L132).
- **Where he comes from**: a summoned familiar is created through different means from the sanguine horror, but Clive observes it is what Jason summoned (Book 1, Ch75 L12), and if Jason reached diamond his familiar would reach the full strength of a sanguine horror (Book 1, Ch75 L14).

### 3. Later developments, Books 5 to 9

- **Silver vessel**: he needs a silver-rank vessel with simple requirements, such as a wheelbarrow-load of silver-rank blood quintessence (Book 5, Ch27 L57).
- **Blood clone**: Colin takes a form like a blood clone of Jason (Book 6, Ch29 L10), and Clive can switch-teleport him (Book 8, Ch10 L30).
- **Origin**: he comes from the border between the realms of the All-Devouring Eye and Legion, where life and annihilation are neighbours (Book 8, Ch55 L92).
- **Refined**: Jason refines his physical vessel with the astral throne and his connections with the astral gate (Book 9, Ch64 L90). A window offers him the chance to awaken as a Voice of the Will after extended hibernation in the astral kingdom (Book 9, Ch69 L55).

### 5. Using this in a story

Colin is a mascot who happens to be a nightmare. He is happiest eating, enjoys his role in a fight and has become a person with opinions, so play him as cheerfully ravenous rather than menacing. His regeneration is a reason Jason does not use him lightly in crowds of friends, and his hunger limits what he can do for long.

---

## Gordon

### 1. Summary

Jason's third familiar: an Avatar of Doom, a floating cloak holding a nebula in the shape of an eye, circled by blue and orange eye-orbs that fire beams. He cannot speak, but he answers questions with his orbs and has a distinct personality, and he is the key to the intrinsic-mandate magic Jason uses to work with authority.

### 2. Facts

- **Summoned**: the doom essence ability Avatar of Doom summons a familiar (Book 2, Ch64 L12). At first it is unnamed, an iron-rank incorporeal familiar whose orbs make sustained beams, one of disruptive force and one of resonating force, and whose damage leaves Vulnerable (Book 2, Ch72 L140-144).
- **Named**: asked if it wants a name, it flashes its blue orb, and Jason says, "I'm going to call you... Gordon" (Book 2, Ch72 L148-155). Orange is no and blue is yes: Gordon's orange orb flashes brightly as his signal for no (Book 3, Ch46 L96). Shade relays him: "Gordon says that I should be last" (Book 3, Ch46 L95).
- **Appearance**: a disembodied cloak with a nebula-like cloud of blue and orange in the shape of an eye, and eyeball-patterned orbs drifting around it that fire unrelenting beams (Book 3, Ch4 L28).
- **In a fight**: his beams inflict Vulnerable on a lizard (Book 3, Ch8 L76); his energy dash is fast but his reflexes are sluggish, and a blow can dissipate his vessel entirely (Book 3, Ch13 L38).

### 3. Later developments, Books 5 to 9

- **Subsumed**: while he is subsumed into Jason at silver rank, Jason can call up one of each orb to use for himself (Book 5, Ch49 L43).
- **Shields**: his orbs make shields that protect Jason (Book 6, Ch38 L35; Book 7, Ch26 L29).
- **Reputation**: the Magic Society has only iron- and bronze-rank information on the "floating death orbs" and finds it terrifying (Book 8, Ch25 L48).
- **Origin**: he comes from the realm of the All-Devouring Eye (Book 8, Ch55 L92).
- **The orrery**: in Book 9 he draws a glowing orrery that looks like a solar system made of fireworks, a work he completes by floating down next to Jason (Book 9, Ch94 L92).

### 5. Using this in a story

Gordon is the silent member of the household, and the humour comes from how much he communicates without words. He is not shown speaking in the lines checked, so give him lights, orb movement and a taste for whatever Jason is eating. His orbs are the best shield and ranged support Jason has, and his dependence on his vessel is his weakness. Note that a different Gordon, a human, is called "Other Gordon" in Books 4 and 5 (Book 4, Ch38 L26: "Hanging Rock?" Other Gordon asked).

---

## Onslow

### 1. Summary

Clive Standish's familiar: a rune tortoise whose shell carries runes that throw elemental attacks, and whose shell the whole team rides in, shelters in and runs the battlefield from. Onslow is gentle and a little stubborn, and Clive talks to him as "little buddy".

### 2. Facts

- **First sight**: Onslow is at the front of Clive's airboat with his head jutting forward "like a dog with its head out a car window" (Book 1, Ch68 L106). Clive sits on his shell with Jason beside him (Book 1, Ch69 L56).
- **Powers**: he fires elemental attacks from the magic runes on his shell, which Clive recharges with his own mana (Book 3, Ch4 L29; Book 3, Ch38 L54). A rune dims when he takes a hit (Book 1, Ch56 L24).
- **A seat for the team**: Clive picks up Stash and climbs on Onslow's shell with Neil, and the tortoise floats off the rooftop on a cushion of air (Book 3, Ch66 L46-47). Clive drops down off him when the tower is threatened (Book 3, Ch69 L97).

### 3. Later developments, Books 7 to 9

- **Precaution**: when a familiar tears open the side of a ship, Clive sends Onslow out as a precaution (Book 7, Ch6 L52).
- **Power in a fight**: he pours elemental magic into the enemy from his rune shell, throwing hailstones and lightning bolts (Book 8, Ch10 L29).
- **Open-sided craft**: he expands his shell into an open-sided flying craft, and the unshelled tortoise takes the form of a small green humanoid wearing a child's clothes from Clive, "an adorable team mascot" (Book 9, Ch81 L23).
- **Under fire**: his shell draws constant barrage from the monsters, which Clive answers with ritual-enhanced wind barriers (Book 9, Ch87 L24). Humphrey tells Belinda to keep anyone from focusing on Onslow (Book 9, Ch84 L23).
- **"Little buddy"**: Clive asks, "What do you say, little buddy? Can you get us down any faster?" (Book 9, Ch97 L49).

### 5. Using this in a story

Onslow is the team's place of safety. A party that has him can retreat, heal and regroup in the middle of a battle, and a story that removes him removes that base. Play him as slow, kind and immovable, a mascot who also happens to be a fortress.

---

## Shade

### 1. Summary

Jason's shadow familiar, a formal, dry astral being who is one of the Reaper's shadows. He first appears as the keeper of the Reaper trials, becomes Jason's closest and most constant companion, and acts as his butler, driver, spy and sounding board. He says himself that he is a shepherd of death, and that he has changed more than the others of his kind.

### 2. Facts

- **The invigilator**: he appears as the shadowy figure who runs the trials of the City of Fallen Echoes and offers to explain their practical dangers or their origins (Book 2, Ch44 L65). He hands out vials and medallions with his shadowy hands, which are cold to the touch (Book 2, Ch44 L119).
- **A familiar before**: he tells the competitors that the grandmaster who built the place was an outworlder with the dark essence like Jason, "I was his summoned familiar once" (Book 2, Ch44 L93).
- **Summoned by Jason**: when Jason chooses to summon a familiar after the trials, he greets Shade with "I was hoping it would be you" (Book 2, Ch72 L28).
- **Bodies**: he appears as three bodies that become horses for a ride (Book 3, Ch5 L80) or large lizards for rough terrain (Book 3, Ch60 L8), and his bodies move among monsters as elusive shadowy figures (Book 3, Ch49 L45).
- **The Reaper's child**: the Reaper says "Of all my children, Shade has ever followed his own path" (Book 3, Ch69 L306).

### 3. Later developments, Books 4 to 9

- **Manner**: formal, dry and discreet. He objects to "a decidedly indiscreet practice in the middle of the day" (Book 4, Ch9 L21), is told by Jason to "please show our guests out" (Book 4, Ch45 L81), and is described by Jason as "very nice" and "a good friend, even if he does occasionally keep things from me" (Book 4, Ch32 L21).
- **The Reaper's view**: he tells Jason, "You have a habit of not staying dead. This is not something the Reaper likes. You have its gratitude, however" (Book 4, Ch3 L28).
- **Vehicles**: he drives as a sports car and then a sleek four-door sedan (Book 4, Ch5 L33), and drives a motorhome onto the water (Book 4, Ch24 L33). Jason calls him "the single greatest thing on this planet" when he arrives as the car (Book 4, Ch3 L83).
- **A voice**: his voice can come from Jason's shadow, as when he speaks for Jason in a meeting (Book 4, Ch46 L78), and Jason can channel his sight and voice through him (Book 4, Ch18 L40). Jason compares talking through him to a better phone (Book 4, Ch50 L76).
- **Silver rank**: his bodies can teleport between each other (Book 5, Ch16 L103-108).
- **Brother**: Umber trapped him in an astral space for centuries (Book 8, Ch55 L85-86); see `../Umber.md`.

### 5. Using this in a story

Shade is the straight man to Jason's jokes and the one who says the sensible thing in the plainest words. He is loyal but not transparent; he keeps secrets for reasons he considers good, and he is not afraid to let Jason be wrong. His speech is formal and unhurried. He can carry Jason, his luggage, his voice and his mana-draining attacks, so a story that removes him leaves Jason without a driver, a go-between and an informant at once.

---

## Shimmer

### 1. Summary

Belinda Callahan's astral lantern: a small living lantern that orbits her, projects shields, fires force bolts and feeds mana to her team. It is the quietest of the team's familiars, but it is on the field in almost every battle.

### 2. Facts

- **Summoned**: the final ability of Belinda's power set is a summoned familiar, an astral lantern (Book 2, Ch71 L50). Belinda names it: "How about Shimmer?" (Book 2, Ch72 L62).
- **In a fight**: it projects shields to protect the team (Book 3, Ch4 L32) and, with Gordon, beams the enemy to split their attention (Book 3, Ch11 L68). The force bolts of a lantern familiar prove more dangerous to its own side against Hendren's defences (Book 3, Ch58 L60).

### 3. Later developments, Books 7 to 9
