- **Mana supply**: against constructs its attacks do little, so it mostly replenishes mana (Book 7, Ch4 L78). At the battle of Yaresh it fires force bolts at worms and pumps out mana to the team (Book 9, Ch59 L67; Book 9, Ch82 L36).
- **Pairing**: Belinda's other familiar replicates Humphrey, swinging a huge sword (Book 7, Ch4 L78).

### 5. Using this in a story

Shimmer is the supply line: shields, bolts and mana. It has little personality in the text beyond its droopy orbit when Belinda is tired, so play it as a quiet companion rather than a speaker.

---

## Stash

### 1. Summary

Humphrey Geller's bonded familiar: a baby mirage dragon whose full name is Velitraxistaasch, who prefers "Stash". He changes shape constantly, starts as a barking lizard, a bird or a puppy and ends up large enough to crush a company of messengers, and he talks, mostly in cheerful, blunt remarks.

### 2. Facts

- **Name**: "It's short for Velitraxistaasch. He likes 'Stash,' though." (Book 1, Ch38 L58). Jason works out what he is at once and Humphrey is startled (Book 1, Ch38 L59-61).
- **Bond**: "Our mothers arranged him becoming my familiar. Or me becoming his person, depending on how you look at it" (Book 1, Ch38 L64). A bonded familiar is an actual magical creature that can die, unlike a summoned familiar (Book 2, Ch42 L70).
- **Shapes**: a canary on Humphrey's head and a puppy (Book 1, Ch38 L62), a bird that swoops from the sky and turns into a puppy in his arms (Book 2, Ch20 L29), a barking lizard (Book 1, Ch78 L84), and a bark lurker or a darting bird in a fight (Book 3, Ch4 L31).
- **Biscuits**: Jason feeds him a biscuit and he turns into a puppy to snuffle it from Jason's hand, then yaps his agreement (Book 1, Ch78 L85-88; Book 2, Ch70 L11).
- **Speech**: "Stash chase worm! Stash chase worm!" (Book 1, Ch87 L180).
- **Scouting**: he leads Humphrey's party by barking in the right direction to the margolls (Book 2, Ch20 L31).
- **Special attack**: Humphrey's racial ability evolves into Attack of the Mirage Dragon (Book 2, Ch61 L147).

### 3. Later developments, Books 6 to 9

- **Mother**: his mother is Carlivexistrix, a vast dragon with scales of changing rainbow colours who asks to be called Carli (Book 6, Ch47 L736-738, L804). Humphrey apologises for not doing the best job of helping her little boy grow up (Book 6, Ch47 L737).
- **Language**: "Porky pies!" and "Stick it up your bum, lady" at a Magic Society official, and "Telling people to bugger off is kind of my thing" (Book 6, Ch47 L761-763).
- **With Sophie**: he is sprawled in Sophie's lap in puppy form (Book 7, Ch57 L34), and she keeps biscuits for him (Book 7, Ch6 L87).
- **Forms in battle**: a marsh hydra (Book 8, Ch10 L29), a copy of Belinda and then of Jason (Book 8, Ch42 L35), and a hopping insect that pulls Neil out of a messenger's path (Book 9, Ch98 L93).
- **Given a task**: Jason tells him to stick to the messengers and keep them safe (Book 9, Ch81 L35).

### 5. Using this in a story

Stash is the team's most affectionate and least filtered voice. He is a dragon child, so give him a child's appetite for biscuits, praise and bad language, and a child's sudden courage. Keep him changing shape. His mother is a major dragon, which is a hook if the story needs dragon allies.

---

## The One Day War (War of Four Cities)

### 1. Summary

During the Rimaros monster surge, the Builder cult sends mobile cities against the Storm Kingdom: a ground city that walks south, an underwater city and a flying fortress-city that appears over Livaros. The adventurers answer with six diamond-rankers, gods' miracles and a single true bomb carried into the flying city. The war is later called the War of Four Cities or the One Day War (Book 7, Ch66 L27).

### 2. Warning signs

- **Rumours and reports**: Rick Geller reports a "ground city" that moves on thousands of thick legs, which started moving south several weeks earlier (Book 7, Ch59 L124). It passed through the arid lands and the Arkivahl Desert; two cities have already fallen, with news several days old (Book 7, Ch59 L127-129).
- **The assessment**: at least four diamond-rankers are needed to attack it, five or more would be better (Book 7, Ch59 L130).
- **A second city**: an underwater city is already in the kingdom and on the move; the two converge on the northern reaches (Book 7, Ch59 L131).
- **The Builder's invasion platforms** appear worldwide (Book 7, Ch8 L36). Soramir and Jason look to Dawn, who answers "No" (Book 7, Ch59 L133-134).

### 3. Preparation

- **Rallying**: the royal family, the Irios house and the royal guard are reassured by the gods' help (Book 7, Ch65 L18); Archbishop Rimmon says each of the gods will do what they can, with the Archbishops of War and Ocean empowering defences and the sea (Book 7, Ch65 L17-19).
- **Soramir's warning**: "many of us will fall today" (Book 7, Ch65 L20).
- **The weapon**: many teams, Jason's team among them, are handed devices; only one is the true bomb, so the cult will have to guess (Book 7, Ch65 L21). Jason's team is given a dummy; Keel's crystal turns red to confirm it (Book 7, Ch65 L70-75). Liara holds the true device (Book 7, Ch65 L77).
- **The magic defences**: Farrah is a formation magic expert, wanted to breach the Builder city's defences (Book 7, Ch65 L27).
- **Travis's weapon**: Jason brought Travis into the fold to produce the weapon that destroyed the flying city (Book 7, Ch66 L44).

### 4. The battle

- **The kraken city**: the floating city is "actually a city-sized kraken construct", with ten panels opening into tentacles each a diamond-rank construct, swatting airships from the sky (Book 7, Ch64 L10-12).
- **The diamond-rankers**: the adventurers have six, with Soramir conspicuously absent (Book 7, Ch64 L14). Each faces a Builder cult diamond-ranker, and the clashes spell doom for lower-rankers nearby.
- **The lower ranks**: their objective is to invade the city, fight to its core mechanisms and destroy them, while the cult aims to bleed them (Book 7, Ch64 L15-16). The adventurers' quality tells over time (Book 7, Ch64 L17).
- **Barrier**: a diamond-ranker slays the lamprey and turns to breach the defence screen, with gold-rankers pounding it (Book 7, Ch64 L18).
- **The flying city**: a rainbow light above Rimaros disgorges a city in the sky, a stone disc with a ritual circle on its underside that fires a beam at Livaros; the gods' intervention shields Livaros (Book 7, Ch65 L28-30; Book 7, Ch66 L34). Jason notes "That is a Builder fortress-city, appearing over Livaros" (Book 7, Ch64 L72).
- **The raid on Arnote**: while forces are mobilised, pirates raid Arnote, and Pelli fights the pirate captain outside the town of Kasilaro (Book 7, Ch64 L20-25). The raiders who enter the town are struck down by black, withering death (Book 7, Ch64 L28-29).
- **The bomb**: Liara, with the siblings Ledev and Jana, carry the true device through the underground industrial depths; they join a mixed group including Vesper and Jeni Kavaloa (Book 7, Ch65 L77-80). Vesper tells her "We're close now," and the group draws attention so Liara can sneak the rest of the way (Book 7, Ch65 L83-87). Liara feels Vesper's aura wink out (Book 7, Ch65 L88). Ledev stays behind (Book 7, Ch65 L90).

### 5. Aftermath

- **A month later** the Storm Kingdom and the Adventure Society are still adjusting (Book 7, Ch66 L27).
- **Dead**: Ledev and Vesper both died saving the city, and Jeni Kavaloa died fighting alongside Vesper (Book 7, Ch66 L36).
- **Effect on adventuring**: quotas are raised and safety standards lowered; bronze-rankers were kept out of the war and are combined into larger groups sent on silver-rank missions (Book 7, Ch66 L29).
- **The other cities**: the kraken city sank to the sea floor; the flying city fell and Livaros was shielded; only minor coastal flooding hit Provo and Arnote (Book 7, Ch66 L34).
- **Jason's part**: he is at the periphery of the events; Arabelle reminds him that his intervention persuaded Dawn to move and that Travis's weapon destroyed the flying city (Book 7, Ch66 L37-44).

### 6. Open questions

- The exact casualty numbers and which diamond-rankers fought.
- The fate of the ground city beyond "burned into desolation" (Book 7, Ch66 L33).

---

## Era Guide: The Battle of Yaresh (Book 9)

### 1. Summary

After a four-month journey south from Rimaros, Jason's convoy reaches the rainforest elf city of Yaresh as messengers besiege it. The Church of Knowledge gathers an army in the background, the messengers use apocalypse beasts and world-taker worms as weapons, and the battle ends with a breach the messengers' commander Marek has to concede. Jason's identity as an astral king comes out, and Marek and Tera become his captives.

### 2. The world at the start

- **The journey**: the trip will take "about four months" (Book 9, Ch26 L26); the convoy stays at Rajoras for a few days (Book 9, Ch29 L13) and clears Cartise's ruins on the way (Book 9, Ch31 L11).
- **Orders from Knowledge**: a priest of the Church of Knowledge tells Humphrey's team to follow the Adventure Society's orders for a couple of weeks, after which the church will requisition them for the main conflict (Book 9, Ch42 L57). Clive says Knowledge's forces are mobilising (Book 9, Ch42 L80).
- **Yaresh**: the walls are a line of massive trees with glossy black stone between (Book 9, Ch43 L33). Jason says "See you in two weeks" (Book 9, Ch43 L72) and returns to the city's defence. Hana Shavar runs the refugee camp (Book 9, Ch74 L43).
- **The messengers**: the messenger architecture is obsessed with circles (Book 9, Ch55 L3); Jes Fin Kaal, the Voice of the Will, leads them (Book 9, Ch66 L3).

### 3. Key events

- **Messenger agents in the city**: Benella presents Jason to Kaal as a threat (Book 9, Ch56 L4-20); Fal Vin Garath calls Shade "Reaper spawn" (Book 9, Ch52 L32).
- **Apocalypse beasts and worms**: Carlos suspects a heat-consuming parasite with a swarm hive mind (Book 9, Ch57 L16); "[Inexorable Doom] on [World-Taker Worm]" (Book 9, Ch58 L45); the messengers know the apocalypse beasts and use them as weapons (Book 9, Ch62).
- **Defence plans**: the two cloud palaces are reconfigured into defence bunkers (Book 9, Ch76 L88); Hana directs the clergy away from Jason's bunker (Book 9, Ch80 L7).
- **The trigger**: an Adventure Society functionary hides his anger behind an aura mask (Book 9, Ch77 L13); a naga egg is triggered in the city (see ../../06_Places/Yaresh.md).
- **The breach**: Marek Nior Vargas leads the breach force over the entertainment district (Book 9, Ch86 L16); the breach is a trap (Book 9, Ch96 L14). Eilaf Hayel commands the defence of the entertainment district (Book 9, Ch94 L3).
- **Intervention**: Raythe tells Schaat "Leave Asano be. Turn around and leave this city" (Book 9, Ch97 L74); Schaat is found dead (Book 9, Ch98 L4-5).
- **The duel**: Tera Jun Casta challenges Jason; Jason's last word stops her dead (Book 9, Ch101 L18).
- **The end**: the command council advises withdrawal (Book 9, Ch102 L9); Marek orders his people in (Book 9, Ch103 L64) and later asks Jason to wait (Book 9, Ch104 L5). In the weeks following the battle, countless tons of rubble are repurposed to rebuild the city (Book 9, Ch103 L85).

### 4. Who is where

- **Jason's team**: the convoy, Taika and Zara among them (Book 9, Ch41 L119); Rufus, Sophie and Jason as mobile responders (Book 9, Ch79 L21).
- **Allies**: Charist and Allayeth, Hana Shavar, Marcus Xenoria later, the gold-rankers of Yaresh.
- **Messengers**: Kaal, Marek, Hess Jor Nasala, Fal Vin Garath, Tera; their astral king is Vesta Carmis Zell (Book 9, Ch78 L82).
- **The wider world**: Knowledge's army gathering (Book 9, Ch42 L80); Dawn's recording supplies the real list of auxiliaries (Book 9, Ch6 L41).

### 5. Known and unknown

- **Known by the end**: a healer realises Jason is a silver-rank astral king (Book 9, Ch62 L30-34).

### 6. Dangers and hooks

Besieging messengers, apocalypse beasts, world-taker worms, infiltrators in the Adventure Society. Hooks belong in ../../10_Roleplay_Support/Plot_Hooks.md.

---

## Era Guide: The Builder Cult in Greenstone (Books 2-3)

### 1. Summary

For about a year after the Reaper trials, a hidden cult of the Builder works inside Greenstone's aristocracy and through the church of Purity. It implants star seeds, suborns Thadwick Mercer and others, and prepares to claim an astral space. Jason's team learns of it in Book 2 and breaks it in Book 3, ending in the astral space the cult built and the death of Jason.

### 2. The world at the start (Book 2, about M3 to M5)

- **Where things stand**: Jason is an iron-ranker in Greenstone; Farrah has just died covering civilians (Book 1, Ch113 L41); the Adventure Society campus holds "a continual series of memorial services" (Book 2, Ch6 L3).
- **The first sign**: Cassandra says her brother Thadwick "woke up on the way back" and is "different" (Book 2, Ch4 L24); Henry Geller is among the missing faces (Book 2, Ch4 L7). A Continental Council inquiry team arrives (Book 2, Ch10 L51).
- **Rumours in the upper crust** about Jonah, Thadwick and the others disrupt Greenstone's aristocracy (Book 2, Ch16 L75).

### 3. Key events

#### Book 2

- **The star seed**: Danielle, Emir, Thalia, Arella and the archbishop of Purity witness Jonah Geller's death; the archbishop says a star seed was implanted in the boy and that star seeds are creations of entities that exist only in the deep astral (Book 2, Ch21 L19-28). Clive says he has simulated the enemy's goal: to reinforce an astral space and sever it from the world (Book 2, Ch21 L123).
- **The enemy's shape**: clockwork kings are creatures native to the world the Builder created, which make clockwork cores for constructs (Book 2, Ch25 L35-41).
- **Aristocrats in the cult**: Thalia reports that three star-seed survivors are awake with limited recollection (Book 2, Ch36 L36); a hooded cultist posing as the foreman follows a woman, and the archbishop rebukes the cult's failures (Book 2, Ch37 L24, L31, L33). Guards have stood at Thadwick's door for weeks (Book 2, Ch41 L67).
- **While the iron-rankers are away**: in Greenstone, Thalia is uneasy because most iron-rankers left a few days earlier; in the Mercers' underground black parlour, Timos berates Thadwick, whose use as an infiltrator is at an end (Book 2, Ch59 L3-22).
- **The strike**: a small force of three gold-rankers, six silvers and a dozen bronzes, including Gabriel and Arabelle Remore, hits the cult's island; Timos runs for the nearest portal device (Book 2, Ch76 L3, L58).

#### Book 3

- **Evacuation**: Zato, the last silver-ranker in the cult's local forces, leads the survivors off the island (Book 3, Ch7 L88-89).
- **Jason's capture**: Cole Silva and Lucian Lamprey orchestrate Jason's kidnapping and star seed implantation (Book 9, Ch2 L84); he is held in a stone box, emaciated and bloodied (Book 3, Ch17 L3; Book 3, Ch20 L9).
- **The war council**: Jason's team and allies meet as a war council for the anti-Builder effort (Book 3, Ch31 L56).
- **Into the astral space**: the team arrives on one of twelve portal towers ringing the city (Book 3, Ch35 L4; Book 3, Ch36 L36); a legendary-set site is a subterranean forge (Book 3, Ch42 L6). The church of Purity arrives at the Vane estate through a portal, fifty-eight in all (Book 3, Ch54 L19). Hendren dissolves under transcendent light (Book 3, Ch59 L126).
- **The truth of the place**: Clive says the astral space is artificial, "a giant boarding vessel" (Book 3, Ch65 L79).
- **The ending**: the Builder grabs Jason by the face (Book 3, Ch67 L39); Zato is defeated (Book 3, Ch69 L42); the Builder, the World-Phoenix and the Reaper negotiate (Book 3, Ch69 L279). Jason's soul is vouchsafed on his death (Book 3, Ch69 L378).

### 4. Who is where

- **Jason**: iron in Book 2, bronze by Book 3 (Book 3, Ch45 L90); dies at the climax (Book 3, Ch69).
- **Humphrey, Clive, Sophie, Belinda, Neil**: the team that enters the astral space with him (Book 3, Ch34 L16-28).
- **The cult**: Zato, Timos, Thadwick, Dougall and the abandoned vessel the Builder takes up (Book 3, Ch51 L85-93).
- **Purity**: the archbishop Nicolas Hendren and the priestess Anisa Lasalle, with fifty-eight others at the Vane estate (Book 3, Ch54 L19).
- **Greenstone's powers**: Thalia Mercer and Danielle Geller; Elspeth Arella; the Duke; Adris Dorgan as the effective mayor of Old City (Book 3, Ch26 L76).

### 5. Known and unknown

- **Known to Jason's team**: the cult exists, star seeds, and the aim to seal an astral space (Book 2, Ch21 L123).
- **Open**: how Thadwick was recruited in detail; the cult's full numbers.

### 6. Dangers and hooks

Clockwork constructs and cores; cult members hiding in plain sight among aristocrats; star seeds that can make a person into a vessel; a siege of the Vane estate. Plot hooks for this era belong in ../../10_Roleplay_Support/Plot_Hooks.md.

---

## Era Guide: Earth and the Monster Waves (Books 4-6)

### 1. Summary

Jason wakes on Earth in early June, a year and a half after he left (Book 4, Ch3 L112). Earth's magic is rising; the Network, the Cabal and the Engineers of Ascension fight for influence, monsters arrive in waves, vampires rise, and Jason builds a family compound, a clan and his first Earth domains. The era ends with Jason leaving Earth, in a warm French autumn (Book 6, Ch47 L155).

### 2. The world at the start (Book 4)

- **Where Jason is**: a maternity theatre in an abandoned hospital at Casselton West, with Shade's bodies merged into a car (Book 4, Ch1 L38; Book 4, Ch3 L80). He is bronze.
- **What has changed**: a "gas explosion" in his Melbourne building and the army running "terrorist response exercises" cover the magic (Book 4, Ch4 L87, L94). Earth's magic desolation slows his recovery (Book 4, Ch2 L8).
- **The factions**: the Network, a Cabal front man in Sydney, and the Engineers of Ascension, whom Vermillion calls those who came to magic from outside the normal channels (Book 4, Ch11 L72). The EOA has seized Perth and Melbourne (Book 4, Ch9 L76).

### 3. Key events

#### Book 4: return and the family

- **Contact**: Hiro at the Stone Wall bar in Kings Cross (Book 4, Ch4 L8); Anna Tilden and Ketevan Arziani of the Network (Book 4, Ch5; Book 4, Ch21). Jason's coins left on Anna's counter are authentic category-one coins marked PRODUCT OF JASON (Book 4, Ch22 L83-85).
- **The Starlight Rider**: the media name for his cloaked figure (Book 4, Ch15 L12).
- **Family**: he says "Hello, Eri" (Book 4, Ch26 L59), reveals magic to Emi (Book 4, Ch28 L139), and tells Kaito he has waited six years to say what he says (Book 4, Ch27 L97).
- **The Saint-Étienne rescue**: a French branch hides the Dimensional Fortress, an incursion space found in 1948 (Book 4, Ch51 L30); Jason frees Farrah from it (Book 4, Ch53; Book 4, Ch55 L8).
- **Dawn's explanation**: the link between worlds has been siphoning magic from Pallimustus for centuries, and Purity has struck a bargain with the Builder (Book 4, Ch68 L59, L139).

#### Book 5: the waves and the compound

- **Setting**: Jason has been home nine months and bronze for a year (Book 5, Ch3 L170). The family compound went up in six months (Book 5, Ch5 L59). The village committee becomes a formal body under Erika's direction (Book 5, Ch13).
- **The waves**: the first recorded monster manifestation is in Angola (Book 5, Ch13 L35). Broken Hill is hit; the safe-zone claim is blamed (Book 5, Ch17 L11, L28). Jason's broadcast: "If you want to find heroes, go digging through the rubble" (Book 5, Ch19 L26).
- **Japan**: the old Asano clan's compound in Ashiya, near Kobe (Book 5, Ch34 L66), and Noriko's challenge (Book 5, Ch36-37).
- **Transformation events**: Kostroma first (Book 5, Ch44 L48); reality cores become the prize (Book 5, Ch45 L11; see ../../09_Items_and_Artefacts/Reality_and_Genesis_Cores.md).
- **Vampires**: ancient vampires rise using blood infused with reality-core energy (Book 5, Ch55 L92; Book 5, Ch51 L127). The Berlin attack kills Kaito, Asya and Greg (Book 5, Ch51 L40).
- **Silver**: Jason ranks to silver (Book 5, Ch31 L6).

#### Book 6: the zones

- **Nitra**: a dome appears; Jason opens a door and claims territories, renaming the first Arrival Pagoda (Book 6, Ch1 L4; Book 6, Ch5 L41). 1,327 stable genesis cores are consumed in his apotheosis (Book 6, Ch17 L13).
- **Shako**: a red-haired, freckled man with bright green eyes kills Jason (Book 6, Ch17 L37); Dawn tells him he has broken the compact (Book 6, Ch18 L6).
- **Network schism**: Jason says the Slovak government caused the violence when the Network split (Book 6, Ch20 L81). Gerling goes rogue (Book 6, Ch26 L63).
- **Travis Noble** joins; the plan for a stolen backpack nuke (Book 6, Ch27 L51; Book 6, Ch28 L36).
- **Saint-Étienne**: a second zone, claimed with vampires fighting alongside (Book 6, Ch44-46); it becomes a spirit domain encompassing most of the city (Book 6, Ch47 L3).
- **Leaving**: he says his farewells in France on a warm autumn day (Book 6, Ch47 L155).

### 4. Who is where

- **Jason** on Earth, bronze then silver; **Farrah** freed and beside him; **Dawn** advising; **Shade** as car and body.
- **Family**: Ken, Cheryl, Erika, Ian, Emi, Kaito and Amy; Yumi and Hiro; later the whole clan at Asano Village (Book 5, Ch52 L68).
- **The Network** (Anna Tilden, Keith, Mr North, Gerling, Nigel Thornton's teams), **the Cabal** (Craig Vermillion, Boris), **the EOA** (the Four Cardinals; Book 5, Ch3 L5).
- **Pallimustus friends**: Rufus, Gary, Humphrey, Sophie, Neil, Belinda and Clive are not on Earth in this era; Gary says he had not seen Jason in two years (Book 6, Ch47 L941).

### 5. Known and unknown

- **Known**: magic is on Earth and rising; the link between worlds was tampered with by an outworlder who brought a door (Book 5, Ch22 L48); Jason can enter transformation events.

### 6. Dangers and hooks

Proto-spaces opening in towns, vampire occupation of Europe and Russia (Book 5, Ch66 L8), political factions that fight over cores, and the family's safety. Hooks belong in ../../10_Roleplay_Support/Plot_Hooks.md.

---

## Era Guide: The Reaper Trials (Book 2)

### 1. Summary

After Farrah's death, Emir Bahadir opens an astral space in the delta and invites iron-rank teams to compete for the legacy of the Order of the Reaper. The City of Fallen Echoes holds the trials, a shipful of time-displaced priests and a Reaper-run test of virtue. Jason's team wins a set of rewards, and the tale of the Order's survival becomes a live question.

### 2. The world at the start

- **The prize and the search**: Emir spent most of two years looking for the conditions to open the astral space (Book 2, Ch22 L82); the prize for the winning team is five legendary awakening stones (Book 2, Ch22 L90).
- **The announcement**: Emir declares Jason under his protection, explains the Order of the Reaper's contract and introduces Callum Morse (Book 2, Ch34 L45-47).
- **The mood**: Farrah's wake is held in the cloud palace's guest wing, with Padma and Farrah's parents present (Book 2, Ch38 L4, L40, L69). Emir tells the gathering "things are not so simple as I have described" (Book 2, Ch40 L57).

### 3. Key events

- **Launch**: iron-rankers board the cloud ship once the palace has resumed its shape (Book 2, Ch40 L63); adventurers spread across Sky Scar Lake by every means (Book 2, Ch41 L4). The zone is "City of Fallen Echoes (Gate Tower Three)" (Book 2, Ch43 L95).
- **Shade's account**: the legacy goes to whoever embodies the ideals of the Order (Book 2, Ch44 L39); the last grandmaster sent all the initiates into the city (Book 2, Ch44 L76); the vorger do not kill, and the initiates were turned into abominations (Book 2, Ch44 L105).
- **The city**: siege golems and flesh abominations adapt to how they are fought (Book 2, Ch45 L52; Book 2, Ch52 L34). Imran Keane joins Jason's party (Book 2, Ch53 L64). A blood root vine is hit with Inexorable Doom (Book 2, Ch54 L122).
- **Rival teams**: Padma's team is the last to join (Book 2, Ch55 L69), and Beth's team stays put beside Jason's near the end (Book 2, Ch56 L73). Before the trials Beth's and Rick's teams had met in the mirage chamber contest (Book 2, Ch29-30). All reach the city centre, where Humphrey's team staggers in ragged (Book 2, Ch55 L9-12).
- **The final day**: Shade announces that the second trial ends in one day (Book 2, Ch56 L82) and warns of nightmare hags, diamond-rank astral entities (Book 2, Ch57 L68). Rewards include a True Light (Book 2, Ch57 L142), Summoner's Die: Form (Book 2, Ch58 L41) and an Immortal Crest (Book 2, Ch58 L102).
- **The Order's truth**: Shade says the Order, "we make connections", instead of keeping a roster of assassins (Book 2, Ch60 L120), and a Reaper Token goes into Jason's inventory (Book 2, Ch60).
- **After**: Emir arrives with 419 time-displaced priests "asking for you by name" (Book 2, Ch63 L9). The Healer thanks Jason for returning their people and Jason receives the title Godless Prophet (Book 2, Ch65 L78, L85). The team spends days in Jayapura awakening abilities and getting tattoos (Book 2, Ch74 L14).

### 4. Who is where

- **Emir and Constance**: host from the cloud ship and palace (Book 2, Ch40 L57-63).
- **Jason's party**: Neil, Jory and Keane fight together for several days, and Keane is added to Jason's party (Book 2, Ch52 L71; Book 2, Ch53 L13, L64). Humphrey's team arrives separately at the centre (Book 2, Ch55 L12).
- **Other teams**: Beth's, Rick's, Padma's, plus Valdis's Team Shining Scabbard (Book 2, Ch56 L13, L73).
- **Shade**: the guide throughout (Book 2, Ch44; Book 2, Ch72 L28).

### 5. Known and unknown

- **Two accounts**: Emir says churches scoured the Order away (Book 2, Ch25 L73); Shade says it survives and the trials are a lure (Book 2, Ch44 L72-78). Treat a character's story as belief (see ../../10_Roleplay_Support/Spoiler_Management.md).
- **Open**: what the Order actually planned for the winners, and how much of Emir's search reflects his old history with Callum.

### 6. Dangers and hooks

Nightmare hags, adaptive abominations, rival teams under time pressure, and the question of what the legacy requires. Hooks belong in ../../10_Roleplay_Support/Plot_Hooks.md.

---

## Era Guide: The Rimaros Surge (Books 7-8)

### 1. Summary

Jason returns to Pallimustus at the standing stones in a cyclone, just as a long-delayed monster surge begins. In the Storm Kingdom he meets its royal family, settles at Palisaros, helps defeat the Builder's invasion in the One Day War, and then faces the Order of Redeeming Light, a mining complex rescue and the birth of his astral kingdom. The era ends with the Builder withdrawing and Jason technically an astral king.

### 2. The world at the start (Book 7)

- **How he arrives**: standing stones in concentric circles on a dirt oval on Earth (Book 7, Ch1 L5); on Pallimustus the surge is "some five years overdue" and his companions gather in a recently fortified town (Book 7, Ch1 L30, L56).
- **Where the team is**: Gary and Virid are sealed in a smithy for nineteen days (Book 7, Ch2 L76); the team arrives in Rimaros by skyship, with Farrah pointing out "the city of islands" (Book 7, Ch5 L56).
- **The Storm Kingdom**: obsessed with extreme specialisation (Book 7, Ch5 L13); Emir is not allowed in it, having stolen from the royal family (Book 7, Ch3 L51). Dawn warns that invasion platforms are appearing worldwide (Book 7, Ch8 L36).

### 3. Key events

#### Book 7: the surge and the One Day War

- **Registration and search**: Rimaros requires outside adventurers to register, with tests for Builder infiltrators (Book 7, Ch7 L58, L64); the Builder cult response team takes Jason in (Book 7, Ch11 L5).
- **The royals**: Zara's mourning period is nearly over (Book 7, Ch17 L111); Liara Rimaros and Vesper Rimaros handle him; Soramir tells him he sensed his arrival (Book 7, Ch18 L42).
- **Settling**: Jason's team reaches Palisaros and the cloud house (Book 7, Ch13 L75; Book 7, Ch57 L3). Humphrey names the team Team Biscuit (Book 7, Ch56 L74).
- **Purity stronghold**: the Ecumenical Council declares Purity fallen and a strike force is prepared (Book 7, Ch16 L51).
- **The expedition**: bone feasters attack the fortress town of Arcazitlan (Book 7, Ch25 L78); Jeni Kavaloa leads a mixed expedition into an astral aperture and seals it (Book 7, Ch47 L33; Book 7, Ch51 L5).
- **The Order**: Melody Jain leads the enclave with Sendira (Book 7, Ch58 L38).
- **The war**: Rick Geller reports a ground city of thick legs (Book 7, Ch59 L65-72); Jason's team is given a device to carry into the city, only one of which is the true bomb (Book 7, Ch65 L21). A Builder fortress-city appears over Livaros (Book 7, Ch64). It had been a month since the War of Four Cities, or the One Day War (Book 7, Ch66 L27).
- **The cost**: Ledev and Vesper both died saving the city (Book 7, Ch66 L36).

#### Book 8: the Order and the throne

- **The Order**: Sendira, Fila and Ramona raid; the Order travels by underwater vehicle (Book 8, Ch9 L3, L11). Liara leads an expedition into the Builder complex (Book 8, Ch7 L4).
- **Casallini**: a gang is hired against Sophie (Book 8, Ch24-26; see ../../06_Places/Minor_Places/Casallini.md).
- **The mining complex**: below the sea floor; Korinne Pescos leads the rescue (Book 8, Ch30 L3; Book 8, Ch31 L33).
- **The throne and gate**: the Fundament Gate is broken down (Book 8, Ch49 L87), an incomplete spirit realm consumes the Firmament Core, and the astral throne lets avatars exist in his domains (Book 8, Ch49 L105; Book 8, Ch50 L65).
- **The Builder's deal**: Jason tells the Builder "I want them gone. Today" (Book 8, Ch54 L63); the Builder withdraws his forces (Book 8, Ch56 L35). Weeks later the Builder's forces abandon the Storm Kingdom as a battlefront (Book 8, Ch59 L6).
- **Aftermath**: for two months Jason sits in his house "telling the most powerful people in the city to bog off" (Book 8, Ch62). Dawn says he is an astral king because he has an astral throne and gate (Book 8, Ch65 L88).

### 4. Who is where

- **Jason**: Arnote and Palisaros; later astral king (Book 8, Ch65).
- **Team Biscuit**: Humphrey, Sophie, Neil, Belinda, Clive and Gary, with Rufus and Farrah (Book 7, Ch56 L74).
- **Royals**: Soramir, Liara, Vesper and Zara (Book 7, Ch17 L111; Book 7, Ch18 L42); the Storm King rises from the throne to look down at Jason (Book 7, Ch59 L86).
- **Enemies**: the Builder and his forces, the Order of Redeeming Light (Melody, Sendira, Laront), Purity loyalists.
- **Elsewhere**: Dawn advising; Travis at the largest Artifice Association workshop in Rimaros (Book 7, Ch60 L94).

### 5. Known and unknown

- **Known**: the Builder's invasion platforms (Book 7, Ch8 L36); the Church of Death's certificate says Jason has died four times (Book 7, Ch17 L104).

### 6. Dangers and hooks

Invasion platforms and fortress-cities, bone feasters, Purity cutouts, a political royal court, and a mining complex hiding prisoners. Hooks belong in ../../10_Roleplay_Support/Plot_Hooks.md.

---

## Awakening Stones

### 1. Summary

Single-use stones that awaken an essence ability, found, traded and rewarded by rank and rarity.

### 2. Facts

- **What they do**: "An awakening stone that unlocks the power of consumption... Effect: Awakens an essence ability", requiring an unawakened essence ability (Book 1, Ch11 L17-20). The stone melts into the user's hand "with little fanfare" (Book 1, Ch22 L70).
- **Why they matter**: Rufus tells Jason he cannot "properly progress towards bronze rank until you awaken every essence ability you have. That's all twenty" (Book 1, Ch22 L68).
- **Naming and rarity**: stones are "unranked" and carry a rarity, such as the Awakening Stone of the Feast (common, Book 1, Ch11 L17), of Adventure (rare, Book 1, Ch22 L99), of the Stars (epic, Book 1, Ch4 L44) and of the Reaper (legendary, Book 2, Ch57 L31).
- **Affinity**: the Reaper stone "sharing affinity with the Reaper" is used in Jason's awakening ritual with the Reaper token (Book 2, Ch57 L31-33; Book 2, Ch63 L65), and it gives Humphrey, a human, a special attack (Book 2, Ch64 L72).
- **Divine awakening stones**: a god-made stone is "transcendent rank, epic" and bestows a specific aura power (Book 2, Ch13 L60).
- **Sources**: looting an awakening stone or essence can pay better than the contract to kill the monster (Book 1, Ch57 L21); the Adventure Society gives stones for exceptional service, "although rarely", and some open contracts offer them as rewards (Book 1, Ch64 L81); otherwise stones are bought through brokers, almost always at auction, at exorbitant prices (Book 1, Ch64 L82). Brokers in a small city hold the valuable stuff, like essences and stones, for big auctions (Book 1, Ch61 L36).
- **Family supply**: the Gellers traditionally supply their scions with enough stones to get started, while the rest must be earned (Book 1, Ch64 L80).
- **Restricted-essence trades**: Adventure Society representatives offer good trades for restricted essences, as when Sophie trades a death essence for two epic stones (Book 2, Ch65 L96).

### 3. Using this in a story

Awakening stones are the currency of early progression. Scarcity and who can afford a broker's auction drive many scenes in the delta.

---

## Badges and Guild Membership

### 1. Summary

The badge as an object: how an Adventure Society badge is made, tied to its wearer, tracked, altered and replaced, and what a guild membership adds on top. The organisation file explains what the Society does with badges; this file is about the item and the ways it fails.

### 2. Facts

- **Who makes it**: the Adventure Society manages the badges but the Magic Society creates them, taking an aura imprint from the new member inside a magic circle in about a minute (Book 1, Ch62 L46).
- **What it carries**: every badge is paired with a tracking stone that tells the Society whether the member is alive or dead and finds them if they go missing (Book 1, Ch62 L47). Members claim adventure notices with it (Book 1, Ch62 L44) and hold it against a security screen to close a contract (Book 1, Ch107 L51).
- **The wallet**: the badge is a circular iron medallion embossed with a sword and rod crossed over a shield, carried in a leather wallet (Book 1, Ch62 L61).
- **Stars are on the metal**: an official taps a wedge-shaped stone to the badge and a star is added (Book 1, Ch98 L82), or removed (Book 2, Ch12 L83).
- **It tracks the badge, not the person**: the tracking stones trace the badges, so a discarded badge hides its owner (Book 3, Ch35 L43, L45). A change of aura leaves the imprint on the badge unmatched, so the wearer cannot be tracked until a new badge is issued (Book 2, Ch15 L10). When the Society wanted people's auras re-examined, their badges were also to be replaced (Book 2, Ch10 L85).
- **Jason's case**: the stone cannot track his aura imprint, and Farrah says some people have abilities that block magical tracking (Book 1, Ch62 L47-48).
- **A new badge for a changed aura**: when Jason's aura and personal crest change, he is told to present himself to the Magic Society for a new badge (Book 3, Ch24 L89).
- **Using the tracking**: adventurers find dead colleagues by following a crystal orb that points to the badge (Book 1, Ch84 L101; Book 1, Ch87 L39).

### 4. Using this in a story

A badge is leverage as much as identification. It can summon help or find a body, and an unmatched badge makes a character untraceable. A character who has changed rank or aura needs a new one from the Magic Society, so a missing or old badge is a reasonable sign of trouble. Guild membership is a separate matter with its own politics; do not treat it as a rank.

---

## Cloud Constructs and Cloud Palaces

### 1. Summary

Emir's cloud ship and palace, and Jason's own cloud houses, yachts and palaces made from his Cloud Flask.

### 2. Facts

- **Emir's cloud ship**: it arrives at the Greenstone dock and a walkway of cloud connects to the shore as a hole opens in its side (Book 1, Ch101 L64). Moored, it morphs over about ten minutes into a palace of clouds floating on the water (Book 1, Ch101 L103).
- **Emir's cloud palace**: floating just offshore on the north side of the Island, "fully exposed to the waves and currents, yet remained as immovable as solid ground", with wings and towers in blue, purple, orange and gold (Book 1, Ch108 L3). Walls, floors and ceilings glow with absorbed sunlight the palace stores and distributes, and the floors have a bouncy-castle spring (Book 2, Ch1 L3).
- **Low-magic limits**: out of Greenstone the ship's full functionality is restored when Emir's cloud ship floats above Kurdansk (Book 6, Ch47 L459).
- **Team base**: Jason's team move into Emir's palace after the contest (Book 2, Ch8 L168).
- **Jason's Cloud Flask**: it creates buildings and vehicles of cloud, with forms limited by rank, iron cloud houses, bronze cloud vehicles and silver cloud palaces (Book 6, Ch4 L42-50); items inside a returned construct are stored in a dimensional space until another is formed (Book 2, Ch74 L43).
- **Asano Village**: Farrah prepares a place for Jason's cloud house at the end of a glass tunnel along the seafloor, two kilometres out, reached by a tramway from the compound and sealed by a wall of water, so the house can run underwater (Book 5, Ch5 L106-118); the text checked does not show the house later sitting there.

### 3. Using this in a story

A cloud palace is both home and statement; Emir's flair is the model for how much a palace can show off.

---

## Consumables, Trial Rewards and Crafting Materials

### 1. Summary

Single-use and crafting items: the City of Fallen Echoes rewards, clockwork cores, reality cores and star seeds.

### 2. Facts

- **Lesser Miracle Potion**: "iron rank, legendary", "Salvation in a bottle", it fully restores health, mana and stamina but only works on normal and iron-rank people, and its lingering magic delays other recovery (Book 2, Ch44 L127-129).
- **Medallion of Escape**: "silver rank, uncommon", evacuates the holder from the astral space when their aura is projected into it, and only works inside the City of Fallen Echoes (Book 2, Ch44 L120-123).
- **Immortal Crest**: "iron rank, rare", lets a mystical tattooist draw out a soul crest and only works on an iron-rank essence user (Book 2, Ch58 L102-104).
- **Summoner's Die: Form**: "iron rank [growth], legendary", it randomly alters the form of an iron-rank summon (Book 2, Ch58 L53-56).
- **Clockwork Core**: "iron rank, rare", the core of an artificial monster that simplifies the materials and processes of a construct creature; clockwork cores are made by clockwork kings (Book 2, Ch25 L24-26, L35).
- **Star seeds**: great astral beings, not gods, "can send their followers star seeds"; once implanted and germinated the body transforms, "minor or major" (Book 2, Ch21 L26-28).
- **Reality cores**: roughly the size and shape of an ostrich egg, dark crystal glowing with transcendent light; one appears in each affected zone and the factions scramble for it (Book 5, Ch45 L9-11). They are the source of the transformation zones' treasures and are common in node space (Book 6, Ch1 L20).

### 3. Using this in a story

These rewards are meant to be spent. A potion or medallion is a story decision, not an inventory slot.

---

## Desert Transport: Skimmers and Sand Barges

### 1. Summary

The vehicles that cross the desert around Greenstone: the sand skimmers and sand barges Emir Bahadir's people run, the carriages that move expeditions to relay stations, and the monstrous Ustei war barge. Emir's cloud ship is covered in Cloud_Constructs_and_Palaces.md.

### 2. Facts

- **Sand skimmers**: small desert vehicles that carry a party across the sand. Emir leaves skimmers waiting at Boko to carry the team into the desert (Book 1, Ch109 L21).
- **Pushed past their limits**: one of Emir's people specialises in magical tools and can periodically push the skimmers to speeds beyond their normal capability, so the whole vehicle vibrates (Book 1, Ch110 L4).
- **Their limits**: skimmers cannot go through an astral-space aperture, so the party continues on foot (Book 1, Ch110 L13).
- **Requisitioned skimmers**: expeditions requisition extra skimmers from the city and return them afterwards (Book 1, Ch113 L45, L52).
- **Sand barges**: larger vessels that move people and supplies overland. After Emir's ship sails south down the coast and into shore at an unremarkable patch of desert, his people unload three sand barges that carry the whole group inland to Sky Scar Lake (Book 2, Ch40 L80). The trip takes hours more, into the night (Book 2, Ch40 L81).
- **Expedition transport**: an expedition leaving Greenstone uses carriages to reach desert relay stations, then the captured Ustei barge and smaller barges supplied by the Magic Society (Book 1, Ch98 L4).
- **The Ustei war barge**: it is easily the size of a passenger liner, built on the skeletons of leviathan creatures, with three giant skulls on its flat-nosed bow, and the entire Ustei tribe lives in it (Book 1, Ch90 L14). None of Emir's barges is its size (Book 2, Ch40 L80).
- **Carriages on the Island**: adventurers travel to the Island in a train of carriages (Book 2, Ch32 L38).

### 3. Using this in a story

Desert travel in this world is a logistics problem solved by specialists. A skimmer carries a party across the sand but cannot enter an aperture, a barge carries a whole group and arrives after dark, and a tribe on a barge is a town on the move. Emir's people run most of it.

---

## Dimensional Storage and Portals

### 1. Summary

How things and people are carried across a world without carts or roads: storage spaces that hold a person's goods, dimensional bags that expand them, and portal abilities that move people between places they have seen. Jason's own inventory and shadow gates are the best-documented examples, and the rest of the world treats both as everyday professional tools with hard limits.

### 2. Facts

- **Jason's inventory** is "a video-game inventory only Jason could see", unlike Clive's storage, which is opened by making a circle of floating runes with a portal inside (Book 2, Ch7 L47).
- **Dimensional bags** are items with a rank. They add slots to an inventory in proportion to their rank, and at iron rank one bag slot is available and holds only iron-rank bags (Book 1, Ch76 L43, L49). Bags come in novelty shapes, such as a pair of boots (Book 1, Ch76 L58).
- **Storage as a profession**: adventurers carry their possessions in dimensional bags or dimensional space abilities and travel light (Book 2, Ch32 L38). Some portal workers "aren't even adventurers and do storage space transport for a living" (Book 7, Ch20 L23).
- **Loot goes straight in**: looted coins appear in the storage abilities of Clive, Humphrey and Jason (Book 2, Ch42 L79), and Jason keeps a ledger of how much belongs to whom (Book 2, Ch19 L60).
- **Storage can be looted**: Jason could have taken the items from Farrah's storage space by looting her body like a monster, and neither man suggested such a defilement (Book 1, Ch112 L17).
- **Limits on contents**: it is "almost impossible to place living material inside dimensional storage", with only a few carefully chosen plants viable (Book 2, Ch24 L13).
- **Portal-type abilities**: they open only to places the user has visited before, along with other limits (Book 1, Ch113 L58). A portal fails if the destination has changed enough that the visualisation is wrong; redecorating is fine, demolishing the building is not (Book 3, Ch39 L37).
- **Jason's shadow teleport**: the iron-rank effect of Path of Shadows is "Teleport using shadows as a portal. You must be able to see the destination shadow" (Book 1, Ch22 L120).
- **Archways**: the portal arches of the Order of the Reaper's astral space are built on the same ability (Book 3, Ch7 L85), and the shadow gates there look like Jason's own (Book 3, Ch29 L97). Travelling through a dark aperture feels exactly like his shadow teleport (Book 2, Ch43 L24).
- **Stability**: a portal may not stay stable long, so travellers follow quickly (Book 3, Ch33 L62).

### 3. Later developments (spoilers)

- **Capacity and cooldown**: portalling a silver-ranker uses all of Jason's portal's capacity, so the others wait out a ten-minute cooldown (Book 5, Ch24 L135).
- **Magically sound destinations**: the destination must be sound, while the start point can be shakier because the user is present and the ability adapts through the user's senses (Book 8, Ch39 L16).
- **Beacons and range**: some portal abilities can target places never visited if an aura-based marker is there to home in on (Book 8, Ch16 L90). Jason's ability to skip the visit works at less than a tenth of his normal portal range (Book 8, Ch26 L5).
- **The Builder cult's network**: the cult and the Church of Purity moved about through a transportation network of portal arches (Book 6, Ch47 L640), which the Magic Society cracked and could track (Book 7, Ch16 L73).
- **Contract use**: a contract in the Storm Kingdom would deploy Jason's team widely to give them many portal destinations for supply runs and rapid response (Book 7, Ch58 L10). Portal specialists of gold rank are in demand (Book 7, Ch47 L62).

### 4. Using this in a story

Treat storage as a luggage problem solved at a cost: anything alive is awkward, and a storage space holds what is lost with its owner if the owner can be looted. Treat portals as a map problem: the party can only be where someone has been, and a changed destination fails the portal. A portal specialist is a job, not just a power, and the scarcity of gold-rank specialists is a reason for delays in any story that wants them.

---

## Hegemon's Will and Hegemon's Dominion

### 1. Summary

The reforged form of Jason's sword Dread Salvation and the scabbard that was made with it. Hegemon's Will is the weapon and Hegemon's Dominion is the scabbard; the game treats them as one item. The scabbard stores afflictions that land on Jason and turns them against the next enemy he cuts. Soul_Bound_Items_and_Regalia.md covers where the sword came from; this file covers what the two pieces do.

### 2. Facts

- **Hegemon's Dominion**: "silver rank [growth], legendary", "the scabbard to Hegemon's Will" and "the embodiment of hegemonic control, representing the hegemon's mastery of his domain" (Book 7, Ch54 L118-119).
- **Bound to Jason**: it cannot be used by anyone else, shares his ability to ignore rank disparity, and is "treated as the same item" as the sword for all magical effects (Book 7, Ch54 L120).
- **What the scabbard does**: afflictions affecting the wielder are periodically moved to it, which removes their effects from him but is not a cleansing effect. The rate depends on the effects' rank relative to the wielder; suppressing the originator's aura speeds the transfer and being aura suppressed slows it. If the sword is sheathed, all afflictions pass to the weapon and affect the next enemy struck. Anything the target resists or is immune to is negated and cannot be passed to another target (Book 7, Ch54 L121).
- **Growth**: its growth is no longer capped by rank, but the upgrade materials are ones Jason has never heard of and are presumably gold rank (Book 7, Ch54 L123).
- **Gary's account**: the crafting was strange and "it's almost like the sword knew how it wanted to be reforged" (Book 7, Ch54 L124). Jason says that after a recent lost fight the scabbard's ability is exactly what he needed (Book 7, Ch54 L125).
- **Hegemon's Will**: "silver rank [growth], legendary", "reforged with a renewed sense of purpose" with the help of a grandmaster craftsman, bound to Jason, and sharing his ability to ignore rank disparity (Book 7, Ch54 L128-130).
- **Invoking conjured weapons**: the wielder may invoke all effects of one conjured weapon into the blade at the weapon's normal conjuring cost, one at a time, and add extra damage for an ongoing mana cost scaled by aura strength (Book 7, Ch54 L131-132). Available invocations are Ruin, the Blade of Tribulation (low cost; the item screen lists corrosive damage and Corrosion, Book 7, Ch54 L133-135) and Penitent, the Blade of Sacrifice (moderate cost; disruptive-force damage, Book 7, Ch54 L136-138). The invocation message for Ruin says necrotic damage will be inflicted, not corrosive (Book 7, Ch54 L146).
- **Mercy and Benevolence**: the wielder may cleanse the holy afflictions his own abilities and soul-bound items inflicted on a touched enemy. Each cleansed affliction gives the enemy an instance of Hegemon's Mercy and the wielder an instance of Benevolent Hegemon (Book 7, Ch54 L139). Hegemon's Mercy is a holy affliction that suppresses all magical abilities and drops off rapidly outside the wielder's aura; Benevolent Hegemon strengthens overlapping allied auras (Book 7, Ch54 L142-143).
- **Set effect**: as part of the Regalia, enemies struck are drained of mana and inflicted with Hegemon's Tribute, and the wielder gains Benevolent Hegemon (Book 7, Ch54 L140, L144).
- **Visible effect**: the white sigils on the blade glow life-force red for Ruin and blue for Penitent (Book 7, Ch54 L145-149).
- **In use, Book 8**: Hegemon's Will prevents a conjuration and gains all the properties of the conjured weapon (Book 8, Ch31 L6); an affliction that cannot be resisted is moved into the scabbard, and returning the sword to it makes the next attack inflict that affliction (Book 8, Ch34 L63).

### 4. Using this in a story

Treat the two pieces as a pair: the sword delivers, the scabbard stores. A sheathed blade is a loaded one. The scabbard converts Jason's pain into the next victim's problem, so a fight against afflictions suits him better than a fight against raw damage. Resisted or immune effects do not pass on, which is the limit a clever opponent can use. When the sword's damage type matters, quote the screen or the message rather than choosing between them.

---

## Jason's Combat Robes

### 1. Summary

Jason's combat robes are the flowing, tailored armour that gives him his dark, shadowy look. The first is the Trap Weaver Battle Robe, bought from Gilbert Bertinelli in Book 1; it is replaced at each rank by a new robe made by Gilbert from monster materials Jason supplies. The robes are armour in a robe's cut, stored in his inventory and swapped on by the outfits tab.

### 2. The Trap Weaver Battle Robe (iron rank)

- **Where it comes from**: Gilbert sells it, with Gary haggling the price, in bronze coins, which is unusual for iron-rank equipment (Book 1, Ch61 L92-114). It is made from trap weaver silk, alchemically treated, with leather panels, "the magic integrated right down to the weaving pattern of the cloth" (Book 1, Ch61 L92).
- **Look**: "almost a robe", in shifting shades of dark grey, with black leather panels over the torso, arms and legs and flowing cloth draped over it; "a strange combination of tactical armour from Jason's world and some kind of wizard robe" (Book 1, Ch61 L93). The drape breaks up the body's lines in the dark (Book 1, Ch61 L99).
- **Item entry**: "[Trap Weaver Battle Robe] (iron rank, epic)", full body armour of silk and leather (Book 1, Ch61 L102-103).
- **Effects**: increased resistance to damage, strong against cutting and piercing but weaker against blunt; repairs damage over time, though extensive damage may need external repair; absorbs blood so it leaves no trail; resists bleed and poison; resists adhesives; adapts its fit to the wearer (Book 1, Ch61 L104-109).
- **Source monster**: trap weavers are iron-rank monsters "responsible for more iron-rank adventurer deaths than any other monster in the Greenstone region" (Book 1, Ch58 L40). Jason later offers Sophie the same silk through his tailor (Book 2, Ch21 L12).

### 3. Bronze: the Dark Hydra Robe

Jason asks about the next robe once he nears bronze (Book 3, Ch2 L75). Gilbert makes it from umbral mountain snake leather Jason supplied, marsh hydra leather and a lining of deep wyrm silk (Book 3, Ch32 L45-46). The entry reads "[Dark Hydra Robe] (bronze rank, epic)" (Book 3, Ch32 L48-49). It is darker than the old robe: mostly scaled matte black with grey trim, keeping the sleek draping lines (Book 3, Ch32 L45). Jason wears it as his bronze-rank battle robe (Book 3, Ch48 L10).

### 4. Later robes

- Jason's silver-era robe is again custom-made by Gilbert; in Book 5 its magic has died after Jason pushed it close to destruction many times (Book 5, Ch30 L58).
- The robes change with Jason's ranks; the old iron-rank robe had much more grey than the black bronze one (Book 4, Ch25 L80).

### 5. How they work in play

- **Outfits tab**: Jason slots armour into an outfit in his inventory and taps equip; smoke surrounds him and the clothes swap (Book 1, Ch61 L115; Book 2, Ch14 L58).
- **Self-repair**: a robe recovers to pristine condition in about four days, and it is cleaned with the rest of the wearer (Book 3, Ch14 L27-29).
- **Recognised as adventurer dress**: workers see the combat robes and weapons and know better than to interfere (Book 1, Ch93 L85).
- **Fit for combat**: loose but designed so it does not tangle in wheels (Book 4, Ch14 L28).
- **Trade-off**: light robes give up protectiveness for flexibility, a weakness Jason uses against Hendren's identical style (Book 3, Ch59 L87).

### 6. Open questions

- The robes after the Book 5 failure (silver and gold-era names and effects) are not catalogued in the lines read.

---

## Reality Cores and Genesis Cores

### 1. Summary

Reality cores are egg-sized crystals of concentrated power found at transformation events on Earth. Factions fight over them, vampires use them to power their blood, and Jason's work shuts the supply off. Genesis cores are different: Jason loots them from anomalies in the zones to expand his spirit domain.

### 2. Reality cores

- **What they are**: "roughly the size and shape of an ostrich egg", dark crystal glowing with internal transcendent light (Book 5, Ch45 L11); "batteries for the universe", fuelling the nodes of reality (Book 5, Ch42 L54).
- **Where they appear**: each transformation event reveals a single reality core (Book 5, Ch48 L71). Jason's door gives him ready access to a treasure trove of them under the landscape, which he leaves where they are (Book 5, Ch48 L71).
- **Use**: they offer a path forward to those bottlenecked at the top of power (Book 5, Ch43 L66). The power can be consumed directly with a simple ritual (Book 5, Ch47 L111) and is being tested for category-four suppression collars (Book 5, Ch49 L22). They have already pushed two near-category-four users over the threshold (Book 5, Ch68 L8).
- **Factions**: the Cabal claims the cores in Kostroma and Africa, the Network the one in the USA (Book 5, Ch45 L11). The fight over cores strains cooperation (Book 5, Ch45 L23). The Network keeps a secure store (Book 5, Ch53 L31).

### 3. Genesis cores

- **Stable Genesis Cores**: unranked, common items looted from anomalies in a zone (Book 6, Ch2 L21, L36). Jason uses them to expand his spirit domain; the cost rises as the domain grows (Book 6, Ch2 L39; Book 6, Ch5 L9; Book 6, Ch6 L35; Book 6, Ch7 L76).
- **Unstable Genesis Cores**: after corpses changed the cores they carried, refining them to a usable state, Jason reasons that since the ones he looted were stable, these must be the unstable variety (Book 6, Ch8 L3).
- **Genesis Reclamation Core**: see ../06_Places/Astral_Spaces/Transformation_Zones.md for the zone-level mechanics.

### 4. Open questions

- The full list of genesis core varieties and their uses in the Cardinas zone.

---

## Soul-Bound Items and the Regalia of the Dark Hegemon

### 1. Summary

Jason's three soul-bound growth items, joined in Book 6 into the Regalia of the Dark Hegemon by the Soul-Imprinting Triune, plus the Robes of the Astral Verdict.

### 2. Facts

- **Dread Salvation**: a sword "crafted with gratitude, in hope it would be of the greatest use in the moment of greatest need"; "iron rank [growth], legendary"; when an affliction special attack meets a physical immunity it gains Stone Cutter, and for a magical immunity Spell Breaker (Book 1, Ch56 L49-52). Gary made it, as a gift (Book 1, Ch56 L47).
- **Dread Salvation in Book 6**: bronze rank, bound to Jason and sharing his ability to ignore rank disparity; it can be imbued with aura for damage at an ongoing mana cost; "The original creator must demonstrate his growth and reforge the weapon" (Book 6, Ch4 L72-77). The window says it "must be reforged by the original craftsperson in order to advance further" (Book 6, Ch4 L82). The reforged sword is named Hegemon's Will on its item screen, with its scabbard Hegemon's Dominion treated as the same item; Gary gives it to Jason in Book 7 (Book 7, Ch54 L108-120). The reforging by Gary and Virid is Book 7, Ch2 L73-74.
- **Cloud Flask**: iron rank growth item that Jason binds in Book 2; it creates cloud buildings and vehicles, with forms limited by rank (Book 2, Ch74 L34-46). By Book 6 it is silver rank, with the cloud house at iron, cloud vehicle at bronze and cloud palace at silver (Book 6, Ch4 L42-50). Its maker is "some unknown diamond-rank craftsman" (Book 6, Ch4 L40).
- **Amulet of the Dark Guardian**: silver rank growth item, the last item produced by his old quest system, a reproduction of his personal crest on an obsidian chain (Book 6, Ch4 L29-39). Its boon Guardian's Blessing absorbs damage per affliction applied and can be given to another person by touch (Book 6, Ch4 L34-37).
- **Soul-Imprinting Triune**: Jason receives it with a hoard of spirit coins in Book 5 (Book 5, Ch29 L77-80); it turns three soul-bound growth items into a set when all three are on his person (Book 6, Ch4 L6-8).
- **Regalia of the Dark Hegemon**: the set bonuses are Hegemon's Authority from the amulet (allies in his aura resist aura suppression, and he can spend instances to strengthen his own suppression), a mist shroud from the cloud flask, and mana drain with Hegemon's Tribute from the sword (Book 6, Ch4 L35-38, L47, L79-80).
- **Robes of the Astral Verdict**: bronze rank, rare cloth armour for summoners that boosts dimension spells, summon damage reduction and disruptive-force resistance (Book 1, Ch7 L17-21).

### 3. Using this in a story

Keep the three Regalia items together on his person; losing any one breaks the set. The Regalia window states that Dread Salvation must be reforged to advance (Book 6, Ch4 L82); the reforging happens in Book 7 (Book 7, Ch2 L73-74; Book 7, Ch54 L108-120).

---

## The Builder's Door and the Fundament Gate

### 1. Summary

The Builder's astral door, the Fundament Gate that Jason absorbs, and how they let him work in the fundamental realm.

### 2. Facts

- **The door**: Mr North tells Jason "It's not a literal door... It's an astral construct with the power to manifest in physical reality in the form of a portal", and that Jason must absorb it so it cannot be taken from him (Book 5, Ch41 L24-26). Jason notes "This door is an object of the Builder" (Book 5, Ch41 L28).
- **The Fundament Gate**: a ring of rough marble standing stones in a dell, "transcendent rank, legendary", whose window lets Jason incorporate it into his Spirit Vault, purging the Builder's influence (Book 5, Ch41 L52-62). It improves his sense of Builder items and adds to Path of Shadows the ability to open a portal to the Fundamental Realm (Book 5, Ch41 L62-64).
- **Consequences**: Jason used the Builder's door when he flooded himself with magic and later "accidentally trashed it for parts" (Book 8, Ch57 L28, L64-67); "the magic of the Builder's door that Jason had absorbed" is distinct from the soul-attack trick he learned from the star seed (Book 7, Ch50 L8).
- **Builder's view**: in Book 6 Shako says that without the Fundament Gate the Builder forged, Jason could not have remade the zone (Book 6, Ch18 L11, L30-36), and Dawn asks for the designs so the World-Phoenix can create a complementary item to anchor the bridge (Book 6, Ch18 L39, L124).
- **Later items**: the Fundamental Realm Authority Token and the Firmamental Bridge Anchor are looted from the destroyed artefacts (Book 8, Ch50 L94-100); see `Tokens_Stones_and_Relics.md`.

### 3. Using this in a story

Treat the door as borrowed authority: powerful, traceable to the Builder and always one dispute away from being taken back.

---

## Tokens, Stones and Relics

### 1. Summary

Transcendent-rank items that mark the favour or authority of great astral beings and gods: astral blessing tokens, divine awakening stones, the Fundamental Realm Authority Token and the Cup of Heroes.

### 2. Facts

- **Astral blessing tokens**: Clive says they are "something great astral beings give out to bestow blessings, as signs of approval"; they trigger racial gift evolutions, and are harmless, unlike star seeds (Book 2, Ch63 L45-47).
- **World-Phoenix Token**: Jason finds it in his starting inventory, "transcendent rank, legendary", "Uses remaining: 1/1" with unreadable effects (Book 1, Ch1 L103-107). It is consumed in Book 3 (Book 3, Ch69 L108).
- **Reaper Token**: an obsidian square engraved with a silver scythe, "transcendent rank, legendary" (Book 2, Ch63 L37-38). Jason takes the Scythe of the Reaper by placing his inventory window behind it, and the token is added (Book 2, Ch60 L173-179). It is melded into his body with the awakening stone of the Reaper in Clive's ritual and consumed (Book 2, Ch63 L65-82).
- **Knowledge's token**: a small stone square like the World-Phoenix Token but washed-out blue, offered with two awakening stones (Book 2, Ch13 L57); Jason is told it would take him home and wonders whether to trust it (Book 2, Ch73 L58).
- **Divine Awakening Stone of Inevitability**: "transcendent rank, epic", crafted by a god to bestow a specific aura power (Book 2, Ch13 L60). The stone's effect line names the ability [Inescapable Doom] (Book 2, Ch13 L62) but its ability description names [Inevitable Demise] (Book 2, Ch13 L66); the compendium uses Inevitable Demise and treats the other as a variant, since no later list shows either. The Soul-Purgation Tablet (aura) removes an existing aura essence ability and cannot be forcibly used on another (Book 2, Ch13 L76-78).
- **Firmamental Bridge Anchor**: the other looted item, "transcendent rank, legendary", with unreadable effects and one use (Book 8, Ch50 L97-100).

### 3. Using this in a story

These items are leverage between beings, not loot; their descriptions should stay incomplete until the owner is strong enough to read them.

---

## Era Selection

### 1. Summary

Seven playable eras, each with its calendar, setting, live dangers and the points where the world changes.

### 2. Facts

**Era 1: Greenstone (Books 1-2, about M0-M6, late 2017 to spring 2018)**
- **Calendar**: Jason arrives in late November (Book 4, Ch3 L112); Leust's lumber conspiracy falls in late summer moving into autumn (Book 1, Ch93 L5).
- **Scale**: iron rank; Jason's badge is iron with one star (Book 1, Ch62 L66-67). Field testing is held at the start of every month (Book 1, Ch38 L158).
- **Live dangers**: a traditional blood cult in the hedge-maze estate (Book 1, Ch9 L50); the Ustei raiding spirit coin convoys (Book 1, Ch83 L4); Old City's "Big Three" crime lords (Book 1, Ch50 L92).
- **Turning points**: Farrah dies covering civilians (Book 1, Ch113 L41); Emir's contest and the City of Fallen Echoes free 419 time-displaced priests (Book 2, Ch63 L9).
- **Spoiler level**: 0-1.

**Era 2: Bronze and the Builder (Book 3, about M6-M18)**
- **Calendar**: the book opens in a mild Greenstone winter (Book 3, Ch1 L14).
- **Scale**: Path of Shadows reaches bronze (Book 3, Ch29 L23); the Reaper-trial astral space has twelve towers about eight kilometres apart (Book 3, Ch36 L36).
- **Live dangers**: the church of Purity under an ecumenical inquiry (Book 3, Ch1 L105); star seeds, implanted through the cult (Book 2, Ch21 L26-28); energy ghouls (Book 3, Ch62 L23).
- **Turning point**: Jason dies covering the team's escape (Book 3, Ch69 L900). **Spoiler level**: 1.

**Era 3: Earth (Books 4-6, about June 2019 to autumn 2021)**
- **Calendar**: Jason wakes on Earth in early June, a year and a half after leaving (Book 4, Ch3 L112); Book 5 opens about nine months after that (Book 5, Ch3 L170); a cold winter day in Book 6 (Book 6, Ch31 L4) and a warm autumn farewell (Book 6, Ch47 L155).
- **Scale**: bronze at the start, silver by the Book 5 chapter "Silver" (Book 5, Ch31 L1).
- **Live dangers**: the EOA has seized Perth and Melbourne (Book 4, Ch9 L76); the Network is publicly known as the Global Defense Network after the monster waves begin (Book 5, Ch17 L7); transformation zones begin with Kostroma (Book 5, Ch44 L48-49); gold-rankers explore Jason's Nitra domain a month after the dome falls (Book 6, Ch23 L3).
- **Spoiler level**: 2.

**Era 4: Rimaros (Books 7-8, from the arrival to about the surge's end)**
- **Calendar**: Book 7 opens at the standing stones (Book 7, Ch1 L5, L16); the surge is "some five years overdue" (Book 7, Ch1 L56).
- **Scale**: silver rank in the Storm Kingdom, where sky islands house the cream of society (Book 7, Ch5 L65).
- **Live dangers**: a hundred and nineteen outworlders appear at once (Book 7, Ch36 L86); the One Day War, after which "It had been a month" (Book 7, Ch66 L27); the Order of Redeeming Light converting towns (Book 8, Ch9 L40).
- **Turning point**: the Astral Throne and Gate appear (Book 8, Ch49 L105). **Spoiler level**: 2.

**Era 5: Yaresh and the messengers (Books 9-10, Jason aged twenty-nine)**
- **Scale**: silver-rank adventurers and gold and diamond power brokers in a small elf city (Book 9, Ch42 L26).
- **Spoiler level**: 2-3.

- **Spoiler level**: 3.

- **Spoiler level**: 3.

### 3. Using this in a story

- **Choosing**: for a first-time scene pick Era 1 or Era 3, where the rules of the world are still being taught. For a high-power story pick Era 7, where a single decision touches whole regions.
- **Staying in the era**: use the character file's "valid from" and "valid through" lines to decide who can appear, and the place files for what a location looks like at the time.
- **Mixing eras**: do not let a character use a power or know a secret from a later era unless the scene deliberately sits after that book.

---

## NPC Rosters by Era

### 1. Summary

Who can appear in each era, with one line showing where each person is on the page. Everyone listed has an individual file in `../07_Characters/` (except where noted).

### 2. Facts

**Era 1: Greenstone (Books 1-2)**
- **Jason's circle**: Rufus (Book 1, Ch11 L49), Gary (Book 1, Ch14 L14), Farrah (Book 1, Ch12 L11; she dies at the end of Book 1, Ch113 L41), Humphrey (Book 1, Ch47 L36), Clive (Book 1, Ch42 L44), Belinda (Book 2, Ch32 L67-72), Jory (Book 1, Ch35 L10-12), Sophie (Book 1, Ch43 L18), Neil (Book 2, Ch28 L44).
- **Authority**: Elspeth Arella (Book 1, Ch65 L60), Vincent Trenslow (Book 1, Ch54 L36), Lucian Lamprey (Book 1, Ch42 L25-30), Pochard Finn (Book 1, Ch42 L30), Danielle Geller (Book 1, Ch47 L89).
- **Patrons and staff**: Emir Bahadir (Book 1, Ch101 L64), Constance (Book 2, Ch1 L112), Hester Maharala (Book 1, Ch109 L6), Gilbert Bertinelli (Book 1, Ch61 L76).
- **Old City and the Island**: Cole Silva (Book 1, Ch50 L92), Clarissa Ventress (Book 1, Ch43 L29), Adris Dorgan (Book 1, Ch104 L68), Thalia Mercer (Book 1, Ch53 L45-49).
- **Builder cult**: Zato (Book 3, Ch7 L89), Timos (Book 2, Ch59 L87).
- **Teams and rivals**: Beth Cavendish (Book 1, Ch83 L23), Valdis Volaire (Book 2, Ch48 L77), Sigrid Freyn (Book 3, Ch4 L43), Padma Parsell (Book 2, Ch38 L65), Dustin Kettering (Book 2, Ch29 L8), Hannah and Claire Adeah (Book 1, Ch67 L15). Purity: Anisa Lasalle (Book 2, Ch10 L92) and Nicolas Hendren (Book 2, Ch19 L73); Cole Silva's right hand Killian Laurent (Book 3, Ch17 L14); Callum Morse (Book 2, Ch34 L91).

**Era 2: Bronze and the Builder (Book 3)**
- All of Era 1 except Farrah. Dawn appears in person (Book 3, Ch69 L1036), with Shako and Velius in the dome chamber (Book 3, Ch69 L233-238).

**Era 3: Earth (Books 4-6)**
- **Jason's family**: Erika, Ian and Emi (Book 4, Ch3 L132), Hiro (Book 4, Ch1 L66-67), Ken and Cheryl (Book 4, Ch4 L105), Kaito and Amy (Book 5, Ch44 L3), Akari (Book 5, Ch14 L61-66).
- **Network and allies**: Anna Tilden (Book 5, Ch51 L48), Nigel Thornton (Book 4, Ch41 L91), Asya Karadeniz (Book 4, Ch36 L37), Greg (Book 5, Ch7 L12), Travis Noble (Book 6, Ch28 L36).
- **Cabal and EOA**: Craig Vermillion (Book 4, Ch11 L101), Mr North (Book 5, Ch8 L14-18), Gerling (Book 6, Ch1 L4).
- **Network figures**: Ketevan Arziani (Book 4, Ch46 L82), Adrien Barbou (Book 4, Ch43 L58), Audrey Blaine (Book 5, Ch10 L17), Noriko Asano (Book 5, Ch36 L3), Li Li Mei (Book 4, Ch63 L25).
- **Returned**: Farrah is freed from Barbou and the EOA (Book 4, Ch51 L62); Dawn returns in a new avatar (Book 5, Ch20 L21-24).

**Era 4: Rimaros (Books 7-8)**
- **Storm Kingdom**: Soramir (Book 7, Ch18 L42), Zara Rimaros (Book 7, Ch18 L66-72), Liara Rimaros (Book 7, Ch45 L54), Pelli (Book 7, Ch13 L51), Argy (Book 7, Ch13 L18), Kasper Irios (Book 7, Ch41 L3).
- **Adventurers**: Korinne Pescos (Book 7, Ch47 L54), Estella Warnock (Book 7, Ch62 L79).
- **Church and order**: Gabrielle Pellin as priestess of Knowledge (Book 7, Ch9 L7), Melody Jain and the Order of Redeeming Light (Book 8, Ch18 L3-6).
- **Rimaros and guilds**: Trenchant Moore (Book 7, Ch21 L13), Jeni Kavaloa (Book 7, Ch47 L33), Orin and Amos Pensinata (Book 7, Ch47 L39; Book 9, Ch6 L47), Baseph and the Rimaros children (Book 8, Ch62 L32), Miles Cotezee (Book 7, Ch12 L69), Helsveth (Book 3, Ch69 L920 for her first appearance).

**Era 5: Yaresh (Books 9-10)**
- **Elf diamond-rankers**: Allayeth and Charist (Book 9, Ch98 L4).

### 3. Using this in a story

- **Check absence as well as presence.** Farrah is dead from the end of Book 1 until Book 4 (Book 1, Ch113 L41; Book 4, Ch51 L62); Kaito, Asya and Greg die in Book 5 (Book 5, Ch51 L40, L52).
- **Open the character file** for appearance, voice and secrets before you give a rostered NPC a line.

---

## Plot Hooks by Era

### 1. Summary

Open situations in each era that a scene can start from. Each hook names a situation the books establish (with its line) and a question for the scene to explore; none of them states an outcome the books do not give. Section 3 adds fixed points and branching suggestions for each era.

### 2. Facts

**Era 1: Greenstone**
- **The Ustei in the desert**: nomads from the north are raiding spirit coin convoys far from home (Book 1, Ch83 L4; Book 1, Ch89 L50). *Why did they come south?*
- **An unregistered spirit coin farm**: a coin that did not come from a registered farm means an unregistered one "needs to be found and shut down" (Book 1, Ch42 L58, L64). *Who runs it?*
- **A corrupt branch**: the Magic Society's local director was banished to Greenstone for improprieties and uses the Fortress as his office (Book 1, Ch42 L25-30). *Who protects him?*
- **An indenture sale**: criminals' indentures are sold or auctioned to recoup the cost of their crimes (Book 1, Ch92 L32). *Who buys a particular indenture, and why?*
- **The Healer's temple**: a priest tells those who stand against the church that they are no longer welcome (Book 1, Ch94 L3-7).

**Era 2: Bronze and the Builder**
- **Star seeds**: a captured adventurer will not consent to examination, and his family must authorise it (Book 2, Ch10 L26-50). *Who else carries a seed?*
- **Purity under inquiry**: an ecumenical council investigates the church of Purity (Book 3, Ch1 L105).
- **A rain elemental over the Old City**: it appears in the city and bronze-rankers gear up to confront it (Book 3, Ch6 L18, L43).
- **Hunting Killian Laurent**: Jason's team spends days in the hunt for the man who possessed a star seed (Book 3, Ch24 L3).
- **The veldt contract**: an unconventional contract east of Greenstone beyond the desert astral space (Book 3, Ch27 L62).

**Era 3: Earth**
- **The EOA moves on Sydney**: they have "seized control in Perth and Melbourne, and now they're making no secret of their overtures into Sydney" (Book 4, Ch9 L76).
- **The Dimensional Fortress**: a sector of the grid has been offline for seventy years (Book 4, Ch51 L30-37).
- **Grid sabotage**: shutting down only localised areas, with a wider failure expected in two months (Book 5, Ch3 L8-19).
- **Reality cores**: one appears in each affected zone and the factions scramble for it (Book 5, Ch45 L9-11).
- **A domain nobody can enter safely**: gold-rankers explore Jason's Nitra domain until the hostile effects grow dangerous (Book 6, Ch23 L3).
- **The atomic device**: Jason, Farrah and Travis work on a semi-disassembled device on a cold winter day (Book 6, Ch31 L4).

**Era 4: Rimaros**
- **The outworlders**: a hundred and nineteen appear at once and must be placed (Book 7, Ch36 L86).
- **A political marriage that fell through**: Zara was matched with a member of House Irios for stability before the surge (Book 7, Ch18 L66-72).
- **Guild quotas**: guilds receive sixty percent of the surge quotas though few adventurers belong to them (Book 7, Ch12 L23).
- **A kidnapped heir**: Gibson Amouz is spirited away from the island (Book 8, Ch20 L4).

**Era 5: Yaresh and the messengers**
- **A messenger camp**: Knowledge's army and the messengers set camp outside Yaresh while the city's government and Adventure Society worry (Book 9, Ch42 L80).
- **Servant races and masters**: messenger masters may treat servants as they please within limits (Book 9, Ch56 L5).

**Era 6: Cardinas**

**Era 7: The return**

### 3. Branching notes and fixed points

How to use these: each hook below lists two or three directions a scene could take. **The directions are table-top suggestions, not book facts**; the books give no outcome for any of the hooks. Beside them, the **fixed points** are events the books establish in that era; no branch may reverse them, and no scene set before them may speak as if they had happened.

**Era 1: Greenstone**
- **Fixed points**: Farrah dies covering civilians (Book 1, Ch113 L41); Thadwick set up the Leust land-grab (Book 1, Ch94 L58); Emir's contest frees 419 time-displaced priests (Book 2, Ch63 L9).
- **The Ustei**: (a) the raiders are tracked to a base; (b) a convoy guard defects; (c) the raid is a diversion for something in the city.
- **Indenture sale**: (a) the buyer is a rival crime lord; (b) a noble buys to hide a debt; (c) a team tries to buy the indenture out.
- **Corrupt branch**: (a) an inspector comes; (b) a blackmail file surfaces; (c) the deputy turns informant.

**Era 2: Bronze and the Builder**
- **Fixed points**: Hendren dies in the cult camp (Book 3, Ch59 L126); Zato is defeated and the Builder negotiates with the World-Phoenix and the Reaper (Book 3, Ch69 L42, L279); Jason dies at the climax (Book 3, Ch69 L900); Killian flees by ship (Book 3, Ch25 L4).
- **Purity inquiry**: (a) a witness recants; (b) a priest defects; (c) the inquiry is sabotaged.
- **Rain elemental**: (a) a rival team claims the kill; (b) the manifestation hides a second one; (c) the Society shifts blame.
- **Hunting Killian**: (a) a false lead; (b) a patron hides him; (c) he is captured early (not in the books).

**Era 3: Earth**
- **Fixed points**: Kaito, Asya and Greg die in Berlin (Book 5, Ch51 L40); Jason claims Nitra (Book 6, Ch5 L41) and Saint-Étienne (Book 6, Ch47 L3); Shako kills Jason (Book 6, Ch17 L37); Jason leaves Earth in autumn 2021 (Book 6, Ch47 L155).
- **EOA on Sydney**: (a) a negotiated buy-out; (b) a street fight; (c) a leak inside the Network.
- **Reality cores**: (a) a faction ambush; (b) a trade between rivals; (c) a double-cross.
- **Nitra domain**: (a) a rescue; (b) a gold-ranker is taken; (c) a delegation demands entry.

**Era 4: Rimaros**
- **Fixed points**: Vesper, Ledev and Jeni Kavaloa die in the One Day War (Book 7, Ch66 L36); the Astral Throne and Gate appear (Book 8, Ch49 L105); the Builder withdraws his forces (Book 8, Ch56 L35).
- **The outworlders**: (a) a placement dispute; (b) one of them is hunted; (c) a guild recruits them all.
- **The failed marriage**: (a) House Irios seeks compensation; (b) a duel; (c) a new match is proposed.
- **A kidnapped heir**: (a) a ransom demand; (b) a rescue by a rival; (c) the heir resists rescue.

**Era 5: Yaresh and the messengers**
- **Fixed points**: Jason is recognised as an astral king (Book 9, Ch62 L30-34); Schaat is found dead (Book 9, Ch98 L4-5); Marek and Tera become Jason's captives (Book 9, Ch103 L64; Book 9, Ch102 L54-67).
- **Messenger camp**: (a) a parley; (b) a raid on supplies; (c) a spy inside the camp.
- **Servants and masters**: (a) a servant's plea reaches the city; (b) a master is humbled; (c) a servant is smuggled out.
- **The Aristocratic Faction**: (a) a vote of no confidence; (b) a staged scandal; (c) a deal with an outside broker.

**Era 6: The Cardinas zone**
- **A buried city**: (a) a food crisis; (b) a succession dispute; (c) a refugee flood.
- **A hero's price**: (a) a rival bargain; (b) a delay; (c) Gary's own request.
- **The echo array**: (a) an experiment goes wrong; (b) an old guardian; (c) a message from the surface.

**Era 7: The Return**
- **Indenture and the System**: (a) a noble house yields; (b) a house refuses; (c) a region tries to hide its indenture.
- **A pirate ship on Earth**: (a) a mutiny; (b) a captured crew member; (c) a deal for safe passage.
- **Finnegan, Elizabeth and McKean**: (a) a captured aide; (b) a false sighting; (c) a double agent.
- **Knowledge's long plan**: (a) a church defector; (b) a messenger who knows more; (c) an Earth faction that wants a god of its own.

### 4. Using this in a story

- **Use one hook per scene.** Pick the hook from the era you chose, make sure its spoiler level matches (`Spoiler_Management.md`), and let the books' own situation do the work.
- **Do not invent resolutions.** Where a hook asks a question, the books may or may not answer it; check the character and place files before improvising.

---

## Spoiler Management and Secrets

### 1. Summary

How to use the four spoiler levels, a table of the series' main secrets with the line where each first surfaces, and rules for who knows what.

### 2. Facts

**The four levels** (`../00_Project_Structure.md`, section 6)

**Main secrets and where they first surface**
- **Jason is an outworlder**: he tells Rufus that coming to this world is "a fresh start" (Book 1, Ch25 L43); Danielle later investigates him at Gabriel Remore's request (Book 1, Ch48 L101-102). Level 1.
- **Star seeds and the Builder**: an archbishop explains that great astral beings send followers star seeds (Book 2, Ch21 L26-28); Clive explains that great astral beings cannot act in physical reality directly and recruit others (Book 2, Ch21 L112). Level 1.
- **The Order of the Reaper**: Emir says the churches "scoured" it away (Book 2, Ch25 L73); Shade says it survives as a network of quiet allies and the trials are a lure (Book 2, Ch60 L120-122). Level 1.
- **Dawn's role**: the World-Phoenix's First Servant is its primary vessel (Book 3, Ch69 L1036); her true body is a celestine with ruby eyes (Book 7, Ch54 L87). Level 1-2.
- **Jason's deaths**: the Church of Death's certificate reads "died four times. So far." (Book 7, Ch17 L104). Level 2.
- **Astral king**: Jason's astral kingdom appears with the Throne and Gate (Book 8, Ch49 L105); a healer realises he is a silver-rank astral king (Book 9, Ch62 L30-34). Level 2.

**In-world belief versus fact**
- Emir's account of the Order of the Reaper (Book 2, Ch25 L73) is contradicted by Shade's account (Book 2, Ch44 L72-78); treat a character's story as belief unless a later line confirms it.
- A great astral being need not fear a god's retribution because gods cannot see into an astral space outside their world (Book 3, Ch62 L33).

### 3. Using this in a story
